# Batch Summaries

One entry per completed batch, newest last. This is the durable home the quality gate needs: `logs/` is gitignored, so a batch summary written only into a phase log leaves no record for the next writer.

Rules for an entry:

- Write it after the chapters are finished, not before, and do not pad it to a length. Six to ten lines is usually right.
- Say what the batch **changed**, not what it was about: the new situation at the end, the cost paid, the promise made or broken, and the one question the next batch inherits.
- Record any canon the batch invented that later batches must obey, and put the durable part in `state/continuity.md` rather than leaving it only here.
- The one-line `Last batch summary` field in `state/current.md` names the most recent entry in this file.

## Batch 0001 — Volume 01, Chapters 1–10, "The Nineteenth of Mudmonth"

**Landed.** Chapters 1–10 in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0001.md` to `chapter-0010.md`, four digits, **40,105 words** (40,075 excluding the title lines), ten complete scenes, POVs Aren ×8, Mara ×1 (Ch 3), Tovan ×1 (Ch 9). Two Gatehouse lines in the whole batch, one at the ringing (Ch 4) and one at the return (Ch 8), as budgeted. **Per chapter:** 3,129 / 3,276 / 3,615 / 3,202 / 3,589 / 3,597 / 5,413 / 4,134 / 3,893 / 6,257. Ch 7 and Ch 10 run long and are the two to watch in review.

**What the batch changed.** Aren bought a cracked bell for two pence, signed a hold and then a six-mark guild bond putting it in his own name, broke an undated promise to ring nothing, crossed on the night of the eighteenth, was gone nine days instead of the three the road-crews' saying allows, and came home to an empty cradle under a Crown seal, a closed road, a lost market, and a fever. He spent the only thing he brought back — the writ's **counterfoil** — in the Bellweather Claims Archive on the thirtieth, with no condition and no copy, together with a sworn account in his own hand that names his own eleven-year silence and both of his omissions. Stage 0, marked, unqualified.

**The state at the end of the batch.**
- **Old Toll bell:** in the watch storehouse by the fish weir, under a Crown seal, **taken off the abutment at first light on the nineteenth of Mudmonth** by Rell's watch, four men and a cart, on an order dated the **sixteenth** and delivered on the evening of the eighteenth. The cradle on the near abutment has been empty **eight days** when he returns on the twenty-seventh, and the bell has now been gone sixteen. Nobody in Merefen can produce the office that authorised it.
- **The Old Toll sill:** usable once and marked. A pale, warm, dry cross the size of two hands where the packing was lifted and set down out of line. **Anchor-glass is now the gate:** two Steppe discs, four marks the pair, money before cutting, ready in a fortnight. No stitch is prepared and none can be until that is paid for.
- **Aren's guild bond:** six marks against a tool-chest worth three and a half, witnessed by Tovan, standing off the guild's removal notice of the eleventh until the sitting on the twenty-fourth — a sitting that will now never happen.
- **The counterfoil:** custody entry and a claim number in the Archive, dated the thirtieth of Mudmonth, three days before the province's renewal, entered under the seal and *beneath* Aren's account at Lysa Fenn's order. Ferris Oat's brine weights are **held and cited, not filed.**
- **Rell's watch book:** a statement entered the twenty-seventh, in the sergeant's hand, including the first public appearance of the phrase *road-keeper of Merefen* and *holder of the Bellweather hinge*. Rell wrote it down not believing it and said so in the room.
- **Lysa Fenn:** a sealed order is not yet decided. The hearing on the seizure is set for the **fourth of Greenmonth**, in the salt-loft room, watch captain under oath, the bridge guild's notice of the eleventh in front of her. She has read the county's schedule of provincial offices and found no keeper's office, and has said the honest answer to the seizure is *yes, it was lawful, and it took three days from the day written on the paper to the morning the bell came off the abutment, and somebody must answer for it.* **Three days is the figure, not eight and not nine** — eight is how long the cradle stood empty when Aren got back, and nine is how long he was gone.
- **What Tovan has refused:** the road, the secret, and being the man holding the span. He has also refused, in Aren's hearing, to be the one who gives a statement about the removal — and he has already been asked for one.
- **The next crossing's bill:** four marks he does not have, and a glasswright in Merefen's batch-two slot. The old Toll sill is the anchor and it is spent.

**Canon the batch invented that later batches must obey.**
1. **The province's renewal is dated for the second of Greenmonth**, not the anniversary. The nineteenth is a Sunday and the Archive is shut on it. A claim entered before the second is a claim of the old year; after it, a claim under a finding that nothing survives. This is the volume's administrative clock.
2. **The "three days" is the road crews' saying**, not a Gatehouse panel: three days to open an old road and three to come back. Volume 01's two-line Gatehouse budget is spent, and the saying is the only warning Aren gets. The drift is therefore six days on the first crossing.
3. **Aren crossed in the clothes he left in**, and the field notes rode in the breast pocket of that same coat. They came back with him. The coat is the same coat and it is the reason the account in Ch 10 can be specific.
4. **His bar went through with him** and came back; it is lying on the Old Toll setts where he set it down in the fourth second of the return.
5. **The Bellweather fever was real** in Bellweather-Living: two hundred and eleven dead of the low quarter in Mudmonth, YR 310, kept in a local ledger, and the number is given to Aren by **the ferryman on the north bank**, not by anybody else. Also on the page: **a woman on Ferry Lane who keeps her shutters shut**, and a man at the tanner's who has not been out in daylight in three years. **The woman on the gallery is a third person and not the Ferry Lane woman** — she is the one who asks *How would you know?* and who says nobody here has ever wanted to see a stranger's hand. Three separate women in Ch 5; do not merge them. The Crown's fever account is a true thing carrying a false one. Held as the moral complication of the whole case.
6. **The third bell** is the crossing-closed bell, rung from the crown at night, once and long. It cracked on the third stroke on the nineteenth of Mudmonth, YR 301, and that ringing *is* the hinge. The bell is in the path's founder's yard on two oak trundles under a tarred cloth, unrecast.
7. **The path's bell-founder's yard, the toll-house steward's paper, the bench-clerk, the arch's second pier, and the outer ring's eight-inch crack** are all now on the page. The second pier's ring stone is sound; the mortar behind it was raked out from inside by water coming up under the arch. There is no repair that does not begin with the arch out of the water for a season. That is the climax, planted.
8. **Aren's fee in the town ledger: eleven marks and four pence**, itemised in Ch 10 to six lines, of which eleven days' crew pay fell to Tovan and not to the trust. Tovan's own tally in Ch 9 (oak 1s 4d, lime 1s 6d, stone and cartage 2 marks, nails 8d) is the material line of the same column, at two marks three and sixpence, and the two columns agree. It is the smallest number in the book and it is not the point.
9. **Mara's roll of the dead is still unbuilt** and its rule is now stated in her own head: a roll built out of Ferris Oat's ledger must be entered as a ledger, in its own order, with the weights standing beside the names, and never as a speech. Do not spend that in Volume 01.
10. **Hushwater Reach is on the page in Chapter 7**: the crossing book's second page, eleven crossings and nine names, a cooperative that counts **ferry hands and hearths and not souls**, and a best estimate of **near sixty** offered by three assessors and a man from the abbey and defended as a defect of the counting. The erased Hushwater in the main world is eighty. No character compares the two and no chapter may.

## The question Chapter 11 opens on

Chapter 10 closes on the **thirtieth of Mudmonth**, so the day map for the next batch is the only calendar that counts. **The first of Greenmonth is tomorrow**, Chapter 11. **The second of Greenmonth** is the day the province renews its finding that nothing survives, and everything in the file becomes a year weaker — that is the day after tomorrow from the thirtieth, not four days out. **The hearing of the fourth** on the sealed seizure is two days after the renewal, and three days after Chapter 11 opens. Underneath all of it: a bell under a Crown seal that a magistrate can find lawful and cannot un-find; four marks for two discs of Steppe glass that a man with a shilling a day does not have, against a sill that will not carry a second road until it is mended with them; and **the four marks are Tovan's, not Aren's** — see the handoff note below. The path is on its eleventh day without him and has sent nothing, because a path does not write letters and a man at the first stage has no door.

**Two things the handoff must not get wrong.** **The four marks belong to Tovan.** Ch 9 gives them to him twice ("He could carry four marks. He had four marks"), Ch 10 restates it from Aren's side ("a brother who had four marks and a bridge to finish"), and Ch 20's card is explicit that Tovan has the money and will not be asked. Aren has a shilling a day, eleven days of unpaid wages, a bond for six against a chest worth three and a half, and about a mark and a quarter. Nobody may hand the batch's central scarce resource from one brother to the other. **And the bell is one bell, and the page says so now.** Ch 7: Aren states it flatly to the bench — nine stone, split lip to shoulder, the founder's mark on the shoulder is theirs, read in a Merefen scrap yard before he ever came up the north steps; it is under a tarred cloth on two trundles in Bellweather's yard and on an iron cradle on a bridge abutment in Merefen, and he rang it there, and both are true. The Crown's clearance entry in Merefen reads *unclassified salvage* with no city and no river on it, entered in **YR 302, the year after the writ**. That is the volume's hinge and a reader is allowed to hold exactly one belief about it.



## Batch 0002 — Volume 01, Chapters 11–20, "What the Fever Cost Everyone Else"

**Landed.** Chapters 11–20 in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0011.md` to `chapter-0020.md`, ten complete scenes, POVs Aren ×5 (11, 13, 15, 18, 20) / Mara ×3 (12, 14, 19) / Tovan ×1 (17) / Lysa Fenn ×1 (16). **Zero Gatehouse lines, as budgeted** — Volume 01's two were spent on the ringing and the return, and the silence is the batch's pressure. Six names, all pre-established: Jos Rusk, Bram Ottery, Halven, Pei Tarrow, Sarn Oyelaran, Ilvane Quarren. **57,708 words** for the batch, title lines included, **post-repair**. **Per chapter:** 5,878 / 5,183 / 4,250 / 4,429 / 6,710 / 4,634 / 5,762 / 4,182 / 8,610 / 8,070. Ch 19 and Ch 20 run long and are the two to watch in review; Ch 19 carries five scenes. *(The pre-repair figures were 56,071 and 5,831 / 5,196 / 4,229 / 4,413 / 6,321 / 4,620 / 5,759 / 4,012 / 8,491 / 7,199. Ch 17 is shorter because the Tarrow coda was cut and Ch 20 longer because the ninth is now said aloud on the bridge.)*

**What the batch changed.** The Archive stopped keeping records and became a party. A magistrate found the seizure **lawful** and made the *hole* under it a public record in her own name; the guild's trust stays in abeyance; the bell stays under a Crown seal. A surviving Hushwater ferryman had his cause put in dispute in writing, on purpose, to weaken it. A registrar's fee turned out to be a charge for a search, and the ledger it guards is missing from a shelf it is entered on. A **watch sergeant's unasked statement** went into a court record and named the man in the stair who has been selling the fever since the seventeenth — and the statement cost Ottery his stripes and his accrual, and no promotion. A guild clerk served a finding on seven villages **by name** and left the sum line blank. The Old Toll sill was cut for a pin and set with two Steppe discs paid for out of a **debt against a cutting contract**, not a bond, with a maker's account of how the glass will fail and three hands on a dated undertaking. And the second of three crossings this season is spent on paper for the morning of the ninth.

**The state at the end of the batch.**
- **The Old Toll sill:** a seat cut square in a block of the fourth course, two annealed Steppe discs set edge to edge across the scar, a pin and an oak collar, the maker's mark under the near disc, and **Quarren's page on how it will fail.** A scar is a place a thing came through and can come through again: **two is easy, three is not a number a glasswright will sell.** The sill is now an anchor a man made himself with his own hands and his own money.
- **Aren's money:** four marks paid, of which a mark and a quarter was his own and **two marks and three quarters is a wage advance against Tovan's face-stone contract**, at eight pence a day, carried on if the cutting fails, entered on the guild roll as a debt and not a surety, and never in a sentence with Tovan's name in it. He has nothing left. Eleven days of wages still owed by the trust. The bond of the eleventh, six marks against a chest at three and a half, is still on the roll and the bench votes on it on the **ninth of Greenmonth** while its surety is under an arch.
- **The ninth of Greenmonth:** a market day, the bench's sitting, the provincial summons, and a crossing. **He will not answer the summons.** That is a gap in a provincial ledger, and Lysa Fenn said in advance that a man who does not appear has left a gap and a court that notices a gap will sometimes do something about it.
- **The bells' paperwork:** two instruments, no office behind either, and a finding that says so in a magistrate's hand. The Crown's **YR 302 clearance entry is out of place in a bound order, in a hand the same age as its neighbours**, filed unargued with a copyist's attestation. Nobody explains it and nobody may.
- **Waiting:** the missing-anchor ledger opens on the **fifteenth** if the order is returnable then. Lysa is at the Dunmere assizes on the eighth and ninth, so the ninth's bench sits without her.
- **People:** Ottery relieved of the sergeant's post and of nine and three months' accrual, told by Rell in a corridor, and unrewarded. Rusk has a claim number, a cause in dispute, and a wrapper with a tradesman's hand on it. Pei Tarrow is on Tovan's payroll at the yard rate and has said he is not a symbol. Sarn Oyelaran has a warrant for a third post in the day-book and has begun to be needed. Halven has a gap on a shelf and a date on a request.

**Canon the batch invented that later batches must obey.** Items 51–67 in `state/continuity.md`, including: the currency (**one mark is four shillings / 48 pence**, so four marks is 192 pence and sixteen shillings, and eight pence a day is 24 days — `bible/terminology.md` said 8 shillings and has been corrected to match); the seam of the **quarter-inch bench mark**; the **watch-sergeant's statement** and Ottery's cost; the magistrate's finding of the fourth, clause by clause; the **YR 302 out-of-place clearance entry**; Mara's second wall rule, **no combined figure is entered in this office**; and the **anchor-glass pair in Quarren's rack**, already a year annealed, which is why the slip's fortnight is not the governing term.

**Repair pass (review of Batch 0002).** The batch was re-read against the bible, the cards and the item 67 weekday chain, and thirty-five findings were applied to the prose and six to the state files. **No plot moved, no chapter was renumbered, no beat was removed.** The substantive changes: Ch 17's closing coda into Pei Tarrow's interior was cut and the chapter now ends in Tovan's POV on the boy's bell report; Ch 15 no longer asserts a second bell (*either of these papers*), Ottery's statement is dated the **second** of Greenmonth instead of the seventh, the invented thirteenth-of-Mudmonth repacking is gone, and **Rell now puts the twenty-seventh to the court and is shown frightened**, which the card required; the **weekday chain is now clean across all ten chapters** (Ch 11's three Tuesdays, Ch 12's Thursday, Ch 13's two Thursdays, Ch 16, Ch 19 and Ch 20's fourth-is-a-Thursday, and Ch 17's two Tuesdays were all wrong and are fixed); Ch 13 no longer prints the four thousand beside the villages and no longer says *in March*; Ch 18's three forward-look dates, the report's hour, and the seventeen-of-Mudmonth contradiction are fixed, and **the chapter now carries no System display at all**; Ch 19's YR 302 question now asks the right year, the clerk is *refused* permission to add the villages, the twenty-four-day figure is consistent in both places, the Tarrow coda inventory was cut, and the three missing card beats (the fee bought a date and not the ledger, the missing-anchor book, the order returnable on the fifteenth) are now on the page; Ch 20's money bag, the nine shillings, the wrong day for Mara's refusal, the three places the factor's stone was standing, and the paragraph that performed the thing Aren's character had just refused are all fixed, and **the ninth is now said aloud on the bridge to Tovan and Quarren** so that the card's emotional change is cashed; Ch 16's hours now run in order; Ch 11's river and its Mudmonth count are fixed; and the batch now uses `---` scene breaks consistently, which Chapters 1–10 do not.

**The question Chapter 21 opens on**

The ninth of Greenmonth, and the bench sits before he is under the arch, and the crown's copy of the finding has seven village names in a clerk's hand and a blank line under them, and a road warden's sworn account of a city of four thousand is the only document in the county that says Bellweather is standing. The path on the other side of the door has been running its own business since the day he got off it and has sent nothing, and a man with a cut scar in a sill and two discs of glass in it is going to find out what a prepared return stitch costs when there is nobody standing at the far end of it and a crown's paper is standing at this one.

## Batch 0003 — Volume 01, Chapters 21–30, "The Second Crossing and the Refusal"

**Landed.** Chapters 21–30 in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0021.md` to `chapter-0030.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes, POVs Aren ×8 (21, 22, 23, 25, 26, 27, 28, 30) / Mara ×1 (24) / Tovan ×1 (29). **Zero Gatehouse lines, zero System displays, by budget** — both of Volume 01's two lines were spent on the ringing and the return, and the silence is the batch's pressure. **Four new names, four of the six the card allows:** Alis Merrow, Hollis Dree, Captain Ruen, Reeve Aldis Fenn. **Both unfilled slots left unfilled** — the hooded woman in Chapter 27 is the woman on Ferry Lane whose shutters have been shut since the thaw, and the girl on the north steps is the girl from Chapter 5; naming either would be a seventh voice the batch does not need. **54,375 words**, title lines included, after the repair pass (54,008 on landing). Per chapter: 4,765 / 6,008 / 4,565 / 5,224 / 5,640 / 5,588 / 5,923 / 5,328 / 5,378 / 5,956. Ch 22 runs long (the terms and the conditions) and is the one to watch.

**What the batch changed.** The ninth of Greenmonth is a market morning, the Merefen bridge guild's bench votes on the bond of the eleventh with its surety four days off under an arch, and the town says the chalk; the ninth-of-Greenmonth provincial summons goes unanswered and a clerk writes *absent* in a ledger. Nineteen path days and six main-world days. The bench's terms change from a favour to a *witness*, and a toll-house steward buys a foreign stranger on a purchase-on-credit entry and then reads out a line of a four-hundred-leaf creditor's register that **names Marshal Edric Hunn as the signatory of the severance of "the Alder Vale crossing and its dependent places," nineteenth of Mudmonth, YR 301, with the press-mark of press four, die eleven** — the same press whose device, a hand laid flat on a book, is in wax on a post and a storehouse door in Merefen. The same page exonerates and indicts: a Crown's written refusal of Aren's request for an extension, dated the eighteenth, in a copy he has had since noon on the nineteenth of YR 301. **The page does not leave the city**, by the toll houses' condition, on the ground that a name in a creditor's book is a war. And the city refuses to be a case: an archivist says out loud that a copy is a claim and that a restoration is a transfer of ownership, and the bench gives Aren **a consent with conditions, a named steward, and a visible road, and nothing else.** He answers Captain Ruen's question in public, extends his Chapter 10 sworn account with the fact that he was on that deck in a Crown's cloak at the second hour with two hundred people on it, hands the field book to the guild hall, and commits his last crossing of the year to a date. He comes back into a fever, files the account aloud, and tells Mara what a crossing costs in the body.

**The state at the end of the batch.**
- **The Old Toll bell:** under a Crown seal in the watch storehouse by the fish weir. Not coming back this volume. Aren has said out loud, in a filing room, that he is not going to get it and is not going to try.
- **The bond:** carried. Tool-chest at three and a half against six, the balance on his wages at a shilling a day, entered as a debt of his and not the guild's, entered as proposed by him and withdrawn before the vote. The minute carries the reeve's statement that the surety was not in front of the bench and the clerk's statement that he proposed it and left.
- **His money:** nothing in hand, eleven days of wages owed by a trust in abeyance, two marks and three quarters of wage advance outstanding at eight pence a day, six marks on the roll at a shilling a day. No new money anywhere in the batch.
- **The register page:** read aloud in the counting house on the thirteenth day and in the guild hall the same night, **twice, and no more.** A clerk's fair copy is in the counting house's press, on a wrapper in Aren's hand stating the copy is not produced in court and is held in this city. **The name does not travel in this batch.** What travels is a fact on a date in two hands: that a creditor's register in Bellweather has a line for the nineteenth of Mudmonth, YR 301, with the press-mark of press four, die eleven. The page itself reaches the north in **Chapter 46** through the scheduled crossing, and enters the Archive as a claim in **Chapters 48–50**, exactly as `outline/volume-01.md` plans.
- **The agreement:** a consent with conditions, a named steward (**Sera Quen**, the ferrywright at Hushwater Reach), a visible road entered in a book with a date, a place, an hour and a name, two witnesses (**Sera Quen and Captain Ruen**) and one record (the guild-clerk's entry), and a promise with a date on it: the marking of the second pier in the middle of the year at the lowest water. **The counterweights are his third and last crossing of the year and a new seat nobody has yet cut.** There is no fresh Steppe glass north of Merefen before Longlight.
- **A Crown letter** dated the twelfth of Greenmonth, addressed to *the road-keeper of Merefen, or such officer as now holds that office*, received by nobody and answered by nobody, **chalked on the outside of a drawer in the toll-house** with the date, the hour, the words *No such office in this county* and *Not answered*, in the toll-clerk's own hand.
- **The Archive:** a second claim number entered **on Mara's own account, against nobody**, for the production of records, resting on two facts and no speech; a claim number for the Stonebarrow woman's dressed fragment of a bridge face; the fourth press's shelf register with a **removal date — the nineteenth of Mudmonth, YR 301 — and no name and no office and no authority**; and a sheet headed *the cost of a crossing, as known to this office*, entered on the sixteenth so that he is told it by a paper and not by his own body.

**Canon the batch invented that later batches must obey.** Items 69–92 in `state/continuity.md`. The load-bearing ones: the **press-mark** rather than a wax (a creditor keeps the press, the die and the impression because wax is rubbish in eleven years); **a name in a creditor's book is a war**; the **four conditions** Merrow imposes (no copy, a fair copy that is not produced, the fact leaves and the line does not, no motive — and Aren is caught breaking the fourth in the open and the old clerk enters the arrest); the **sixth pier mark is still eight inches and not growing**; the **fourth camp** on the north bank and the **third bay of the gate bridge, a third of it not there**; the **toll houses' want** — the northern trade, three shut houses, four feet of paving, a hundred and twenty marks of uncollected toll; **Hushwater Reach's counting book and the woman who will not let it be copied** (*a copy is a second thing that somebody can lose*), which is **a different woman from Sera Quen** (item 88); the **three claim numbers, all entered on the sixteenth, a Friday** (item 89); and the **Crown letter's chalk, in which the word no stands three times** (item 90).

**Card errors corrected, recorded so the next writer does not inherit them.** (1) The Chapter 30 card said the bench voted **on the sixth** of Greenmonth; the bench sits on the **ninth**, and the vote is on the ninth. (2) The Chapter 26 card said the register names a marshal "**eleven years dead** in the world's terms"; **Edric Hunn is alive**, which is the whole point of the reveal, and is written that way. (3) The batch prompt said "the third of three crossings is spent" for this crossing; **two are spent, one is pledged to a date in the middle of the year.** (4) The Chapter 30 card's account omits the new anchor-glass price, which is the batch's second counterweight; it is on the page. (5) The Chapter 25 card's ending (the page is in a hand he has seen on wax) and the Chapter 26 card's opening (the same line read again) were one scene; they have been split so the line is printed once, in full, in Ch 26, and Ch 25 ends on the seal-book's wax impression. (6) **The Chapter 29 card's beat that the Crown letter's body records the road-keeper's non-attendance on the ninth was cut on purpose and is not to be restored** — the provincial ledger's *absent* is the better carrier of it, and a Crown hand saying the same thing would put the town in the position of answering a paper it cannot answer. Recorded as continuity item 92.

**Repair pass (review of Batch 0003).** The batch was re-read against the cards, the volume outline, the item 69 day map and the prior twenty chapters, and **sixteen findings were applied to the prose and eleven to the state files. No plot moved, no chapter was renumbered, no beat was removed, and no controller file was touched.** The substantive changes: **the sixteenth of Greenmonth is a Friday**, and the batch had said Tuesday in two state files, in Chapter 30's opening and on the Market Square board in two places — the board line is now *Road warden in the cart on Friday*; **Chapter 27's "five days" and "eight days" since the name was read are now two**, because the name was read in the guild hall on the thirteenth day and Chapter 27 is the fifteenth, and Chapter 28's "five days" is counted from the eighteenth and was left alone; **Chapter 26's arrival paragraph was rewritten on page facts** (it had the toll-house steward and the guild's man confused and a clause with no referent) and its doubled instruction to the clerk, its repeated clause and its unclosed quotation are gone; **Chapter 28 no longer dates the sworn account a year early** — it was filed on the thirtieth of Mudmonth, YR 312, six weeks before, and it also no longer says he both put it there and did not; **Chapter 21's clerk entry is now the undertaking of the eighth of Greenmonth and the *second* of that month's three *no reason given* lines**, so that the **ninth page, entered at the yard on the tenth**, is the live reading in Chapters 29 and 30 and the clerk's "there will be a third" in Ch 21 lands on it; **Chapter 30's second witness is now in the room** — Tovan Kest puts the week-book on the table before the account is sworn, so the chain of custody is on the page and not inferred from a signature; **Chapter 24's closing is back inside Mara Vey's point of view**; Merrow's "six days" in Ch 25 is now **twelve**; Ch 26's four-day span is attributed to the boats and not to the tender and the day's column says which day it is headed; Ch 27's two mid-paragraph quotation marks are closed so Captain Ruen's speech stays in his voice; and **Chapter 27 now separates Sera Quen from the woman who keeps the Hushwater crossing book out loud, in a hall, so the two cannot be merged by a later writer.** Two further date errors found in the same class while repairing and also fixed: Chapter 26's promise to read the name "on Thursday" (the reading happened that night) and Chapter 30's claim number "on Saturday" (all three were entered on the sixteenth, a Friday). Two findings were **recorded rather than changed** and are now in canon: **Hollis Dree's second statement of the two hundred and eleven** in a public hall in Ch 27 is a second source and not a substitution for the ferryman (item 36), and the **Ch 29 letter's missing body** is a conscious cut (item 92).

**The question Chapter 31 opens on**

The sixteenth of Greenmonth is a Friday and the fever broke on the eighteenth, and the town is going to say the chalk, and a bench is not going to sit again for a year. **Nothing crosses: Block 4 is ten chapters with no door in either direction, and the reader is going to feel the absence as a cost.** What is on the page: a road warden with one crossing of the year left and no money for a seat; a keeper of records with a claim number against nobody and a shelf register with a date on it and no name; a Crown letter in a drawer chalked *not answered*; a marble bench that is going to decide in a year whether to restore a trust over a lawful Crown holding; and, in a press two flights above a salt merchant, a copy of somebody's chapter that nobody outside Merefen has yet read.

## Batch 0004 — Volume 01, Chapters 31–40, "The Road Shut"

**Landed.** Chapters 31–40 in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0031.md` to `chapter-0040.md`, ten complete scenes, POVs Aren ×5 (31, 34, 36, 38, 40) / Mara ×3 (32, 35, 39) / Tovan ×2 (33, 37). **Zero Gatehouse lines, zero System displays, zero crossings in either direction, by budget and by plan** — the two lines were spent in Batch 0001 and Block 4's whole engine is that nothing moves. **Four new names of the six the card allows** — Dace Millward, Iven Sarrat, Rowan Petch, Harl Mounce — **and two slots left unfilled.** **64,632 words**, title lines included, after two repair passes. Per chapter: 6,682 / 6,868 / 6,259 / 5,567 / 7,121 / 5,420 / 4,420 / 5,883 / 5,827 / 10,585. **Ch 40 is the longest chapter in the manuscript and is the one to watch in review** — it is the batch's climax and it carries the chain, the lending, Millward's cost and the coda, and the two blocks of restatement a review named have been cut without its load-bearing material going with them; Ch 35 is the second and carries the batch's longest single speech, which is deliberate.

**What the batch changed.** Thirteen weeks in which no document and no person crosses in either direction, and every one of them costs somebody something in somebody else's accounts. **The Crown declared contact illegal in writing** — the tolls of the Old Toll crossing suspended from the first of Longlight by a letter to the holder that also forbids him to permit *entertainment* or correspondence about the crossing, with the town not required to be told; and a second sealed instruction to the watch that names no law, requires no court, and directs the watch to prevent the assembly of any body of this county in respect of the object. **Rell is pressed from above and stays in post**, frightened, correct, and shown to be right on every count by a commendation with a seal on it that he nailed to his own wall with four nails, having concluded that he is not being honoured but *picked*. **The town ate the loss and said why in chalk**, and a town which cannot pay anybody is a town whose men are free, and what they do with that is the question of Chapter 31. **A competent woman was competent in a room where being right is expensive** — Mara named the price of her own favour out loud before handing it over, three times, and entered a claim against herself, and made a second keeper, and was told by her own copyist that she has begun to price being right and has noticed. **The Archive was petitioned, then subpoenaed, then petrified** — a writ of enquiry that does not want to succeed and only wants the thirtieth day to arrive, during which the office may not make a copy of anything, and which takes effect when it is stamped. **Edric Hunn's first letter arrived on the eighteenth of Longlight**, eleven hundred words, accurate, containing no offer and no name but his own, and one sentence with a subject and a verb in it, and a courteous request at the end for the names of everybody he has spoken to. **News from the other side arrived late, secondhand, two readings, and cannot be acted on by anybody in the county including the man it is about**, and the crack in a ring four days off is either eight inches or eleven or neither. **And the volume's last move is a decision made without a crossing**: a man files four dated pieces in a creditor's chest, a press, and a yard's book, tells two people where they are, chooses a building over a person, and says why in a room.

**The state at the end of the batch.**
- **The Old Toll crossing:** trust in abeyance, so no owner, so no crew; kept in repair by the bench out of its own materials and time under a Saturday resolution; four marks and six a year, entered; the bell moved into a room in the watch house with a lock on it; the tolls suspended; the wharf road shut; a handrail in Weir Street with a line in a yard's book saying it was not paid for and not asked for.
- **Aren's money:** eleven shillings and fourpence in a jar. Four marks for two discs of glass, which is sixteen shillings, which is four shillings and eightpence away. Two marks and three quarters carried on a cutting contract; six marks on the guild roll owed by a trust in abeyance; eleven days of crew wages owed by nothing. The third crossing of the year is pledged to **the twenty-sixth of Longlight, a Wednesday, four days after he decided not to spend it.**
- **The seat:** a block of the fourth course with two used discs in it, a maker's mark under the near one, and no glass to be had north of Merefen before Frostmonth. Quarren's page says three is not a number he would sell.
- **The documents:** a sworn account of the thirtieth of Mudmonth; a finding of the fourth of Greenmonth; a bench minute of the fifteenth of Longlight; a Crown returnable order returned unexecuted with **the office of keeper of the Bellweather hinge written into a printed space on the back of it by a county clerk**; a report of the twenty-first of Longlight with five limits; four pieces of a chain in three places; **and one document that is in none of them, in a locked room over a cooper, and never will be.**
- **People:** Rowan Petch is a frightened clerk who has been careful once while somebody watched; Sarn Oyelaran is a keeper with a key; Jos Rusk has his name in a provincial file and is owed a hearing and does not know yet what it is for; Bram Ottery is still in Merefen, unpaid and unpromoted, and is not in this block; **Lysa Fenn has no page in this block and her finding is carrying the county.**

**Canon the batch invented that later batches must obey.** Items 93–110 in `state/continuity.md`. The load-bearing ones: **a crossing with no owner is a crossing with no crew, and a crew is not an owner**; **petitioning, subpoenaeing and petrifying, in that order, and the third works because it does not want to succeed**; **a printed space left blank is read as an answer**; **a lending is not a custody**; **a thing set is not a man's paper**; **a ring opens in the cold and shuts in the warmth and a bench has two numbers a year and has never compared them**; **a person can be arrested for a document and an office can only be closed**; **the marshal's letter contains no names and that is why it is asking for them**; and **the half-inch datum now on a Crown's schedule sheet in a county's remarks column**, which nobody can disprove and the guild's man will not let into the guild's roll.

**Card errors corrected, recorded so the next writer does not inherit them.** (1) The prompt's weekday chain for Longlight was arithmetically incompatible with the audited Greenmonth chain and was rejected; the prose follows item 93. (2) The prompt's "Mara ×4" counted a coda that stayed inside Chapter 35; the delivered shape is **Aren ×5 / Mara ×3 / Tovan ×2**. (3) The prompt's cast plan named "a Council clerk or courier" and delivered a **county clerk of the provincial court** plus a factor and a toll-holder, which is the same work and one name fewer. (4) **The prompt's Ch 39 card said the toll-house steward was "in Bellweather-Living"**, which cannot be true in a block with no crossing; the review repair resolved it the only way that keeps the engine, and the resolution is now on the page in Chapter 39's fifth limit and Chapter 40's whole crisis.

**Repair pass (review of Batch 0004).** A review returned thirty-eight findings; twenty-nine were applied to the prose and the rest recorded as nits. **No plot beat was removed, no chapter was renumbered, no controller file was touched.** The substantive changes are listed in continuity item 110. The two that a later writer must not inherit: **the report of the twenty-first of Longlight is a main-world bridge and it is *capable of being read of more than one bridge in more than one country*, and that ambiguity is entered in the record on purpose** — a later writer must not resolve it silently; and **the chain is three pieces said aloud, a fourth written afterwards, and a fifth (the marshal's letter) that stays in a room over a cooper and has no chain of custody and never will.**

**Second repair pass (review of the first repair pass).** A second review of the repaired batch returned twenty-eight must-fixes, seventeen nits and four defects in the Batch 0005 prompt, and **all of them are applied**; the durable record is `reviews/volume-01/batch-0004.md`. **No plot beat was removed, no chapter was renumbered, no controller file was touched, and no good prose was replaced** — every fix is arithmetic, a date, a distance, a pronoun or a piece of restatement. The one that mattered most: **Chapter 40's coda was opening a Gatehouse crossing** — a ring marked on the far side, the air folding, a market shutting, a ferry turning back, *and not one of them came through* — which broke the batch's zero-crossing budget, sat on the wrong side of the wall four days after Aren's decision, and spent Block 5's failed oath off-stage. **The far side now does the ordinary promised marking of the twenty-sixth at the lowest water** — two men and a clerk, a rule, eight inches in a book, the woman who keeps that road keeping her hands open and nothing coming — and the only thing Bellweather-Living argues about that morning is whether the road is a road or a door, which is put off as the second question. Also removed: **two real-world month names** (a *June* in Ch 37, a *March* in Ch 40); **Millward's nineteen years of holding tolls he bought two years ago**, in four places; **a swapped geography** in which Mara was four hundred miles away and in his own building, and the provincial office was four hundred instead of two hundred, and the Gallow Ford bridge was placed four hundred miles off when it is in this county; **Chapter 40's clock, which ran backwards three times**; **Chapter 40's four candidate actions in three slots**; **the three-instrument taxonomy lectured near-verbatim in both Ch 32 and Ch 35**, now owned by Ch 35 alone; **a seat with two discs in it described as having no glass**; **the fifth *no reason given* counted as the fourth and three words counted as seven**; **a reeve's seven documents two of which were the Crown's**; **Aren's trade reasoning as thirty-one years old at thirty-two**; **Sarn Oyelaran as *he* in a chapter that calls her *she* four times**; **the seventeenth of Longlight as a Thursday when it is a Monday**; **the word *Keeper* as eleven characters when it is six**; **a datum arithmetic with no support, rebuilt so that the quarter inch is in the stone and the nine inches is in the heads**; **a yard idle four days when he was gone nine**; **nine loads followed by *there is no ninth load***; **an eleven-day interval called nine days twice**; **a note dated the third in one chapter and the fourth in another**; **Chapter 38 promising Mara would find out in a month when Chapter 40 delivers four days**; **Rell listing two gifts that never happened in a scene**; and **a circular *no fresh Steppe glass before Longlight* in two chapters set inside Longlight.**

**The question Chapter 41 opens on**

A report about a number off a mark in a coping has been in a filing room for three weeks and the only man who could read it has spent a third crossing of the year saving itself for a day four days off, and on Wednesday four days off two men and a clerk are going to put a number in a book, and a woman who keeps that road is going to write *not here* — and in the main world a writ of enquiry into the custody of a record office is returnable on the ninth of Rainmonth, an answer to it is being written by a woman who is about to be right for the fifth time, and a clerk in a provincial filing room has a memorandum asking who else a man has told, and the answer to that question is in a building with a form on it. **What is on the page:** a chain of four dated pieces in a creditor's chest, a press, and a yard's book; a bench-minute that a bench voted on a Saturday and will be asked for; a town that has said the chalk; a quarter of an inch of datum in a Crown's own hand; and a sill with two used discs in it that has been touched twice since the fourth of Greenmonth and returned nothing.

## Batch 0005 — Volume 01, Chapters 41–50, "The Oath and the Two Bridges"

**Landed.** Chapters 41–50 in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0041.md` to `chapter-0050.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes, POVs **Aren ×5 (41, 42, 45, 47, 49) / Mara ×4 (43, 46, 48, 50) / Tovan ×1 (44)** — the carded shape exactly, no eleventh chapter, no rebalancing. **Zero Gatehouse Order lines, one System display in the whole batch** (the ferrywright's four-line slate of terms, Chapter 49), **one crossing out, one scheduled crossing, one return** — the only block in the volume that has a crossing. **The word "Gatehouse" appears once in the batch, in Chapter 47's notebook, as a standard of measurement for a held road, and that is a measurement and not an appearance.** **No new name, and four slots of the six-name cap left unfilled** — corrected in the Batch 0005 review pass, because an earlier version of this line said *one new name: Edric Hunn*, which was false: **Edric Hunn is first named in `chapter-0026.md` and is a signature on the Crown's letter from Chapter 38, and the batch-0005 prompt itself calls him already established.** What the batch gave him is **his first page and his first room**, and nobody is named who was not already named in Chapters 1–40. **53,719 words**, title lines included. Per chapter: 5,583 / 3,612 / 5,111 / 6,363 / 3,570 / 3,921 / 8,090 / 5,344 / 5,329 / 6,796. **Ch 47 is the volume climax and carries the batch's longest scene; Ch 42, 45 and 46 are the three shortest and are the three to watch in review** (Ch 42 does a great deal in 3,612 words and Ch 45 ends on a question rather than a turn).

**What the batch changed.** A bench that had kept a mark on a ring for six years finds out, in a room, from a stranger, that it has two numbers a year and has never once compared them, and that the difference between the two is the winter and the ring is standing still — and eleven weeks later the second pier goes, not because the ring went but because a **scour hole in the bed of the river** was written down in a minute and nobody could fund the nine-month cutwater it needed. A creditor whose tolls were released by a withdrawn writ in YR 301 puts the four-generation **oath of the crossing** in front of nine men in a hall with two hundred people at the back, and they renew it, and it fails in five days. **Aren's first move is the old move:** he puts a Bind into the span, the load goes into the second pier exactly as the load path says it should, stone cracks in one line, nine feet of it, a hull-hand's rib and a stranger's back are broken, and he stops in the second minute **because it was his** and hands the job back. The work is done by nineteen men he does not know, the **narrower oath** is made up on a drying floor in six hours, the two **stewards** answer the bench-clerk's question of the thirtieth, the repair is stood in the open, and the road opens wide with four sets of hands on the anchor **and Aren translating, which is the work he is actually for.** In Merefen the Old Toll sill opens on the same night, a hand's width, in register, and it is warm for the third time in five months, and nobody in that county can say why. The bell comes back to the town on the twenty-fourth on the bench's own authority — a repair to a parapet, a scrap roll entry, and **Captain Rell lays it down in daylight in front of three hundred people and enters four lines in his own hand saying that if any officer above the county thinks that wrong he may come and take it off the bridge.** The writ register page comes south on a line and two hands and is entered as a claim with **the name on a separate sheet under a separate number**, two copies outside Crown custody, a third in a creditor's book, a fourth in a lock-up on a canal wharf at a farthing of salt. **Edric Hunn comes to Merefen, asks to be recorded, offers rank, back pay, a shield for the Archive and an inspection, and is refused in ninety seconds in a room with a clerk in it.** **Stage 1, the First Stitch, by qualification and not by award.**

**The state at the end of the batch.**
- **Aren:** Stage 1 — one open route, one carryover (a hand's habit, not a working), a permanent road-marked left palm, a return that must be prepared before every crossing by two hands, a date and a witness at each end or it does not open, and **no ability to make the far side answer.** He has no office; a guild roll; a bench seat offered him in Bellweather-Living and refused in a hall; eleven marks and fourpence of guild money owed by a trust in abeyance and two marks three quarters at eight pence a day on a cutting contract; **a copying post at three shillings a week he took on the sixteenth of Fallowmonth, and the county's schedule with the ruled column headed *Keeper* that he refused, and the difference between the two years' answers written out in his own hand and dated.** The verse has a second hole in it, bigger than the one from Mudmonth, and the higher of the two lost notes is the one his mother used to end on.
- **The bell:** back on the Old Toll abutment, on a new cradle, banded with the town's own iron off a gate-post at the end of Weir Street, forty-one rivets, the old split sealed from both sides at the same hour with a dull red oathlight by a Bellweather-Living steward's hands. Nothing in it breaks again in this volume. It is rung by a boy of fifteen on the near abutment and the ferrywright on the far end, because that is the arrangement, and it is not Aren's to ring.
- **The channel:** a **line and two hands**, no glass, a Merefen steward (Tovan Kest) and a Bellweather-Living steward (Sera Quen) with an independent witness at each end, the day written at the top of a page in a book on a north bank, in the guild's roll, in a day-book over a salt merchant and in two slates. The first thing across it is a toll dispute about nine tons of paving stone, argued out in two languages for eleven minutes and settled on a line of a crossing book.
- **Bellweather-Living:** a separate, sovereign community that has lost a pier, refused an inspection, written a narrower oath, taken nine months of debt onto a debt that was let go eleven years ago, and sent one page out of a four-hundred-leaf register in eleven years. It keeps its own dead. The two hundred and eleven is on a monument and the toll is still a farthing too high.
- **Mara:** the first publicly accountable person in this county. A claim number, a public copy, a lending, a transaction, a second form answered with a number instead of four names, a line and its sister line about the brine weights four lines apart, an eighth ruled line in a served schedule that is **still empty**, and a form gone to the county council with a printed space under *Function of the office* that she filled with the founding's own words and left the summary empty.
- **Documents:** the sworn account of the thirtieth of Mudmonth · the finding of the fourth of Greenmonth · the bench minute of the fifteenth of Longlight and the county's refusal on the back of a returned order · the report of the twenty-first with its five limits · a leaf of a provincial ledger with **absent** against the ninth of Greenmonth, certified by Rowan Petch on his own motion and not on a fee · the writ register page, a copy of a copy, not produced in court · **the name on a separate sheet under a separate number** · the Crown's two instruments entered lawful and never mentioned again · a chain of custody made deliberately gappy by a watch captain and a long-hand's declaration on a bridge · a maintenance specification whose eighth line says the number of uses is not a thing this bench may know · **and, in a locked room over a cooper, a Crown's letter of eleven hundred words with a grey wax seal and still no chain of custody on it.**

**Canon the batch invented that later batches must obey.** Items 113–124 in `state/continuity.md`. The load-bearing ones: **the two-disc seat took its third use on the twenty-sixth of Longlight and failed, and the wagoner who took the glass away was right;** **the road is a line and two hands and not a piece of glass;** **a Bind puts the load where the load path says it goes, which is why it works and why it failed;** **a scar is a place a thing came through and can come through again;** **the narrow oath is four lines about a book and about who answers, and it releases every person in that city who has been asked for a promise about a stone;** **the two stewards are Sera Quen and the man with the mallet, named on a sheet, neither signing the other's line;** **the bell comes back on a repair to a parapet, not on a Crown's permission;** **a claim and a name may be read together by a court and by nobody else;** **and the eighth ruled line in the villages' schedule is the city's, and it is still empty.**

**Card and prompt errors corrected, recorded so the next writer does not inherit them.** (1) The prompt's day map gave the third fever as *the twenty-second to the twenty-fourth*; **that is a slip in the prompt.** The established pattern (item 25, Ch 30) is three days beginning on the night a crossing returns, and Chapter 49 opens on the ninth of Fallowmonth. **The fever runs the second, third and fourth and breaks on the fifth.** (2) The prompt's Ch 44–50 plan left **no money in the batch** for a third seat and required the batch's answer to be *somebody else pays*; the delivered answer is that there is no third seat — **the road is a line and two hands** — and the bench pays for the ordinary work out of its own chest, which is a fact and not a paper. (3) The prompt's Ch 45 card had Aren read the scour and the bench record it; **Ch 42 does that, on the day of the marking**, because the oath cannot be renewed knowing a number nobody has read, and Ch 45 is the second examination and the oath. (4) **The prompt's cast row names one character and says + 4 unfilled. The batch spent nothing and left four unfilled,** because **Edric Hunn was never a new name — he is in `chapter-0026.md` and on a signature in Chapter 38, and the prompt calls him established — and the two unnamed wounded in Chapter 47 were deliberately cut without names** (a hull-hand addressed by trade, a stranger named in a book in another world and not on the page). An earlier version of this entry recorded the batch as spending one name on Edric Hunn and claimed a new name was written in Chapter 47; **both claims were false and are corrected here and in continuity item 124.** (5) The prompt's Ch 46 card has Mara fetching a page out of a city; **the page is made in the city and brought south, and Mara is at the receiving end**, because the fourth term is that any paper out of the north comes to her hands, and she cannot fetch anything.

**The question the Volume 02 close inherits, and the three hooks Chapter 51 opens on**

A claimant's form at the Archive about a fever in the eastern counties, which has already produced a list of names to be removed from the public roll, and which is the first thing in eleven years that has come in on somebody else's initiative. A registrar who quotes a fee for a missing-anchor ledger that should not exist. And the writ register's own index, which shows **a second severance authorization in the same hand, four years later, in a county no one in Merefen can name** — and which is now visible to four people in two countries, because a page of that register is in a claim in a filing room over a salt merchant and a fair copy of a fair copy is in the counting house's own press in a city that does not admit to the Crown that it exists. **Edric's office has also stopped asking Aren to stop. It has started asking who else he has told, and the last three minutes of Chapter 50 are a marshal in a county court being told, in a minute, that the answer is no and that the refusal is on the record.**

## Volume 01 close — volume close record and Volume 02 outline

**Landed.** **No chapter prose.** This phase read the fifty chapters, the whole of `outline/`, the whole of `state/` and the whole of `bible/`, and wrote two documents and five state files. Deliverables: **`outline/volumes/volume-01-close.md`** (the volume-close record) and **`outline/volume-02.md`** (the Volume 02 outline, Chapters 51–100). State: `state/continuity.md` gains items **126–146** and a hand-off; `state/current.md` is rewritten for the phase; `state/open-threads.md` gains twenty carry-in threads; `state/chapter-summaries.md` gains a block-level note. **No controller file was touched. `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and stale by design and was not opened for writing. No prompt was created: `workspace/volume-02/volume-01-close/PROMPT.md` was the single prompt for this phase, the five Batch 0001–0005 prompt directories keep their prompts and take their `.done` marker from the controller, and the next prompt — Volume 02 Batch 0006 — is created by that phase and not by this one.**

**What the close changed.** It turned Volume 01 from a plan that had been executed into a book that has been read, and it wrote down what that book turned out to be. Volume 01's argument — that a man who can cross believes that makes him the person who should decide, and that he is instead one of the things other people can hold — **is proved in Chapter 47** and **not proved in Chapter 50**, where he takes a marshal's money, tells a marshal what to do with a shield, and argues a city's refusal in a room the city is not in, and says out loud in a minute that he has four people in a fen town. The close says so in those terms, because a close that praises the volume is a bad close.

**What it corrected.** Eight findings, recorded not fixed. **(1)** The 268-thousand-odd word count, the batch splits, the POV counts and the two Gatehouse lines are confirmed against the files. **(2)** **Batch 0005 added no new name and left four of its six slots unfilled; five files once asserted otherwise and all five are corrected — continuity item 124 stands and this close repeats the correction rather than restating it.** **(3)** **Edric Hunn is not a Volume 02 name.** He is first named in `chapter-0026.md` and is a signature on the Crown's letter in Chapter 38; what Chapter 50 gave him is his first page and his first room, in which he was refused in ninety seconds. **(4)** **The Chapter 10 column does not foot.** The six lines of the trust's account add, in pence, to six hundred and twenty-eight — **thirteen marks and four pence** — and the chapter states **eleven marks and four pence**, which is what Lysa Fenn reads aloud and what four state files carry. The column closes at eleven marks and four pence only if the cartage line reads three shillings and sixpence and not two marks three shillings and sixpence. The gap is **ninety-six pence, two marks exactly and not one** — an earlier version of this entry called it a one-mark error and that was wrong by a factor of two, and taking the two marks off the cartage line is the whole of the repair. **The prose is wrong, the stated total is canon, and no chapter is changed**, because a two-mark error on one line is invisible to a reader and the figure the book says out loud in four places must stay the figure the book says out loud in four places. The standing rule this produces: **no Volume 02 figure may be derived from that column by scaling it, and every money column is added in pence and footed before it goes on the page.** **(5)** The four carried corrections are stated once, in the close, and are binding: **no third seat and no glass; the road is a line and two hands; Bellweather-Living's two stewards are Sera Quen and the man with the mallet and Aren is neither; the name is on a separate sheet under a separate number.** **(6)** Item 88's instruction not to name the Hushwater crossing-book keeper is **reversed once, deliberately**, and the reason is that Volume 02's far end needs a person who has been on the page since Chapter 23 refusing to let a record be copied, and that Volume 01's defect was not the spending of names but the rationing of them until seventeen consequential people had none — the toll-man and the toll-clerk on the north steps are one man and an earlier count held nineteen. **All four Hushwater women remain separate people.** **(7)** The series time ledger is read as **per-volume, not cumulative** — the eighteen rows sum to one hundred and forty-three months, which is the stated twelve-year span — and Volume 02's seven months is therefore seven months *of the volume*, not of the series. **(8)** Rainmonth and Harvestmonth have never had their lengths fixed in this project; the Volume 02 chain is built forward from the twenty-ninth of Fallowmonth on thirty-day months and says so, and a later volume running past them must fix them first.

**What Volume 02 is, in one paragraph.** The County of Ash is a clay county on a river called the Kiln, with a market town called Kilnholt, nine hundred and forty-one hearths, **two thousand four hundred and fifty people**, and two thousand nine hundred and six fired tiles on a roll of persons — **four hundred and fifty-six more tiles than people, and somebody has been fired twice, and nobody has ever counted, because a county counts fires and does not count itself.** The three figures foot and the reader is meant to do the subtraction: **2,906 − 2,450 = 456**, and the population is exact because an approximate one cannot be subtracted from anything. A person there can soften, which means going back to being clay, and the county's answer is to fire them, which makes the mark the only durable account of them, and a person who has been fired is alive, employed, and no longer consulted. **There is no bell in the County of Ash and there never was one, because nothing was ever refused there, and it has no word for *refused* that does not also mean *lost*.** The Crown's escalation is not a new paper; it is **a count of persons instead of a list of names**, which is lawful, needs no court, names nobody, and is the answer to Edric Hunn's question — and it is answered by a keeper of records who does not notice until three dated lines come back. Halven takes a shield and sells the missing-anchor ledger for it, and the endorsement cannot be withdrawn. The second severance lands inside the volume as an empty *returned* column, an *answered* column eleven days after the tender, and a press-mark of **press four, die eleven** that the reader can check and Aren cannot, and the name goes into **a creditor's fair copy in a city that does not admit to the Crown that it exists**, which is the first document in the series held by a person in a path. And the volume's failure is **not an arch**: it is a form, and the form is a rite that looks exactly like consent, proposed by the man who can see load paths, applied at the scale of four hundred people, with a bound that is a promise about four hundred people to one office — the shape a severance writ has — and it works for eleven weeks and then a man who was restored softens because a stranger read a list aloud four times a day in a county where everybody could already read. **Nobody dies. The volume's catastrophe is a person who is still there, correctly recorded in three books, who does not know which kiln he fires.**

**The question Chapter 51 opens on.** The third of Embermonth's work is three days old and a form has come back from an office that cannot name its own authority, with a column headed *Keeper* and a marginal instruction that manufactures a keeper out of a word, and a woman has come down the road with it who is not a member of anything and who wants that no family in this country is ever again removed from a record by a name nobody entered, and she is right, and she is the vector, and the reader should be able to see it by Chapter 55. **And underneath it, on a desk in a filing room over a salt merchant, is the second answer to a four-hundred-word list, in a hand that is not a clerk's, about a man who has been struck off a roll of persons and cannot be found because he is alive and is not consulted — and a county clerk has looked it up and found that there is no county of Ash on any map in this county.**

**Guardrails honoured by this phase, in one place.** No chapter prose was written. `outline/ending.md` was not moved, and every Volume 02 plan in the outline is checkable against it. The calendar was not re-derived: continuity items 93, 111 and 112 own the weekday chain and the fever pattern, the close records the Chapter 10 arithmetic fault rather than re-deriving it, and the Volume 02 chain is built forward from an audited anchor. **The four thousand and the seven hundred and twenty are not added together anywhere in either new document; the eighth ruled line is the city's and is still empty; Hushwater Reach's near sixty and Hushwater's eighty are not printed together and no character is given the gap.** The mechanism is not explained: the private-keeper clause is named in an office's hand and understood by nobody, the palm is a trade, the stance is unattributable, and the tile on the coping, the soft tile with a river in it, and the roadmark are all left exactly as unexplained as the bell. **No melody cost was added** — the first-crossing loss of one verse's remembered pitch remains the only fixed one, and Volume 02's costs are a morning, a stance and a date in the wrong calendar. **No new final enemy:** Edric Hunn is the antagonist of the whole book, the Office of Provincial Registration is an office, and the word "Culling Order" appears once more in the whole of Volume 02, in a claimant's mouth in Chapter 54, with no member attached to it. **The volume names eighteen new characters across five batches against a cap of six per batch — 5 + 4 + 4 + 3 + 2, with twelve slots unfilled and eighteen and twelve making the thirty the cap allows — plus one four-year-old who does not count and one returned face who is not a new name, and gives every one of the eighteen a want in their own voice. An earlier version of this entry said twenty-four and did not foot; see continuity item 150.**

## Batch 0006 — Volume 02, Chapters 51–60, "The Form and the Registrar"

**Landed.** Chapters 51–60 in `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0051.md` to `chapter-0060.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes. POVs **Aren ×5 (51, 53, 57, 59, 60) / Mara ×2 (52, 58)**; Chapter 54 is the count and the claimant and is Mara's; **Chapters 59 and 60 are the same morning from the two ends, which is the outline's design and is not a duplicated scene** — 59 is the far end of the Kiln and 60 is a gateway on Weir Street, and the empty witness line on the printed return is the same line in both. **34,276 words**, title lines included, and the column foots. Per chapter: 2,645 / 3,112 / 2,902 / 3,318 / 3,204 / 2,958 / 4,517 / 3,661 / 3,398 / 4,561. **Ch 57 and Ch 60 are the two to watch in review** — both are long, both carry a document at full length, and Ch 57's spine is a man saying a word in a room with a clerk in it for the fourth time in eleven years and a clerk refusing to write it down.

**What the batch changed.** A Crown's office that has been asking for names discovers that it can ask for a **figure** instead, and a figure is lawful and names nobody and cannot be refused without refusing a number — and a yard, a bench and a filing room in one week arrive at that arithmetic independently, in three trades, and none of the three calls it craft. **The count is eleven and it is not four, and it goes in a day-book against no person because a roll in that town has a *Keeper* column on it now.** **Halven takes the marshal's shield, sells the missing-anchor ledger for it, and discloses it unasked in a form that cannot protect him**, and the disclosure names a county — **Ash** — and a disposition word that is a kiln word: **FIRED.** A registrar of thirty-four years and a keeper of records both say kiln out loud in a room and neither knows what it means. **The second severance is read, in Chapter 58, off the front leaf of a creditor's writ register, and the reading produces one fact and three written refusals**: not Ash, not the same hand, not the paper of the first of Fallowmonth — and a press-mark, which is the only thing in the case that cannot be argued with, ties an index leaf with **no name in it** to a body line with **a name in it**, in two different hands, in a book that does not leave a city that does not admit to the Crown exists. **A provincial clerk walks three days to ask a bridgewright whether he can file a return that answers nothing, and decides in a gateway in front of four men, and the cost is a career and a night in an inn at his own expense.** And on the ninth of Embermonth at the fourth hour **the road opens onto a county that has not agreed to be a road**, a woman with a set of tile-cutters' tools refuses it by asking him one question, and **he does not argue and does not go through, and he says the one sentence that the rest of the volume is built on: *she did not open it and she was right, and I am not the man who sends anybody, and it made no difference, which is how I know she was right.*** Then he goes anyway, on his own legs, over a bridge, into a market town, with a man in a gateway as a witness who keeps nothing and is paid fourpence a week out of a private box, and the far end answers his trade and refuses his name, and the first entry in a column in nineteen years that is not a place anybody in that county can walk to reads **north**.

**The state at the end of the batch.**
- **Aren:** Stage 1, one open route (Bellweather-Living) and one open but unconsented line (the County of Ash). No office. On the roll of the Merefen bridge guild. **The printed three-week figure is now in his notebook in arithmetic for the first time: six days of absence against a printed warning of twenty-one is minus fifteen days, and the warning was longer than the absence, and the warning did not fail — he is what the figure measures.** The fourth warmth of the palm is dated: **the twenty-fourth of Fallowmonth**, the day the bell went back and the channel opened, and the only one of the four he did not cause. He has said **bell** out loud in a room with a clerk in it for the fourth time in eleven years, and this time the clerk is a provincial clerk, and the clerk has refused to write it down and given the reason. He is out of the county from the ninth of Embermonth, and **the Sedge Reach scour is unread and the bench's mark-day reading is not taken because he is not here, and three Fridays of the copying post — nine shillings — are in the county's hands.**
- **Mara:** keeper of the Bellweather Claims Archive, four years and a half, **in a new room — one room, a loft over the chandler's at the north end, a door on a lane, fourpence a year, the smallest paid item in the business for the second time.** She has answered a Crown's form with a county; she has put a record in a place she has just written cannot be sued for and done it in that order on purpose; she has entered one fact and three refusals about the second severance; she has looked at the eighth ruled line twice and wanted to fill it twice and written down that **the wanting is not the same wanting**; and she has entered the shape of the holding this case needs and does not have. **She has a paper from the County of Ash naming two people and dated in a calendar she cannot check.**
- **Tovan:** the yard's rule holds and is load-bearing for the third time. **He read out loud, in a gateway, the line he had kept under a rule since the second of Fallowmonth**, and gave the reason he has not asked his brother where he goes, and **the reason is that the day I ask is the day you have to answer.** He pays a witness fourpence a week out of his own box — the first time he has paid anybody in that yard for turning up — and **enters the fee and the hour and not the reason**, because a man's reason is a thing believed and a yard is not an office. **Four weeks and a day in the yard.**
- **The four who can see the index:** Alis Merrow, Aren Kest, Mara Vey, Rowan Petch. **The count is eleven and a roll in this town has a *Keeper* column on it now.**
- **The three hooks, as documents after this batch:** (1) a claimant's form about a fever in the eastern counties with eleven names and a column headed *removal* and a column headed *ground*, and a twelfth that can still answer; (2) a registrar's endorsement numbered 4 of the season that cannot be withdrawn, and a volume gone out of the county under a receipt for a box; (3) **an index leaf with nine lines, an empty county cell, an empty *returned* cell, an *answered* cell eleven days after the tender, and press four, die eleven, second impression of the season.**

**Canon the batch invented that Batch 0007 must obey.** Continuity items 153–172. The load-bearing ones: **the private-keeper clause is in print in an office's hand and nobody understands it;** **the three-week figure is printed in the same hand as a standard of measurement and the error is measured against it;** **a press register is a record of what a man said he did with a shelf, not a record of a shelf;** **a returnable paper with a blank in it is read as *nothing to state*, and a question closed by a return is a question nobody has to ask again;** **a fillable cell that a keeper *wants* to fill is a mouth and not a cell;** **the road is a line and two hands and a witness at each end, and the far-end witness line being empty is why it did not open, and it did not fail;** **a clerk who keeps a word out of a file for a man is doing the man's work, and refuses;** **and the County of Ash has no word for *refused* that does not also mean *lost*, and no bell, and a roadkeeper called *the hand*, and a roll of tiles that is a record, and a column headed *hand* that a woman will not fill.**

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so the next writer does not inherit them as errors.** (1) `outline/volume-02.md`'s Chapter 58 card says the two day-book lines about the eighth ruled line are **"a year apart."** They are not: the first is the twenty-ninth of Fallowmonth, YR 312, and the second is the eighth of Embermonth, YR 312, **ten days apart.** Canon wins and the prose says ten days. (2) The Chapter 57 card has Rowan Petch *walk* to the provincial town, with a day's hard walk at six leagues and the town further than four days' hard walk. Two hundred miles cannot be walked in four days and he has a return due on the eleventh, so he goes **on the county post at the first hour on the eighth and comes back on the ninth's post**, three days on the road at his own cost, which is the same beat and is the only possible mechanism. (3) The Chapter 57 card says the answer about the press register comes from "the only man in the county who reads a press register for a living." That is **Halven**, a returned face and not a new name, who is still in Merefen on the seventh because he is waiting for a counter-signature on a receipt for a box that is never going to be completed. **The flat line *a register answers what was entered on the shelf and nothing else* is reserved for Ivet Salter of the third press in Chapter 79 and is not spent here**; Halven's version is a different sentence and a worse one. (4) The Chapter 59 card's *"the bell on the coping carries a mark that is not the Bellweather founder's mark"* is written as **a fired clay tile with a rope on it and no yoke, mistaken for a bell from two hundred yards in bad light** — because the County of Ash has no bell and never had one, and the object is the volume's last planted mystery in Chapter 85. The card is honoured in effect and the Chapter 85 reveal is protected. (5) **The Chapter 59 card says the woman at the far end does not give her name, and she does not — and the name is on the page in Chapter 60, in the far end's own paper of the eighth, because a county that has never refused anything has never had a reason to withhold one.** That is the only way the cast plan's **Ilsa Soke** can be named in this batch without breaking the refusal. (6) Chapter 60 opens the far side's costing: the Chapter 60 card's *main-world absence twenty-six days* is stated by the narrator in the paragraph on what the scour cost and is not known to him at the time.

**A date correction made in Chapters 53, 54 and 56 after the batch was closed, recorded here so that no writer re-derives it and mistakes it for a fresh error.** The block's day map is item 153 and it is the authority for every one of these. **Seven lines of prose and two lines of state were wrong and are now right, and the whole of it is one cause: the batch was laid out on a different week before item 147 was audited, and the weekdays of that layout survived in the text after the dates had been moved.** **(1) The crossing out is the first of Fallowmonth at the fourth hour** — item 113, Chapter 47, Chapter 60 and Aren's own notebook all say so — and Chapter 53's notebook now reads *the road was opened on the first of Fallowmonth* and *I went out on the first of Fallowmonth at the fourth hour*. **(2) Toban's reason for not voting, in the same chapter, put his brother's going *on Thursday at the fourth hour of a market morning*,** which was the day before a chapter in which Aren is standing in the yard, and a month out with the card's *gone a month*, and on a day the county does not trade; it is now **the first of Fallowmonth at the fourth hour of a Monday morning**, and it is the same date the notebook gives nine paragraphs later. **(3) The carrier's two boys said a stranger came through *on the ninth*,** against the notebook's first; it is now **on the first**, and a boy who does not name the month is why the report reads as rumour and not as a fact. **(4) Chapter 56's Tovan, who has wanted to ask Mara where his brother went every day *since the first*, is dated: since the first of Embermonth,** because the chapter is the sixth and the same speech says he has been not saying it for five days, and the sixth less five is the first of Embermonth and not a Fallowmonth date. **(5) The factor's stone went *on Tuesday*** — a day on which the yard demonstrably still had it, since Chapter 51 sets it standing on end on the Wednesday — and is now **on Thursday**, which is the only day between. **(6) Aren's notebook said the dated form *came on Tuesday*** — before it was dated — and now says **came on the third**, which is the day the carrier put it on the bench. **(7) The reeve's bench clerk asked her in a corridor *on Tuesday*** and she has thought about it for two days in a chapter dated Saturday the fourth; it is now **on Thursday**, which is two days. **(8) Mara says the office sent that form to the yard *on Tuesday*** and it is now **on Friday**, the day the carrier brought it. **The two state lines: the Chapter 56 per-chapter summary had read *her brother* and *since the ninth*, and the brother is Toban's and not Mara's; and the Chapter 54 summary repeated *on Tuesday* for the corridor.** **Seven lines of prose and two lines of state, and no card, no beat and no planned event was touched.**

## Batch 0007 — Volume 02, Chapters 61–70, "Twenty-Three Days"

**Landed.** Chapters 61–70 in `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0061.md` to `chapter-0070.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes. POVs **Aren ×9 (61–69) / Mara ×1 (70)** — the carded shape exactly. **37,521 words** by `wc -w` on the chapter files, title lines included, and the per-chapter column foots. Per chapter: **3,290 / 3,563 / 4,131 / 2,404 / 3,666 / 3,690 / 3,244 / 4,448 / 4,658 / 4,427** (as revised by the review repair pass; the batch landed at 37,481 and the twelve applied findings moved it by forty words, none of it new prose). **Chapters 68 and 69 are the two to watch in review** — both are long, both end on a document (a magistrate's three reasons minuted, and a tile with five lines on it), and Ch 69's spine is a man writing down the mistake he is about to make in the correct technical vocabulary, in a notebook, four months early, and going on.

**Cast.** **Four new names of the six the card allows — Pernel Quist, Colm Arreld, Hesper Lound, Ostry Vaile — and two slots unfilled, as planned.** **Bel, four, of Kilnholt, is named and is a child and does not count against the cap.** Ilsa Soke is a returned face and **is not named in this batch**; she is *the hand of the Ninebridge* and *the hand*, she does not correct him, and the reason is on the page in Chapter 64. **The kiln clerk is addressed as *the man with the ledger* throughout and his name is withheld by design.** Everyone else in the block is addressed by trade: the assizes' clerk, the man at the cutting bench, the man at the loading end, the boy with the slate, a man called Ostrand who is dead, a magistrate of Merefen four hundred miles off, a man in the yard with a rule. **The volume total is now nine names of eighteen** (5 + 4), and the unfilled slots are 1 + 2 = 3 of twelve.
