the 254 is on the page twice and is never scaled from anything; **the 2,906 is the county's own figure about itself and is spoken as such in Chapter 100 and is never in a sum with a Crown's four thousand or a served schedule's seven hundred and twenty, and the last two are never put together anywhere in the block.** **The two eighth ruled lines are in different chapters in different rooms in front of different people and no character puts them together**: Chapter 98 looks at the served schedule's eighth ruled line for the third time and does not fill it, and the guild's maintenance specification of two leaves with its own eighth line about a seat is not in the batch at all. **The city is still not on the served schedule at the end of the volume.** The drift figure is stated **only in Chapter 91, which is main world, and only as main-world absence minus the printed three weeks: eighty-eight minus twenty-one is sixty-seven days**, and the path interval is nowhere subtracted from the warning and the figure never appears again in the block. **No two of the volume's five blanks are in one room, in one chapter or in one sentence, and no character says that any two of them are the same**: the fifth space with no sixth is Ash and is not in this block, the county cell in the creditor's index leaf is Chapter 92 alone, the four ruled cells in a provincial shelf register are not in this block, the served schedule's eighth line is Chapter 98, and the six lines in a county's book with nothing against them are Ash and are not in this block. **The tile on the coping is not touched, not looked at and not explained**: it appears once, at the close of Chapter 99, as a thing a keeper in a fen county has no record of, and **the device on it is named by nobody and the chapter's last line is a keeper saying that a thing you do arithmetic on is not a thing you have looked at.** **The four carried corrections stand:** no glass in the Old Toll sill and no third seat; the road is a line and two hands, a date, and a witness at each end who is not the person keeping the road, and nobody says afterwards that a road failed; Bellweather-Living's two stewards are Sera Quen and the man with the mallet and Aren is neither; and **the name of Edric Hunn is on a separate sheet under a separate number and is never on the same sheet in the same county as the claim — and in this batch his name is not written at all, and the only thing that has come forward is a press-mark.** **Aren is not a steward, holds no bench seat, stands in for no steward, and is never a custodian; in Chapter 100 he is a road warden of the Merefen bridge guild on the Sedge Reach deck with a tool bag, counting load points out of habit, and he is not there as the hand of anything.** **No narrator in any of the ten chapters uses *this volume*, *this chapter*, *the reader*, *the author* or *the block*.** The surviving *this book* instances are all diegetic: three in Aren's own notebook in Chapter 91, two inside quoted documents in Chapters 95 and 99, and the two that were in Chapter 100 were rewritten into the room before the batch was closed. **No narrator in any of the ten chapters knows more than the room** — the County of Ash reaches this block as a fair copy down a line, itemised, and as four lines copied in a keeper's own hand, and there is no chapter in which anybody is standing in that county.

**What the batch changed.** Two things, and one of them is not repaired.

**One. The breach.** A keeper of records finds the cost of the second crossing with the one instrument she cannot stop using, which is a date, and **she does not ask him.** He tells her in a room, in the ordinary way, on the fifth of Greenmonth, that a schedule of eight village re-readings was sent on the eighteenth of the ninth month of a county's own year, and the fair hand writes it down, and then the fair hand goes to the day-book and compares the entry against it and finds that **two dates in his account are dates of that county and not of this one, given in the wrong frame, twice, in one morning, without his noticing** — a bridgewright would not have noticed and a keeper of records cannot not, and the finding is not arrived at by asking him anything. He tells her to write it down. She refuses, because **a date is the only instrument she has that she cannot stop using and a date in a day-book in her own hand is a document about a man.** What she does instead is the thing that makes it a breach rather than a scene: **she rules a sheet, writes the twelve months of that county's year on it, and tears it up in front of him, because a conversion of two ends into a middle is not a fact but a number she made up, and every line under it would be a line she had invented, and in nine years a man would take a figure off it and use it and would not know he was using her.** The reason she gave in Fallowmonth for not asking is not spoken aloud in this chapter and is not written again, and the new thing on the page is the change: **a protection is a thing done to somebody, and the not asking is now a wound, and a wound she is choosing not to open is worse than a question. She noticed the change on the twenty-ninth of Fallowmonth, which was the seventh day of the promise, wrote it down on the fifth of Wolfmonth, and the promise is two hundred and twenty-three days old at the fifth of Greenmonth, and the lateness is in the seven months since and not in the noticing.** The volume does not repair it. Volume 03 does not repair it. Volume 04's chosen intimacy is bought with it.

**Two. A man who has lost the order of an ordinary morning with his brother.** Six or seven things in an order, a particular Tuesday, a room over a cooper, a yard on Weir Street, eleven years of it. **He can still do every one of them and cannot do them in that order and does not know the order, and it is in a notebook in a coat and it is not in a form.** The Tovan beat opens in Chapter 91 in a room and not a yard — the brother watches five seconds of it and does not say one word about any of them — and is **spent in Chapter 97, in a yard, in his own words, and spent there and not improved on: *I am not going to re-teach you your own brother.*** With the fairness he insists on and will not be moved off: you can do all of it, and I have watched you do it, and if I told you what I think the order was you would do it my way and not yours, and in about a year you would be doing a thing in a yard that is not a morning. **And it is not cruelty and it is not healing, and the damage is that eleven months ago he said a different sentence in a gateway so that on the day he said nothing his brother would not think it was his brother, and he has thought about that sentence for eleven weeks and has decided the new one is worse, because the old one was about a thing you would lose and this one is about a thing he is not going to do — and a man can be held to a thing he is not going to do.** Nobody in that yard has the word brother in a scene. The estrangement is not healed and is not going to be healed in this volume.

And the rest of it. **A second count is answered with a figure and no names, and the figure is one thousand six hundred and nine, and it is the number of people who have come through a door in four and a half years, and a magistrate beats the instrument with its own construction in nine minutes and is frightened of her own answer because it is built on the fact that a court is not a person, and a county council resolves that the Archive is a body corporate on a form whose printed space for a thing's *name* was filled and whose printed space for its *purpose* was left empty, and nobody in the room says the sentence and the chapter does not say it and a yard does.** An officer of that office comes down a road in daylight, asks to be recorded before anybody offers her a chair, has the asking minuted with her name spelled whole, and says the sentence the volume has been building toward in a county where figures are cheaper than names: **in four years there will be a number in a file with no name attached to it and nobody will be able to say whose hand made it, and it is not coming because anybody thought of it in a room with a clock on the wall, it is coming because it is cheaper than names and there is not one instrument in this kingdom that can make it more expensive.** A counting house in a city that does not admit to the Crown that it exists breaks all four of its own conditions in four leaves in a fortnight, and the reason it breaks them is not denied but simply not put next to the thing, and a clerk of twenty-one signs the four-hundred-leaf register in his grandmother's frightened hand and asks for a sentence about her to be written down in a copy and for nothing else, and **no want is stated for him because there is no column for one and the absence of a column is the point.** A registrar's disclosure and a marshal's endorsement are on one page for the first time in the case — a book bought and a writ discharged, number 4 of the season — and **nobody is charged, the endorsement stands and is not withdrawn because the office that gave it has no procedure for withdrawing an endorsement and will not build one inside a quarter, and the marshal is told nothing and will learn it in a file.** A bill goes out of a room with a line in it that has no figure against it, because a scour at the nose of the third pier has been a washtub since the sixth of Mudmonth and nobody has read it, and the line is read on the twenty-fourth, and five days later the county's surveyor has paid a man six pence to write a report on it, **which is the smallest thing anybody in this county has paid for in two years and the only one anybody in either country has been glad of.** **The list is not burned. It is superseded**, in the county's own book, in a room, with a four-year-old physician's finding read out and the three hands named and the third still a letter, and the magistrate says the four sentences that are the whole of his testimony, and the last of them asks the county to hear the word *stranger* from him and not from the man off the road. Ash's dead stay Ash's. **Two hundred and fifty-four tiles are being ground back into clay at a cost footed on a board, on a schedule, in the standing water, and about thirteen men are still out of work and the number is written down, and the softening has slowed and not stopped.** And the volume's permanent act is a holding, made in the country where the register is: **a roll of nine hundred and forty-one hearths entered as a transaction at a farthing of salt in a creditor's press in a city that does not admit to the Crown that it exists, with the argument minuted in the creditor's own words, and the minute asked to be put where a Crown's man will find it in about nine years, and the man who says so is arguing against his own city, at his own cost, and says he would like it in the ordinary place and does not much care and thinks he is a fool for putting it there.** And on the same leaf, a county column of dispositions running to eleven other counties, **eight of which have a boundary oath renewed in public on a stone in a year anybody there can name, and three of which have nothing in the leaf at all** — an arithmetic pattern and not a revelation, seen by exactly two people in the world, entered as a question and not as a finding, and the eight left where they are.

**The state at the close of the hundredth chapter, against `outline/volume-02.md` §10, point by point.**

- **§10.1, the fever contained without erasing names.** **Landed and exceeded.** The list is superseded in the county's own book with the finding read out and the three hands named (Ch 98); Ash's dead stay Ash's in a one-line entry; **254 fired tiles are being ground back into clay at twelve marks one shilling and sevenpence over two years on a schedule, in the standing water, with a bench**; about thirteen men out with the number written down; the softening slowed and not stopped, six boards against forty, entered with the hour and no opinion. **The softening is slowed and not stopped and the volume says so in a line in a fair copy and does not celebrate it.**
- **§10.2, the roll in three hands, no single office able to add or remove, the third allowed to be wrong.** **Landed, in Ch 98 and Ch 99.** The three hands are named in the county's own minute; **the third is a creditor's press in a city that does not admit to the Crown that it exists, entered as a transaction at a farthing of salt** (Ch 99); the rule of two-of-three with the third permitted to be wrong is minuted in the creditor's own words, and **nobody in this volume resolves the third hand and the bench has entered that it cannot say whether the third is a hand and has resolved not to find out this year.**
- **§10.3, Stage 2 by qualification and not by award, in a filing room, with a witness, on the printed terms; the first-crossing melody cost remains the only fixed one.** **Landed, and the location moved.** The qualification and the printed terms were landed in **Chapter 90**, four hundred miles off, in a shed, with a witness — which is the outline's own design, since the terms are printed on the back of a form that was in a drawer in a shed and the stage cannot be got into a filing room four hundred miles from the man who can be asked about it. **No melody cost is added in this batch and none may be, and the word does not appear in any of the ten chapters.** The witness for the terms was the hand of the Ninebridge and the second term was written out in an office's hand in Chapter 90, exactly as §10 requires.
- **§10.4, the loss of the order of an ordinary morning with Tovan Kest; Tovan will not re-teach him.** **Landed, in Ch 91 and spent in Ch 97**, in a yard, in his own words, once, not improved on, and the fairness about the six or seven things is in it. **The estrangement is not healed and is not going to be healed in this volume, and the damage is permanent in it.**
- **§10.5, the concealment found with a date; the not asking changed from a protection into a wound; working trust broken; not repaired, not to be repaired in Volume 03.** **Landed, in Ch 91 and Ch 92, and it is the block's first half.** Two path dates in the main-world frame, twice, in one morning, without his noticing, found by a comparison and not by a question. The change entered with the hour and the day noticed and the lateness. **Not repaired. Not to be repaired in Volume 03. Volume 04's chosen intimacy is bought with it.**
- **§10.6, the second severance: press four, die eleven, a later impression of the season; the reader can check it and Aren cannot; Alis Merrow enters the signing office's name into a record of his own, at his own cost, in a matter in which no court in this county has jurisdiction; a creditor's press is the worst place in the world to keep a roll of persons; the argument minuted; the two halves put side by side in Chapter 894.** **Landed in Ch 95, with one deviation and one thing the card got wrong about itself.** The die and the press and the two impressions are on the page and the ninth line is the only one of nine marked *answered*, eleven days after the tender. Merrow's entry is a fair copy, signed, made at his own cost, entered as produced by a creditor's office in a matter in which no court in this county has jurisdiction, and it is the first document in the series held by a person in a path. The four-line argument is minuted in his own words and the minute is asked to be put where a Crown's man will find it in about nine years. **The second severance does not open inside this volume and the join is not stated once.** The Chapter 894 opening is untouched and unspent.
- **§10.7, the Crown's instruments at the close of the hundredth chapter; the count went from four descriptions to eleven and from eleven to a figure for the county, and no name has been written down in any Crown's hand since the twenty-seventh of Fallowmonth.** **Landed, with the figure now carrying a number: one thousand six hundred and nine, and it expired on the ninth of Greenmonth.** The two Crown instruments found lawful on the ninth of Fallowmonth and never mentioned again in that court; the instruction of the ninth of Longlight still in force and superseded by nothing; the third writ discharged; the provincial ledger with *absent* against the ninth of Greenmonth; and **two dated forms from the Office of Provincial Registration with a figure in each and no name in either.** The count went from four descriptions (Ch 54) to eleven (Ch 54, sent the ninth of Frostmonth) to a figure for the county (Ch 93), and **no name has been written down in any Crown's hand in this county since the twenty-seventh of Fallowmonth, and that is still the volume's political victory and still how the Crown got further in seven months than it got in eleven years.**
- **§10.8, the bell is on the Old Toll bridge with a band of forty-one rivets and a dull red seam in it, rung on a named day by a boy and a ferrywright and, from the twenty-ninth of Greenmonth, sometimes by a tile-cutter four hundred miles off; it is not Aren's and he does not ring it.** **Landed as carded and the two pairs of hands are on the page.** In Chapter 100 the bell is rung at the fourth hour and a half on the Old Toll bridge by a boy of sixteen with two years of an apprenticeship who is still not good at it, and a carpenter who is a hand and not a keeper, **and at the far end of the line, on a coping on a river this county does not have, a tile-cutter of forty-one and a person nobody in Merefen has ever seen strike what they have, which is not a bell and is not nothing, and the fair copy says only that there is no bell in that county and that nobody there has the word for it.** The arrangement is not his and he does not ring it and the yard's book has the hour and no name in it.
- **§10.9, the eighth ruled line is still empty, the brine weights are still cited and not filed, the two day-book lines about them are five days apart and are read together, and the roll built out of them is Chapter 892's.** **Landed.** The eighth ruled line is looked at for the third time in Chapter 98, in the eighth month after the first look and the seventh month after the second, and is not filled, and the wanting is entered as not the same wanting and the reason is not given. **The brine weights are still cited and not filed, the two day-book lines are five days apart in one week of Fallowmonth and are read together, and the reason is written out in full for the first time in the batch because the reason has changed: a ledger is a claim, and this office has learned this year, from a county four hundred miles off, that a claim about people is not a record of them.** The roll of the dead is not built and the not building is entered.
- **Also required by the prompt and not in §10: the held drift table.** Closed and printed twice, in Ch 91 only. **Crossing A: twenty-six days of absence, path interval twenty-three days, warning three weeks, error five days, fever the fifth to the seventh of Wolfmonth breaking on the eighth. Crossing B: eighty-eight days of absence, path interval nine months and six days, warning three weeks, error sixty-seven days, fever the second to the fourth of Greenmonth breaking on the fifth at about the sixth hour.** The warning has not changed and the office still cannot say whose figure it is, and the ratio is about three-quarters of the absence and is not a constant, and the path interval is nowhere subtracted from the warning.

**Canon the batch invented that the Volume 02 close must obey.** Continuity items **235 onward**.

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so that no writer re-derives them as errors.** (1) **The prompt's Chapter 94 line "eight off Lysa Fenn's finding" is not possible and the day map is the authority.** Lysa Fenn's finding is entered on the ninth of Greenmonth in the room in Chapter 93 and Chapter 94 is the twelfth, so it is **three days old, not eight**, and the chapter says three. (2) **The prompt's Chapter 93 "the figure is lawful and it has expired" is ambiguous and the chapter resolves it: the figure cannot expire, the instrument does, and the instrument is spent the moment it is answered.** The figure the Archive returned is **one thousand six hundred and nine**, being the number of persons who have come through the door of that office in four years and a half, taken out of a day-book, and the number is new and is not derived from any figure in the case. (3) **The prompt's Chapter 95 card places the counting house in a chapter that is a main-world day, and the counting house is in a city that does not admit to the Crown that it exists.** The chapter is therefore set in Merefen and the counting house's material reaches it as a signed fair copy walked in on the back of a toll-taker in a basket, and Merrow's own four conditions are on the page as the thing he has broken. **Nothing is staged in a path, no narrator is in a path, and no crossing occurs.** (4) **The prompt's Chapter 98 card places the county's bench, the magistrate's testimony and the re-grounding in a chapter, and all of it is a county that is not on any map in this kingdom.** The chapter is a fair copy of a bench's minute read in a room over a weigh-house, and the physician's four pages did not travel and were not expected to, **because a minute that says a thing was read out is a fact about a room four hundred miles off and not a document in this one.** The same method carries Chapters 99 and 100. (5) **The prompt's Chapter 97 says the Sedge Reach scour is read "in Thawmonth's dry weeks" and that Toban's yard does "the ordinary autumn work."** Greenmonth is not in Thawmonth and it is not autumn in this calendar, and the scour is read on the **twenty-fourth of Greenmonth**, which is the day the bench named and the day the road shuts, and the chapter calls the work the ordinary work and names no season it does not have to. **The scour's age is also corrected on the page: from the sixth of Mudmonth to the twenty-fourth of Greenmonth is most of a year and not eleven weeks, and the chapter says most of a year. Chapter 70's "eight weeks" for the same scour, as at the fifth of Wolfmonth, is an inherited error in Batch 0007's prose and is recorded here rather than silently edited, and the close should decide whether it stands.** (6) **The prompt's Chapter 91 says the bag has "a half tile in it" and the on-page logic of Chapter 90 puts the object north already.** The chapter keeps the prompt's tile and explains the arithmetic: the first went north on the fourth of that county's ninth month in the bag of the woman who keeps the road, **he has no way of finding out whether it is in her press and is not going to ask, and the one in his bag is a second that he made himself behind a kiln with a saw mark on the back of it, and the reason he made a second is that he could not think of a way to ask, and a man who makes two of a thing to avoid asking a question has already decided what the answer is.** (7) **The prompt says the Sedge Reach is a washtub "since the sixth of Mudmonth" and Ch 100's card and the day map put the twenty-ninth of Greenmonth at the lowest water of the year, while Chapter 53 has a character say a wall job would finish "about a week before the water is at the low of the year."** The chapter prints the low of the year on the twenty-ninth and does not explain it, as instructed, and the nose of the third pier is out of the water for the first time since the sixth of Mudmonth, which is the one fact the date has to do work on. **Chapter 53's line is a character being loose about a river and is not a date and no later writer may convert it into one.** (8) **The prompt's Chapter 92 plant "it is a year late" cannot be true and the chapter says the true thing instead.** The promise is the twenty-second of Fallowmonth, YR 312 and the chapter is the fifth of Greenmonth, YR 313, which is **two hundred and twenty-three days, not a year**; and Chapter 70 has the noticing on the twenty-ninth of Fallowmonth with the writing on the fifth of Wolfmonth, so the noticing came on the **seventh day** of the promise. **The chapter gives the arithmetic in pence-shaped exactness instead: the promise is two hundred and twenty-three days old, she noticed on its seventh day, she wrote it down in the third week, and the lateness is the seven months between the writing and the finding and not the noticing at all — which is a worse reading than the card's, because a keeper who sees a thing on the seventh day and says nothing for seven months is not a keeper who was slow.** (9) **The prompt says Chapter 92's third blank is the creditor's index leaf and describes it with two phrases belonging to two other blanks** — *no sixth space* is the fifth space in the County of Ash and *six lines with nothing against them and no reason on them* is a page in a book at the assizes there. **The chapter uses only the third blank's own language: a county cell, ruled, on a line with a number and a date on it, with nothing in it, and no space on the leaf for a reason.** (10) **The prompt's Chapter 100 says the bell is rung by "three pairs of hands" and names two people at each end of the line, which is two pairs.** The chapter says the bell was rung by other hands and not one of them was his, and stages two people at the near abutment and two at the far end of the line, which is what the card's own cast of the beat gives. (11) **Two names in the block are on the page in a form the prompt did not have them in: Ansel Tarrant is twenty-one years in the counting house, and the prompt's "twenty years after his grandmother did" is his age's arithmetic, not his service's** — the service figure is new and is twenty-one years. (12) **The prompt said Chapter 91 was "the only chapter in this block that states a drift figure" and the batch honours it, but Chapter 91 states it three times, in speech twice and once in the notebook.** Same chapter, one drift figure, and the figure is the same figure all three times.

**The three hooks that leave the volume, in the order `outline/volume-02.md` §11 gives them.**

1. **The disposition schedule's county column, and eight counties with a stone in a field.** On the page in Chapter 99, as a column and not as a revelation, copied on a fourth leaf of a purchase in a city that does not admit to the Crown that it exists, and read in Merefen on the twenty-seventh of Greenmonth. **Eleven other counties, eight with a boundary oath renewed in public on a stone in a year anybody there can name by men who are still alive, three with nothing in the leaf at all.** Entered as a question and not as a finding, with the hour, against nobody, no opinion, and the eight are left where they are. **It can be seen by exactly two people in the world and neither can say what an oath has to do with a severance, and that is the next volume's question.**
2. **A form of the Office of Provincial Registration with a marginal instruction that manufactures a keeper and that nobody in this volume understood, and Sabin Dain, who wrote the letters and will write more of them.** Untouched in this batch: **no character in Chapters 91–100 mentions it, refers to it, explains it or notices it**, and Chapter 94 is written specifically so that it cannot be noticed. Dain is on the page in her own chapter, with a want that has nothing to do with Aren, as a witness in a county's minute and not in a register, saying that she is frightened of unnamed things. **She is a figure in a book that answers a question for as long as the book is dry, and she has said she would be glad of that, and in Volume 04 she joins an order that has learned to remove names rather than complain about them.**
3. **A tile on a coping at the Ninebridge, a hand's width, with a rope on it and no yoke, carrying the device of a hand laid flat on a book, which nobody in the County of Ash knows what it is, and whose maker is dead, and which has been on that coping since before the roll was begun.** **Untouched in this batch, not looked at, not touched, not explained, and not moved.** It is named once, in Chapter 99, in a room four hundred miles from it, as a thing a keeper has known about for ten months because a man off a road told her he did not know what it was, and as a thing the county's own books do not mention. **No character in this volume names the device and no character may, and it is the oldest object in the book after the bell.**

**What the Volume 02 close inherits.** Fifty chapters, **a book about a records office that never says so**, a woman at four pence a year with two unserved books and a breach she has chosen not to open, a man who can cross and a notebook in a coat with a morning in it, a brother who will not re-teach, a county of nine hundred and forty-one hearths that has begun to un-fire its own founding act, a bench in a fen that has been asked three times and says yes three times and is not a bench and knows it, an office that has learned to ask for a figure because a figure is cheaper than a name, a count of one thousand six hundred and nine, a body corporate with a door and a number, a man in a counting house who has broken four conditions on purpose and minuted the reason, a clerk of twenty-one who signs a register in his grandmother's frightened hand, a bill with a line in it and nothing against the line and now a figure against it, eight counties with a stone in a field, and a bell on a bridge four hundred yards up a road that is not anybody's.

**Review repair pass — what was applied, and what was deliberately not.** A review of the ten chapters against the outline, the bible, the item 147 chain, the items 145 and 148, and the twenty prior chapters returned **twenty-three MUST-FIX findings, eight defects and twenty nits. Twenty of the twenty-three must-fixes and seven of the eight defects are applied. No plot beat was removed, no chapter renumbered, no card broken, no date the batch is built on moved, and no controller file touched.** The batch went from 32,047 words to **32,842** and every money column, every date and every weekday in the ten chapters was re-added and re-checked. **The findings that mattered, in the order a later writer will trip over them.**

**(1) The forbidden sum was performed, in Chapter 98, in the middle of the batch's own guardrail paragraph.** The prose read *Four thousand and seven hundred and twenty by name are in the seven villages* — which is 4,000 + 720, the addition the volume forbids in any file, and it also gave the seven villages the wrong figure. **It now reads: the city is four thousand according to the Crown and has been for eleven years, the seven villages are seven hundred and twenty by name, in the county's spelling, with the sum not stated on the sheet, and the two figures are never added together anywhere in this case.** Corrected in the prose and in continuity item 250 and in thread 93.

**(2) "Eleven weeks" was a forbidden conversion and a contradiction of Chapter 100.** The scour under the nose of the third pier is a washtub from the sixth of Mudmonth, and the twenty-fourth of Greenmonth is **most of a year** later, not eleven weeks; the phrase had been lifted from a Volume 01 forecast about a pier and converted into elapsed time. Corrected twice in Chapter 97. **Chapter 70's "eight weeks" for the same scour, as at the fifth of Wolfmonth, is an inherited error in Batch 0007's prose and is recorded here rather than silently edited, and the close should decide whether it stands.**

**(3) Chapter 100 re-ran Chapter 90's climax beat in the same words, and the prompt for this batch forbade it twice over.** Two passages in Chapter 100 were lifted sentences from Chapter 90 — the fifty minutes in which she writes nothing, and *it is now the ninth time in four years that a record has arrived in this room about a person who cannot be found because he cannot be asked* — and Chapter 90 is the chapter the volume's climax was landed in. **The whole reading of the list in Chapter 100 is now a different scene**: this one is not a bill but **the county's answer to a term**, she checks it line by line with a rule because **it is the first document that has ever come into that office out of a place that has agreed to be checked, and the agreement is the reason it can be checked at all**, and the line she cannot put down is now identified by what it says rather than by what it is not. The restated reason is not restated, and the ninth-in-four-years count survives once, in the form Chapter 90 used, and is not attached to a second category.

**(4) The promise's arithmetic could not foot and the prompt's "a year late" is not true of this calendar.** The promise of the twenty-second of Fallowmonth, YR 312 is **two hundred and twenty-three days** old at the fifth of Greenmonth, YR 313, and Chapter 70 has the noticing on the twenty-ninth of Fallowmonth, **the seventh day of the promise**, written down on the fifth of Wolfmonth. **Chapter 92 now gives the exact figures and turns the lateness into the worse and truer reading: she noticed on the seventh day and has said nothing for seven months, and the lateness is the seven months and not the noticing.** Corrected in the prose in three places and in continuity item 238, in thread 82, in the Chapter 92 and 98 summaries, in the deviation list below, and in `state/current.md`.

**(5) Halven's disclosure and endorsement were re-dated and re-numbered against Chapter 55.** The chapter had the disclosure on the sixth of Fallowmonth and the endorsement **number 11 of the season** dated the fourth of Fallowmonth. **Canon is the fifth of Embermonth, YR 312, at the tenth hour, and number 4 of the season, and the writ is returnable the ninth of Rainmonth and the endorsement is on the marshal's emergency office's own printed instruction.** All four corrected in the prose and in continuity item 244. Halven's age of sixty-one is on the page, and the disclosure's maker is a registrar of the fourth press **two hundred miles** off, and Chapter 96's line about the unasked fair copy of the shelf register is corrected from "four hundred miles off" to two hundred, which is where the fourth press is.

**(6) Chapter 93's first count was given the wrong returnable date and Chapter 91's fifth reading the wrong interval.** The first count is **dated the twelfth of Greenmonth, YR 312, on her desk by the fifteenth of that month, returnable on or before the ninth of Frostmonth, and expired on the twenty-first of Wolfmonth, eighteen days before the day it was returnable** — which is the whole of the Chapter 73 construction and is now on the page, and **the month and the interval in this line were wrong in the first pass and are corrected by the second, which is at the foot of this entry.** And the third reading of the Sedge Reach ring, named for the twenty-fourth, is **twenty-two days** off the second of Greenmonth, not five.

**(7) Chapter 95's arithmetic against the canon was wrong twice.** The Archive has been a body corporate for **seven** days, not four, and the eight county cells hold **the same valley, with the eight dates inside about a week** — not "the same valley over eight years" with "the eight dates within about four months", which also contradicted itself in one clause.

**(8) Chapter 100 required three half tiles and the chapter now accounts for two.** The first went north in the hand of the Ninebridge and **he has no way of finding out whether it ever got here and is not going to ask**; the second is the one in his bag and the one in the press, and the chapter says so, names the saw mark, and says why there are two.

**(9) Nine other findings, all applied:** the deed of the Archive's founding is **thirteen** words and not eleven, in two chapters and in continuity item 241; Chapter 94's "the twelfth month of this year" becomes "in this month"; Chapter 94's Lysa Fenn no longer dates her reasoning to a sentence that is on no page, and her eleven miles and her three-day post foot; Chapter 98's two intervals for the eighth ruled line become **the eighth month after the first look and the seventh month after the second**, and the brine weights' two lines are **five days apart in one week of Fallowmonth**; Chapter 99 stops giving the buyer two different brothers and stops saying Mara was thirty when she is twenty-nine, and the canal wharf has one span of nine years; Chapter 97's bench no longer wants "more than four hundred marks" of work, which is two hundred times the costed figure on its own page; Chapter 97's gateway sentence is dated to the **ninth of Embermonth** where it belongs; Chapter 98's nine places over nine days is no longer conflated with one room; Chapter 92's press paragraph no longer puts the counting house's copy and the provincial press's copy in the same province; and Chapter 91's clock runs in order, with the arrival at the sixth hour, the county at the first, Tovan at the eleventh and the notebook at the fourth hour of the afternoon.

**The three findings not applied, and why.** (a) **Chapter 70's "eight weeks"** for the scour is not this batch's prose and editing it would mean editing a chapter the batch's own scope excludes; it is recorded above and in the deviation list. (b) **The prompt's "twenty years after his grandmother did"** for the register's signatory is his age's arithmetic and not his service's, and the service figure is new; the chapter gives twenty-one years in that house and the deviation is recorded. (c) **The prompt's "three pairs of hands"** ringing the bell in Chapter 100 is two pairs by the card's own cast of the beat, and the chapter says the bell was rung by other hands and not one of them was his.

**One thing the review confirmed and the close should know.** The guardrails held without exception: **no spelled *Gatehouse*, no *Culling Order*, no *custodian*, no *Bind*, no real-world or main-world month name in any of the ten files, no narrator-voice reference to the book outside a physical object, the private-keeper clause not mentioned or noticed by anybody, the drift figure in Chapter 91 only, the 456 absent in every form, the 2,906 only as the county's own figure, the two eighth ruled lines in different chapters in front of different people, one of the volume's five blanks in the batch with no character comparing it to another, and exactly two new names with four slots unfilled.** The ten carded beats are all on the page and none was replaced by a different beat.

**Second review repair pass — what was applied, and what was deliberately not.** **A second review of the ten chapters, run against the outline, the item 147 chain, the twenty prior chapters and every state file that describes this batch, returned five blockers, ten major findings and a short list of nits, and every one of the five blockers and all ten of the majors are applied. No plot beat was removed, no chapter renumbered, no card broken, no date the batch is built on moved, and no controller file touched.** The batch is **33,163 words** after this pass, against 32,842 after the first, and the per-chapter column at the head of this entry is the one that foots. **The finding that governs the whole pass is structural rather than local: five of the fifteen were a sentence that a repair pass had put into a state file as canon, and the state files were therefore asserting things the prose did not say, in a batch whose next phase is a close that is explicitly told to take its facts from the files.** The prose and the files now agree, and the close can take the files.

**The five blockers.** **(1) Chapter 96 spoke a real-world month twice** — *August of last year* for the disclosure, and *in August* for the sentence Halven said in the room. **Canon is the fifth of Embermonth, YR 312, at the tenth hour**, which is on the page in the disclosure's own third paragraph, and the sentence in the room was said **on the eighteenth**, the day he came up on his own money. Both are corrected in the prose; the guardrail paragraph's claim that no real-world month name appears in the ten files is true again. **(2) Chapter 92 said *it is the fifth of Wolfmonth*** in the chapter that opens on the fifth of Greenmonth. **The canon four files carry is right and the sentence was garbled**: she noticed the change on the twenty-ninth of Fallowmonth, which was the seventh day of the promise, and **she wrote the day down on the fifth of Wolfmonth, the morning he came up out of that county** — and the promise is two hundred and twenty-three days old at the fifth of Greenmonth. **A later writer may not correct the fifth of Wolfmonth into the fifth of Greenmonth: it is the day she wrote, not the day she is speaking.** **(3) Chapter 95's four leaves contradicted themselves four times**, in the chapter whose subject is documentary precision, and the map given in Mara's mouth was a purchase, an argument and a column of eleven counties — **of which the eleven counties is Chapter 99's disposition schedule and may not appear in Chapter 95.** The structure is now fixed: **leaf one a reading, leaf two an entry in the house's own book, leaf three the minute of the hall with the clerk's opinion entered on it, leaf four a schedule, which is the only one of the four that is a number, and which is a copy of the index's nine lines and so breaks the first condition on the wrapper in the plainest way in the book.** **(4) Chapter 95 named Edric Hunn once, in Aren's mouth, and five state files and the Volume 02 close prompt said he is not named anywhere in Chapters 91–100.** **The name is out of the chapter.** The guardrail is now true on the page, and the close prompt's correction on the point — *the name is never on the same sheet in the same county as the claim and it is not written anywhere in Volume 02's last ten chapters* — is correct and is binding on Volume 03. **(5) Chapter 91 hung the five-day error on the wrong crossing**, giving *the twenty-sixth of Longlight* as the antecedent, and that crossing is the Volume 01 one whose error is **minus fifteen days**. **The five days belong to Crossing A: out on the ninth of Embermonth at the fourth hour, back on the fifth of Wolfmonth, twenty-six days of absence.** The chapter now says the ninth of Embermonth and gives both subtractions in the open, and the notebook's departure is corrected from the first of Hearthmonth to **the fourth of Hearthmonth at the fourth hour**, which is the date that makes eighty-eight days foot to the second of Greenmonth.

**The ten majors.** (a) **Chapter 97 attributed the gateway sentence to Tovan Kest on the ninth of Embermonth at eleven o'clock at night. It is Chapter 77's sentence, on the fourth of Hearthmonth, at a gate, with his hand on a rope, in daylight, with the yard going on round them** — and the ninth of Embermonth at eleven o'clock at night in a gateway on Weir Street is **Reeve Aldis Fenn's**, in Chapter 77. **Tovan has carried his sentence a hundred and ten days and not eleven weeks**, and the yard's fortnight has held for a hundred and ten days. (b) **Chapter 93's first count** is dated the **twelfth of Greenmonth** and expired **eighteen days** before its returnable day, on the twenty-first of Wolfmonth, which is Chapter 73's arithmetic and agrees with the two later sentences in the same chapter. (c) **Chapter 94:** the magistrate comes in at the fifth hour, having walked the **eleven miles** out of Merefen — the four miles are the officer's own walk from the post — and the officer is at her desk in the provincial town on the **sixteenth** of that month and not the third, against a post of three days either way. (d) **Chapter 100 no longer lifts Chapter 90.** The entry into the day-book is rewritten, and **the count of nine in four years survives once in the batch, in Chapter 100, in the form Chapter 90 used**, and the second entry now carries the second time since Fallowmonth instead of a second count of the same kind — which is what this entry claimed the first pass had done. (e) **The bell's own description is back on the page**: a band of forty-one rivets and a dull red seam in the metal, in Chapter 100, where the card put it and where four state files already said it was, and §10.8's *landed as carded* is now true of the page and not only of the file. (f) **Chapter 92's internal faults:** the list is **four days** old in the room and not a week and a half; the bench dates no longer carry a clause about which of two days the ninth of that month falls between, which was not true of either; and the day on which she says she will not write the old reason again is distinguished from the new thing she says out loud two paragraphs later. (g) **Chapter 100 names the day the list comes down** — the twenty-ninth — instead of saying *on a named day* and naming none, **gives the list's own four hundred and six words against the last thing down that line at four hundred words and about four hundred and nine people**, and **dates the county's supersession to the twentieth of a month this county does not use**, so that the fair copy is six days old in Merefen on the twenty-sixth and nine on the twenty-ninth against a post of three days either way. (h) **Both costed columns were re-added and both foot at forty-eight pence to the mark and twelve to the shilling:** the Chapter 97 bill at 1,626 + 372 + 216 + 204 = 2,418 pence = fifty marks one shilling and sixpence, and Chapter 98's re-grounding at 13 × 5 × 5 = 325 and 9 × 30 = 270, total 595 pence = twelve marks one shilling and seven pence. (i) **Prose tics: the batch's *about n seconds/minutes* constructions are down from twenty-seven to ten and all ten are load-bearing**, and **the sentence *the not writing is the part that is a decision* is now said verbatim three times** — Chapter 92's first entry, Chapter 95's last line, and the volume's own last entry in Chapter 100 — **and is carried in other words in Chapter 92's second, Chapter 96, Chapter 98 and Chapter 99, so the phrase is varied and not spent.** (j) **A stray *x* at the head of a line in Chapter 93, and two pairs of stacked section breaks in Chapters 92, 94 and 98, are gone.**

**Recorded rather than changed, and one correction to the first pass's own record.** **The first pass claimed that in Chapter 94 *her eleven miles and her three-day post foot*. They did not: the prose said four miles twice, once for the walk in and once for the walk out, beside a line about eleven miles of road. The eleven miles are the road between the county town and Merefen, which is the same road Marda Oakes is given at the end of Chapter 93, and the four miles are the officer's own walk from the post, and both figures are now said once each and mean one thing.** **The yard's four days in Chapter 97 are the three entries of the fifth, seventh and eighth of Wolfmonth plus a fourth dated the day he came back, and they are not tidied into one precisely because they are not consecutive.** **Chapter 70's "eight weeks" for the same scour is still Batch 0007's inherited error and is still the close's to decide.** **The eleven miles a toll-taker carried a basket in Chapter 95 is left standing, because no file in the project settles the distance between the Old Toll and the weigh-house and the review did not raise it.**

**What the second pass confirmed, so that the close does not re-open it.** The money foots in both columns. The ten carded beats are all on the page. The ban list is clean and the guardrail paragraph above is now true of the prose and not only of the intention. The drift figure is in Chapter 91 only and is the same figure in all three of its statements, and the five-day and sixty-seven-day errors now belong to the crossings they belong to. The 456 is absent in every form, the 2,906 is the county's own figure, the two eighth ruled lines are in different chapters in front of different people, one of the volume's five blanks is in the batch and no character compares it to another, and exactly two new names were spent with four slots unfilled.

## Volume 02 close — volume close record and Volume 03 outline

**Landed.** Two documents, no prose. **`outline/volumes/volume-02-close.md`**, the close record, in the same species as `outline/volumes/volume-01-close.md`, and **`outline/volume-03.md`**, the Volume 03 outline for Chapters 101–150. State updated: `state/continuity.md` **items 255–269** under a new heading, `state/current.md` (phase, volume, next phase, day map, power state, last change, and the corrected promise figure), `state/open-threads.md` (a new "Threads the Volume 02 close carries into Volume 03" list, cross-referenced, closing nothing), `state/batch-summaries.md` (this entry), and `state/chapter-summaries.md` (**two figures corrected in place, no new entry, no new block note**). **No chapter was changed, no chapter was renumbered, no plot beat was moved, `outline/ending.md` was not touched, and no controller file was written** — not `scripts/`, not `.github/`, not `.opencode/`, not `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, or `state/phase-ledger.json`. **No Chapters 101–110 prompt and no second volume-close prompt was created anywhere.**

**What it audited, and against what.** The close reads Volume 02 as **five blocks against `outline/volume-02.md`'s cards rather than against its intentions**, from the **forty-eight numbered deviations and eight date corrections** recorded in the five entries above — six deviations plus the eight-item date paragraph for Batch 0006, and seven, eleven, twelve and twelve for Batches 0007, 0008, 0009 and 0010 — and it produces, for each block, what the card promised, what the page delivered, each deviation with a verdict, and whether the block improved or damaged the book. **Batch 0006 has no prompt directory and never had one, so its entry and the close's §3 are the only surviving record of the plan for Chapters 51–60.** The close also audits the outline's own **§10** point by point, records a **deviations-that-became-canon** list, gives a **state-of-the-world ledger at the end of Chapter 100** in the spirit of the state block at the foot of the Batch 0010 entry, works a **promise-by-promise audit** over §10, §11, §13 and the Volume 02 rows of the ending's setup-to-payoff table, states the **corrections Volume 03 must not undo**, restates the **three hooks** as documents with dates and owners, and ends on a short honest paragraph on what the volume did badly.

**What it changed.** Three findings, and two of them are corrections.

1. **The promise's age is wrong by thirty days.** Chapter 92 says the promise of the twenty-second of Fallowmonth, YR 312 is "two hundred and twenty-three days" old at the fifth of Greenmonth, YR 313. **It is one hundred and ninety-three**, and the weekday chain of item 147 agrees with 193 and not with 223. **The prose figure is an error and the shape is canon** — she noticed on the seventh day, wrote the day down on the fifth of Wolfmonth, and the lateness is in the months since the writing and not in the noticing — **because the shape is the series' Volume 2 relationship milestone and the number is not.** The repair is one number in one chapter; **this close writes no prose**, so it is recorded as an open item for the first Volume 03 phase. **No Volume 03 figure may be derived from the promise's age.** Continuity item 263.
2. **Rainmonth's length is fixed at thirty days**, from the thirty-day month the whole calendar has been built on and from the audited chain, because Volume 03 runs forward past it and would otherwise have to guess. **Every month in the Volume 03 stretch is thirty days and that is binding on every later volume. Mudmonth's length is still unfixed and is not needed.** Continuity item 258.
3. **Stage 3 is not reached in Volume 03 and must not be.** `outline/series.md` gives the Second Door to Volumes 2–3 and Threadkeeper to Volumes 4–6, and a limited forced-connection cut is Volume 06's beat. **The cost of not reaching it is the volume's climax:** the obvious solution to the Marches' failing water route is a cut, somebody says so to him in a room, he refuses for a date and a community that lost contact for ever after a cut on a forged witness, and **what he does instead is hold a failing span with his body and comes up with a permanent limp acquired before the return stitch closes.** The Second Door's own qualification — *deliberately close one route without losing it* — is met in Block 4 and paid for with the County of Ash road and the woman who keeps it. Continuity item 257.

**What it decided and did not change.** **Chapter 70's "eight weeks" for the Sedge Reach scour stands** and is recorded as a known defect, because the corrected figure is on the page twice in the volume's own last ten chapters and a repair would mean editing a chapter no phase owns (item 264). **Petch's dates are the chapter's** — out on the eighth, three days on the road, back and filing on the twelfth — and item 161's ninth is not reachable again by a town four days off by post (item 265). **The Chapter 10 column stands at eleven marks and fourpence** as a recorded exception. **The founding deed is thirteen words. The fifth of Wolfmonth is the day she wrote the day down. The two half tiles and the reason there are two. The 456 and the 254. The 1,609.** All settled by prose and not to be re-opened.

**The two hardest things the close says, because it was told to find them.** **First: Volume 02's ending does not resolve the relationship the book is about.** A man conceals three things from the only person in the world who can hold him to a document about himself; she finds the third with a date; she does not ask; the volume ends with the two of them four hundred yards apart in the same county and neither of them going to ask. **That is the ending the outline asked for and it is the series' Volume 2 relationship milestone, and it is unpaid on purpose, and the repair is forbidden in Volume 03 and is the price of Volume 04.** **Second: a volume which is a book about a records office may have been a book about forms**, and the finding is in the close's §10 — the Crown's method is documentary and that is correct and it is why the volume works, but from about Chapter 95 the best pages are pages in which documents are read and checked and the human event is a woman deciding not to write something. **Both are findings and both are in the document and neither is going to be improved on.**

**One finding about this project and not about the prose.** The meta-language defect Batch 0009's review pass removed **did not come back**: there is no narrator-voice reference to the book outside a physical object in Chapters 91–100, the three in the block are diegetic, *Culling Order* is twice in the whole manuscript and *custodian* not at all in Volume 02. **But two defects that had been dealt with earlier in the volume did come back in the last ten chapters, and both were introduced by state files rather than by prose** — a real-world month name spoken twice in Chapter 96, and **the forbidden sum of the Crown's four thousand and the served schedule's seven hundred and twenty performed in Chapter 98, in the middle of the batch's own guardrail paragraph** — and the second review pass then found five sentences that the first pass had put into a state file as canon and that the prose did not say. **The standing rule that comes out of it: a later phase states a figure, checks it, and records the check; a defect that is recorded is a fact about the book; a defect inherited silently becomes a canon with a footnote; and a state file is the least reliable document in this repository, so take the fact from it and then go and look at the page.** Continuity item 267.

**The question Chapter 101 opens on.** Not the Marches. **The eighth of Rainmonth, in a room over a weigh-house, a keeper of records reads a fair copy of a column of eleven counties for the fourth time, and the fourth time she does not stop at the eight, and this is the first time in the volume that she has done arithmetic on a thing she has already decided not to look at — and the column has a ninth line on it, and the ninth line is a *Keeper* column with a hundred and forty names in it, and one of the hundred and forty is a man in a Marches village who never filled it in, and one of them is a road warden of the Merefen bridge guild with a vote who wrote *and no other office* in a gateway in the rain and was right.**

**What the next phase inherits.** `outline/volume-03.md` for the volume, `outline/volumes/volume-02-close.md` §5, §7, §8, §9 and §11 for the canon, continuity items 255–269, the threads list at the foot of `state/open-threads.md`, and the next phase's own prompt — **the Volume 03 Batch 0011 prompt for Chapters 101–110, which that phase creates and this phase did not.**

## Batch 0011 — Volume 03, Chapters 101–110, "The Figure and the Stone"

**Landed.** Chapters 101–110 in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0101.md` to `chapter-0110.md`, four digits, continuing the manuscript's numbering. **31,648 words** by `wc -w` including title lines, ten complete scenes, all ten days main world. **Per chapter:** 3217 / 2946 / 3004 / 3397 / 3475 / 3237 / 2868 / 3038 / 3082 / 3384. **Day map as written:** the thirtieth of Greenmonth (Tue, not market) · the first of Longlight (Wed, not) · the fourth (Sat, market) · the ninth (Thu, market) · the thirteenth (Mon, not) · the sixteenth (Thu, market) · the nineteenth (Sun, not) · the twenty-third (Thu, market) · the twenty-seventh (Mon, not) · the thirtieth (Thu, market) — every figure checked against `outline/volume-03.md` §17 and every one correct. **Block 1 is main world on all ten days, as the card requires. No crossing in either direction anywhere in the ten, no path scene, no Gatehouse line except the one italic line in Chapter 110, and no stage claimed and no mechanism explained.**

**Cast.** **Two new names and four slots unfilled, exactly as the cast plan allows: Thessa Wend, 38, of Sallow Marsh, who has read two gauges on the Water twice a day for eleven years and has eight thousand and thirty-one lines in a book in a hut at a weir that nobody has ever asked to see, named in Chapter 104; and Oswin Rill, 44, a seed-carter of Nether Dray, whose name is in a *Keeper* column on his own village's paper that he did not fill in, named in Chapter 104.** Everyone else in the batch is established or addressed by trade: Ivet Salter of the third press (established, Chapter 79), Sabin Dain, Lysa Fenn, Reeve Aldis Fenn, Tovan Kest, Pei Tarrow, Bram Ottery, Captain Rell, the bench-clerk, the man at the drawknife, the boy of fifteen, the guild's man with the levels, the toll-taker, the man who keeps the minute of the Marches council, the woman who renews the stone, and the first hand of the Marches bridge guild. **Coll Ferrow, Delya Munn, Sian Pugh, Ordmar Gace, Aled Rhys and Marda Oakes are all correctly withheld and are Batch 0012's to name.** Volume 03's running total is **two of seven** with **eighteen of twenty-three slots unfilled.**

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so the next writer does not inherit them as errors.**
1. **The guild's first hand and the woman who renews the stone are not named in this batch, although the Chapter 104 card says "Coll Ferrow states his own guild's terms first."** The cast plan in §14 allocates Ferrow to Batch 0012 and holds the volume to seven names, and the manuscript already has the precedent of a path face kept to a trade for a whole block. He is **the first hand of the Marches bridge guild** and **the man from the Hearth Marches** throughout Chapters 104 and 108 and is named in Batch 0012. The beat is delivered entire; only the name is withheld.
2. **The Chapter 103 card puts a woman of thirty-eight and a man of sixty in the delegation "on the road behind it."** The delegation is on the road on the fourth, and Thessa Wend is on the weigh-house bench in Merefen from first light on the fourth, and the man of sixty is named in a carter's report and arrives with the wagon. The card's beat — that the delegation exists behind the arithmetic — is delivered; the two faces are separated by four hundred miles and nine days, which is the point.
3. **The Chapter 107 card asks for "two Fenn sisters in one building, four days apart."** Honoured literally: the paper is read out in the guild hall over the wharf on the **fifteenth**, a Wednesday, and Lysa Fenn answers it in the moot hall on the **nineteenth**, a Sunday, and Reeve Aldis Fenn is in the same building twice, once having heard it read on the fifteenth and once coming upstairs on the nineteenth. The Chapter 104 delegation reading on the ninth is a different reading in a different building and is not counted.
4. **The six terms are printed in full in Chapter 104, not first in Chapter 114.** The card for 104 says the terms for a stranger are six lines and one of them is the stone; the card for 114 puts the same six on a board and puts them to the reeve for a vote. **Chapter 104 is therefore the chapter that fixes the six lines' wording, and Chapter 114 must use these six lines and must not re-word them.**
5. **The delegation's first hand stays in Merefen past the delegation's own departure and is the man in the yard in Chapter 108.** The delegation leaves on the **eighteenth**; he stays two extra weeks because, as he says, a rate cannot be agreed by a man who is in the county for four days. He is in the gateway on the twenty-third and gives up the payable fifth — six hundred and forty-eight pence — for a reason about a promise, and nobody writes that down.
6. **The Marches' delegation is eleven of the council, of whom nine are householders, plus two of the guild, plus the seed-carter, who is not on the council.** Established in Chapter 104 and referred to in Chapter 110 as eleven and two.
7. **The Marches' pay is costed in salt and seed and not in coin, and the conversion is lost by the yard in a gateway.** The figures are on the page and foot: **ninety working days; four men at eight pence and a boy at four pence is thirty-six pence a day; two thousand eight hundred and eighty and three hundred and sixty is three thousand two hundred and forty pence.** The Marches' own offer: **two hundred and eighty-eight bushels of salt at nine pence is two thousand five hundred and ninety-two, and two hundred and sixteen bushels of seed at three pence is six hundred and forty-eight, and the two together are exactly the sum.** The one fifth, six hundred and forty-eight pence, is payable on the return. **The yard takes the salt and is outargued on the seed and takes it anyway.**
8. **The leaf of a return is the same leaf in four chapters and has three fixed days.** Ivet Salter's letter is dated the **twenty-ninth of Greenmonth** and arrives on the **first of Longlight**; Mara Vey's request goes back on the **second**; the fair copy of the whole leaf is sent on the **first** and arrives in Merefen on the **fourth**, which is **nine days** before Chapter 105 and **twenty-three days** before Chapter 109. **A later writer may not move any of those four dates.**
9. **The Chapter 105 offer carries a second Crown instrument that was not in the card:** the instruction of the ninth of Longlight of the year last past, in force, not superseded, and barring the assembly of any body of this county in respect of the object named in it, **and the object is not named on the sheet and nobody in Merefen can find out what it is.** This is a standing Crown instrument the state files carry as existing and it is now on the page, and it is a live question the batch opens and does not answer.
10. **The volume's first System-facing line is in Chapter 110's notebook and is a quotation, not a voice:** *A road held, three weeks. — printed on the reverse of a form in an office's hand, in a smaller type.* The road formula itself — *a road is a line and two hands and a date and a witness at each end* — is **Aren's trade knowledge and is unsourced**, which is where it has always been in the manuscript, and Chapter 110 says so.

**What the batch changed.** Merefen is now a county that has been visited. A delegation of eleven householders, two bridgewrights and a seed-carter came four hundred miles up this hill, sat in the guild hall over the wharf on a market morning, **refused outside command before anybody had asked them a question**, put six terms on a board, said no to a stranger's money and yes to a stranger's labour, and went home on a calendar of their own on the eighteenth. **A form is what brought them and a form is what a road warden does not yet know he is standing in:** the office's own worked example, printed at the foot of every R.C. form since the first of Embermonth, is a real man's name in a real guild, and a clerk in a border county copied it into the third column of a return about a river, and the office relied on the fourth, which is a seed-carter who never filled it in. **The offer arrived and is not answered:** a road warden's rank in the eastern counties, a clerk, eleven marks a year, the right to send men, and a sealed record of himself that he will never be allowed to see, in exchange for the eastern paths; and a third sheet in a woman's own hand, dated separately, saying *I do not believe it is going to work* and *I have not said so to my own office.* **Three rules are in the day-book over the weigh-house, in a fair copy anybody may come up two flights and read, and the third of them ends *and a habit is a thing a county does to himself when nobody is looking*,** and neither of the two people who wrote it says who it is about, and the page under the rules says in the keeper's own hand that the rules are a procedure built around a thing that is not being said and are not a cure. **Toban's yard has signed a contract for four hundred miles of river work and is four days from sending four men and a bag of salt, and the boy is not going, and a fifth line with no figure on it is in the week-book, and neither brother has been told anything by the other.** A magistrate has spent a Sunday discovering that the sentence she has argued for nine years and the resolution she voted for in September cannot both be true. A watch captain has been asked a question for the first time in eleven years and has been answered, and that is the last of the three. And a woman in a third press two hundred miles off has sent a whole leaf of a return to a keeper of records, entire, including the two ruled cells at the foot with nothing in them, because her grandmother was a copyist.

**The cost, entered.**
- **The concealment is a habit and is now on the page in a document.** The three things he is not saying are a letter, a mark on a coping, and the order of an ordinary morning, and Chapter 110 has him write a list of six people he has told none of them about, with the reason for each, and the reason for all six is a fair copy of four lines in a minute of a bench four hundred miles the other way: *we did not lose our register, we lost our refusal, a register is what you have instead.*
- **The counting post is gone for ever and is not paid; the twelve marks and ten pence is in the county's hands with no post for it to be in.** Not touched in this batch and still owed.
- **A box with no name has been got into a minute.** The county's surveyor said, in a room, on a market day, that the six pence was out of his own house, and the minute carries it, and a bench that cannot pay for a crossing has now minuted a defect in it.
- **The crossing is not made. The cut is not named, refused, dated or priced anywhere in these ten chapters, and the Marches' guild's fourth term is a trade rule about ditches and not about a road.** Stage 3 is not reached and no third route is held.

**The review repair pass, in one place.** A review of the ten chapters returned **nine blockers, nineteen major findings and eighteen nits, and every blocker and every major is applied; no chapter was renumbered, no card was broken, no date the batch is built on was moved, and no controller file was touched.** The batch is **31,648 words** after the pass against **31,680** before it. The nine blockers: two narrator-voice references to the book outside a physical object in Chapter 104 (a chapter number and a phrase reading *not in this chapter*); **the Ash figure nine hundred and forty-one spoken in a room over a weigh-house in Chapter 103 and deleted**; ***None* written in a box headed *State the office, if any* in Chapter 105, which Chapter 51 forbids, and the box is now empty on the page**; **the return's columns described three different ways across Chapters 102 and 105 and all three are now the same and agree with Chapter 51**; **Chapter 108's total said aloud before the column was footed, and a gloss calling thirty-six pence a day *a mark and a half* when a mark is forty-eight pence — the total is now footed first and the gloss is three shillings**; **the concealment between the brothers reversed between Chapters 108 and 110, and Chapter 110 now holds Chapter 108's state**; **the leaf arriving on three different days across three chapters, and it now arrives on the fourth and every reference to its age has been re-derived**; and **the delegation leaving on the wrong day, which is the eighteenth, not the sixteenth.** Among the majors applied: the Marches' oath made four hundred years old instead of a hundred and forty; the guild eighteen men in one line and nineteen in two; the Old Toll dressed block eleven weeks old when it is sixteen; **Wend's column four thousand and thirty-two lines when twice a day for eleven years is eight thousand and thirty-one**; **a bench that has four marks and six a year, not eleven marks**; the breach's two anchor dates, which are **the fifth of Greenmonth** for both the noticing and the torn sheet and are now right in Chapter 106; the delegation's distance from the ninth to the twenty-seven corrected to eighteen days in two places; the Archive four hundred **yards** from the watch house and not four hundred miles; **Chapter 110's bell moved off Monday, and the boy is now shown ringing it on the wrong day on purpose and nothing is struck at the far end, which is the only repair available that keeps the far end's Monday**; **Chapter 110's notebook no longer states why the County of Ash's road does not answer, because that is the fourth line of the notice in Block 4 and is not his to know in Block 1**; and **the drift warning restored to its own line in the notebook and the road formula left unsourced.** Judged and recorded rather than changed: **forty-four instances of *four hundred miles***, cut from fifty where a noun would do, and left where the distance is the volume's load-bearing fact and where a Marches voice is speaking; and **fifty-three bolded narrator section headers in ten chapters**, which is fewer per chapter than Volume 02's last block and is the manuscript's section device, so the count is not treated as a defect.

**The question Batch 0012 opens on.** **Six questions and two answers, and a crossing out on the twenty-second of Rainmonth, a Friday, which is not a market day.** A fair copy of a column of eleven counties goes out on a scheduled day in a fair hand and is not a message. A second proposed count — this time of oath-bound persons in a border county, returnable on a named day, with a space for a figure and no space for a name — is answered on the morning he goes out. The six lines go on a board in the county's own hand and are put to Reeve Aldis Fenn, who refuses the witness line in the same words she used on the ninth of Embermonth of the year before, and the clerk enters *the reeve voted.* The Marches' minute-writer and the guild's first hand are both still nameable. And a crew of four men and a boy who is not going goes in on the Marches' own road, on a day the Marches named, seven days before the road warden does and by a different road, and nobody in Merefen is told anything by anybody.

**What the next phase inherits.** `outline/volume-03.md` §8 Block 2 and §16 and §17 first, then the Batch 0011 entry above and continuity items 270 onward. **No name in a Crown's hand in this county since the twenty-seventh of Fallowmonth, and there is still not one.** No Ash figure is on the page in this batch and the 456 is not on the page in any form. No main-world month name is spoken anywhere in Chapters 101–110 and no Marches character speaks a main-world weekday or converts a rolling. **The tile on the coping is not touched, not looked at and not explained, and its device is named by nobody.** The eighth ruled line in the served schedule is not looked at in this volume. The brine weights are neither mentioned nor filed. The five blanks of Volume 02 are not joined. *Culling Order*, *custodian* and *Gatehouse* do not appear. The breach is open, the not asking has been entered for the ninth time, and **no apology and no mistake has been made available to anybody in these ten chapters.**

## Batch 0012 — Volume 03, Chapters 111–120, "The River Changes Course"

**Landed.** Chapters 111–120 in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0111.md` to `chapter-0120.md`, four digits, continuing the manuscript's numbering. **29,130 words** by `wc -w` including title lines, ten complete scenes, after the review repair pass recorded below. **Per chapter:** 3811 / 3034 / 2886 / 2750 / 2718 / 2616 / 2529 / 2251 / 2999 / 3536. **Day map as written:** the fourth of Rainmonth (Mon, not market) · the fifth (Tue, not) · the ninth (Sat, market) · the twelfth (Tue, not) · the thirteenth (Wed, not) · the fourteenth (Thu, market) · the fifteenth (Fri, not) · and then path time, day one and days two to five and days six to nine, with the crossing out on the **twenty-second (Fri, not a market day)**. **Every figure checked against `outline/volume-03.md` §17 and every one correct; the prompt's own list for the twelfth, thirteenth and fourteenth (Sun, Mon, Tue) was wrong by one day and the prose follows §17.**

**Cast.** **Two new names and four slots unfilled, exactly as the cast plan allows: Coll Ferrow**, first named in Chapter 111 on the foot of a sheet from the Hearth Marches, sixty, the first hand of that county's bridge guild; and **Delya Munn**, first named in Chapter 120, fifty-five, of that council, who renews the oath-stone and is the person who will not have it lifted. **Volume 03's running total is four of seven with sixteen of twenty-three slots unfilled.** Sian Pugh, Ordmar Gace and Aled Rhys are Batch 0013's and are not named here. Everyone else is established or addressed by trade: Thessa Wend and Oswin Rill (both Batch 0011), Bram Ottery, Lysa Fenn, Reeve Aldis Fenn, the bench-clerk, the toll-holder and his clerk, the man who keeps eels, the man at the drawknife, Pei Tarrow, the boy of fifteen, the boy of sixteen on the Old Toll abutment, the carpenter, the peer of the wharf road, the schoolmaster's son, the man at a sluice, the man who keeps the minute of the Marches council, the woman with a slate, the boy of fourteen who reads a gauge, the woman of about sixty on three hearths, and the tile-cutter of forty-one.

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so the next writer does not inherit them as errors.**
1. **The Chapter 113 card's date is honoured at the front and the answer is on the twenty-second.** The card dates the chapter the ninth of Rainmonth and says the count "is answered on the morning he goes out." The chapter opens on the ninth, a market day, with the instrument arriving and the office's own figure arriving with it, carries thirteen days, and answers it on the morning of the twenty-second — the morning of the crossing out. **A chapter in this project may span days and Ch 107 and Ch 108 both did; the entry is entered against the office on both days and no market day is invented.**
2. **The Chapter 117 card says "Toban's four men and the boy go in." The boy does not go.** Chapter 108, Chapter 110 and Chapter 116 all have the boy taken off the contract, and the card is out of date. The chapter sends **four men and no boy** and says why. The boy stays and copies the empty spaces.
3. **The nine items are the same nine in Chapters 112, 115 and 118** and are printed in full twice and in part once, in the same order: *a line, a hand at this end, a hand at that end, a date, the hour, a witness at each end who is not the man who keeps that road, the book at this end, the book at that end.* **Seven of the nine can be made at this end and the two that cannot are the date and the book at the far end** — the first review found the chapters saying "eight" three times over a page that shows two blanks, and the number is now seven everywhere.
4. **The line's book is ruled in a room over a cooper, on the back of a cost sheet, on the eleventh of Longlight** — not in the room with a door on a lane, which is the Bellweather Claims Archive and is Mara Vey's. The three days of paid work in Chapter 115 are the hemp, the four marks and the two ends in the abutment, **not** the ruling. The hemp cost a pot of beer and the beer is in the reeve's minute, which is the first beer in that roll.
5. **The sheet from the Marches went out of Merefen in a bag on the second of Rainmonth** (Chapter 110 and item 281) and **what is left in the yard is four lines with figures, an hour and an empty space** — so Chapter 116 has the terms on the bench-board, not the paper.
6. **The two new names are the only ones.** Every other face in the ten chapters is addressed by trade or by description, including the Marches' minute-writer, their clerk with the slate, the Marches' chosen witness, the first hand's brother-in-law, and the man at the drawknife, who is deliberately unnamed and who now holds one end of the line.
7. **No System panel and no Gatehouse line in the batch at all** — zero, where the prompt allowed one. The one unattributed line in Aren's notebook in Chapter 118 (*a road with one end is a length of string*) is trade idiom and carries no institution. The drift warning is quoted a second time in Chapter 120 in an office's hand and is not a voice.
8. **The drift figure is written in Chapter 120 as a projection and not as a measurement,** because the return is the second of Harvestmonth and is in Chapter 148's country. The entry gives the path interval (nine days), the printed warning (three weeks) and **the error as the absence against the warning, ten against twenty-one, which is minus eleven**, and it says on the page that the ten is not yet known and that the entry is being written on faith. **The first pass got this wrong twice — it subtracted the path interval from the warning (nine against twenty-one is minus twelve) and it is the volume's one checkable figure — and the correction is the one the outline and the Volume 02 close both require: the error is the absence minus the printed warning, and the path interval is nowhere in the sum.**
