**The Water came back over the re-cut boundary in the last night of the year, which is the whole of the argument, and the plan held, and one thing did not.** A footbridge of two bays and five yards that nobody built to be in a river took the whole night river for nine hours because it was a lid, and thirty men held a bank in the dark, and a mile of new ditch took the water in a minute and a half, and the salt under four villages did not move one inch and the four villages were not under water at any hour of the night. **A water-barge hand of twenty-seven said the true thing about his own trade in nine sentences and did not vote once, and the true thing is that a boat throws something off and a bridge cannot, so a bridgewright decides in the morning what to throw off in the night, and tonight the people were off it and the water was not, and a man on it is not holding the water off, he is keeping the deck in one piece so that the water has one way to go instead of four, and if he says it wrong everybody on that bank dies of being wrong in the correct direction.** A woman of thirty-eight watched a load point move at the fourth hour and wrote one line in a column nobody has ever asked to see, and bookmarked it, and showed it to nobody. **Nobody died. The number of men on that bank is thirty and the reason it is thirty is a ditch, a bank, nine days of work and a man on a lid.** And at about the fifth hour the near sill went and the near bay caught on the far one and the load went through the right hip and the right leg, and he did not let go of the rail, and the span held for three more hours with one bay hanging off it, and he came off it and got himself up four feet of bank with the help of a man at a sluice who had a bar and was not told to use it, and the drawknife man was nine days gone and there was nobody in that county whose job it was.

**He gave the road away and came up a line in a gateway in Weir Street in the rain at the fourth hour of a market morning, and the yard was shut and there was nobody in the street.** Three days of fever, the second, the third and the fourth, breaking on the fifth, held in another man's hands and carried out by a boy of fifteen. **He gave her the numbers before she asked, in the order they happened: the fifth of a month, the fourth hour, a week and a rolling, the second of this month, a hundred and seventeen days written on faith on the far side and confirmed on the near, a printed figure of three weeks that has not changed and belongs to nobody, ninety-six days of error, about four fifths of the absence and not of the warning, three days of fever, two routes held, and a leg.** And he told her the one thing that is not a figure, which is that he cannot put an ordinary morning in a yard on Weir Street in the order it happened, and he did not tell her that he cannot, and **the third of the three rules ends *a habit is a thing a county does to itself when nobody is looking*, and she wrote that sentence four months ago, and it is about him, and he knows it is about him, and he has not said so, and that is the fourth thing in a coat and it is a habit now and not a choice.** She did not ask. **The ninth not-asking stands, entered on the sixteenth of Longlight, and there is no tenth and there is not going to be one in this year, and in Chapter 150 she puts her pen down and does not write it, and neither of them says why.** No apology, in any form, in any register, and no mistake has been made available to anybody in ten chapters.

**A fair copy of four hundred and eight words and a working came down the line into a room over a weigh-house and it is the first thing that has ever come into that office out of a place that had agreed to be checked and had not been asked to.** It is addressed to a shelf and the shelf is the room and Alish Renn said so before it was opened. The figure at the head is wrong and is struck with the reason in the margin. The county's own figures are three and none of them is a hearth. **A body corporate that can be sued entered a company of seed and bridge, named in the Marches' own hand as the nine jars and the ditch, with a date and a name and a witness at each end, and the two witnesses have never met and cannot be produced to each other and both of those facts are in the entry, and the claim number against it is the number of the account, which is a thing a clerk may do twice and a keeper may not do once.** Its first act is a letter of four lines to any county that has kept a seed of a thing it does not grow, asking for a jar, with the carriage and the jar paid for and the keeper's name promised to a book that cannot be served and not to a book that can. **It went into a bag on the twenty-first and it is not answered inside the year and it is not going to be answered, and the reason is the third line of a notice that came down the same road in the autumn.** Lysa Fenn and Marda Oakes were in that room twice and conceded nothing, and Marda Oakes stopped in the middle of a sentence on purpose and said why, and the second copyist entered the half sentence. **The Archive resolved to hear the delegation on the Marches' own terms and entered the hearing for the ninth of Rainmonth of the year following, at the second hour, in that room, and the resolution says the delegation is not to be sent for and that if it does not come the hearing is not adjourned and not refused and it stands.**

**And the volume's last day is ordinary.** About forty men and stone in four carts and pointing that is never finished on the day it is begun, and a man on a deck of the third bay counting load points into a book that does not exist, and a stick that is for four hundred yards of road and for nothing else. A bench of nine has been asked four times and has said yes four times and is not a bench, and the reeve came up two flights with the fifth asking in her coat and did not put it. A bell with forty-one rivets in it went at the fourth hour and a half, rung by a boy of seventeen who is still not good at it and a carpenter who is a hand and not a keeper, and the arrangement is not his and nothing in the sound of it is his. **A Monday is the day the far end keeps, and it was a Friday, and nothing was struck at the other end of that line, and a book is a place where things get joined whether they were joined or not, so the two facts are in a day-book separately and are not in one sentence.** He stood at the end of the line. And the last line of the block is about the road, and it is not a sentence about a man and it is not a sentence about a county, and the block does not end on the span.

**The cost, entered.** A word taken back in a room and not in a gateway, four rollings late. Nine hundred and forty-one hearthstones in a fair copy four hundred miles from the people who counted them, and a woman four hundred miles off who had a file and no address on it. A list keyed to a roll, entered knowingly, in the minute-man's hand, with the knowing at the head. Two hands that did not have to be asked to put a thumb on a board and a room that had to be made for a woman to come up a stair into. A boundary broken in public by the men who swore it, and a grandmother's name on a stone that is still on it in nine years. Two names with one surname on one board in a county with no office. Nine hundred and ninety-six pence of re-cutting and a hundred and forty of it in elm, on a council's account that has no money. A far-end witness that is a fact in a cart of salt and may never arrive. **A permanent limp, acquired at the fifth hour of the last night of the year on the far side, and four feet of bank climbed with a stranger's shoulder, and a stick cut out of a fence rail in a county that is not on any map, and the guild's man with the levels refusing to measure it in nine seconds so that it will never be a figure.** A yard's book with a fifth line carrying an hour and no figure and no name, written by a man who has been asleep and does not know what he was holding. A reply in a bag that neither accepts nor refuses and is not withdrawn. **One hundred and seventeen days of absence, the widest the printed warning has ever been out, and the printed figure is three weeks and has not changed and the office cannot say whose it is, and no character in the block says the word about a warning, and the finding is that the error is in him and it scales with the work and he wrote that down two volumes ago and has now proved it twice.** Three days of fever. **The breach open, the ninth not-asking entered, and no apology and none to come.** And a letter to a county of nine hundred and forty-one hearths that is not answered, because there is nobody there holding the other end of anything.

**The question the volume close inherits.** The Hearth Marches is a place a record gathers in now, and the reason is on the page in a keeper's hand and is that an oath is a name. The Marches has a stone, a trough, a mile of boundary that does not walk, nine jars, a seed vault with three marks on the outside of it that a woman of sixty-six has decided nothing about and has said she will decide in the spring, and a company that has written one letter. **It has a number, and the number is wrong, and a county of clay has the same number by accident, and nobody is to do anything with that.** It has a fair copy in a room over a weigh-house that anybody could check line by line and a hearing entered for a date the delegation cannot reach. It has a guild that has been handed a mile of water and a yard that has been handed a contract, and two books that can be got at by a person in that county and neither of which can be made to answer to anybody in any other county. **And it has four rollings a year and a salt table a mile wide that will come down over four years, and the first crop off two of four villages is a failure, and a footbridge with a bay hanging four inches off it, and a man who is not coming back.** The close's own question is §11's and it is not this block's to answer: **the frozen valley that has kept a nine-generation promise, and why some path inhabitants recognise a man as a carrier rather than as a visitor.**

**What the next phase inherits.** `outline/volume-03.md` §7, §9, §10, §11, §12 and §14 first, and the Volume 02 close's own species at `outline/volumes/volume-02-close.md`, and **this entry and continuity items 345 onward and the Relationship state and the state-of-the-world ledger at the end of Chapter 150.** No name in a Crown's hand in this county since the twenty-seventh of Fallowmonth, and there is still not one. No Ash figure in the Marches. No 456 in any form. **No main-world month name, no real-world month name and no main-world weekday in Chapters 141–147, and no rolling converted by any character; the tenth to the thirteenth rolling is the whole of what was left and the year ended in it.** The tile on the coping is not touched, not looked at, **is named twice in this block as an object — Chapter 148 line 25 and the last line of Chapter 150, where it has a rope on it and no yoke — and is never explained, and its device is named by nobody, and nothing in this block looks at it.** The eighth ruled line in the served schedule is not looked at. The brine weights are neither mentioned nor filed. The five blanks of Volume 02 are not joined. *Culling Order*, *custodian*, *Bind*, *Gatehouse* and `scheduled day` do not appear. The marginal instruction is not explained and is not in anybody's mouth. **The breach is open, the not-askings stand at nine, and no apology and no mistake has been made available to anybody in Chapters 141–150.** The four gateway answers are uncontradicted, unimproved, and in no book. **Dain's offer is refused, in writing, in a document, and the refusal is not an apology and is not a scene.** Sian Pugh's name is spent and the question of whether it is spent is closed for the fourth time and is not re-opened. **Stage 3 is not reached, attempted, offered, priced or described as available, and the cut is not named.**

---

**Review repair pass, Batch 0015. Recorded so the next writer does not read the fixes as first-pass work.** A review of Chapters 141–150 and of the state files this batch wrote returned **nine findings — no craft findings, and that is the review's own word: the money discipline foots exactly, the mechanism is discharged on the page, and what it found was bookkeeping and precision. Two findings are prose and are fixed in the page. Six are figures and claims in state files and in the volume close's prompt, and all six are corrected. One is judged and not changed. Two further errors the review did not name were found on the page while checking the ones it did, and are disclosed here rather than folded in silently.** Continuity items **373–380** carry all of it, and the batch's plot, day map, money, cast, calendar and four devices were not touched, no chapter was restarted, no scene or dialogue was rewritten, and no outline was edited.

**The two prose repairs, both in this volume's own last block and neither touching a beat.** **Chapter 138 held a paragraph twice, word for word — the cost of the eighth rolling — and the second copy is gone; it stands once, at line 55, and the chapter is seventy-five lines.** **Chapter 138 also held the minute-man's speech about keeping a thing in the same hand and the same book and the same run of the page twice in a row, varying only in its last clause, which the batch's own review did not name; the reading that stands is the one that says *two flights of that stair*, because the paragraph after it and Chapter 145's reading-out of the eighth rolling both say two flights.** **And Chapter 145 enumerated seven columns of the county's form — what is given, to whom in the county's words, by whose hand, the day, the hour, a witness at this end, a witness at that end — and then called them six at all three places, in the volume's climax chapter and in the sentence the whole block is built on; the count is now seven at all three and the argument of the passage is untouched, which is that the enumeration is complete and therefore the two absent columns are absent on purpose. The day and the hour are two columns and may not be merged into one to make a smaller number.** What was kept in every case was the better reading and not the later one.

**The six bookkeeping corrections, and the two the review did not name.** **The block is 34,113 words and not 34,117, and Chapter 147 is 3,380 and not 3,377, and the item that held both numbers held three mutually inconsistent figures at once; the wrong total had been copied into four files, which is the failure the Volume 02 close's §10 item 3 exists to prevent.** **Removing Chapter 138's two duplicated passages took 242 words out of Batch 0014, so Batch 0014 is 35,318 and not 35,560, Volume 03 is 146,524 and not 146,766, and the manuscript is 601,605 and not 601,847; the five columns foot at 31,648 + 29,130 + 16,315 + 35,318 + 34,113 = 146,524, and the three volumes foot at 270,562 + 184,519 + 146,524 = 601,605, and Volume 01 and Volume 02 are untouched.** **The volume close's prompt printed an addition that did not add up — 31,648 + 29,130 + 16,315 + 35,560 + 34,117 = 146,766, which is 146,770 — in the one document whose own rule is to go and look at the page for every figure it prints.** **That prompt also said the five Volume 03 entries hold fifty-one recorded deviations and gave components that come to sixty-two; the entries hold fifty-eight, as ten for Chapters 101–110, eleven for 111–120, eight for 121–130, twelve for 131–140 and seventeen for 141–150.** **It said the word *melody* does not appear in Volumes 02 or 03, and it appears once in the whole manuscript outside Volume 01, at `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0070.md` line 125, inside a return form, as a negative entry in a list of costs — *No melody cost* — so no new cost is added anywhere and a Volume 04 writer told the word was absent would have read a canon violation that is not one.** **It said the tile on the coping is not named, and Chapter 150's last line names it and Chapter 148 names it, and the prohibition a Volume 04 outline would be built on is therefore *not explained and the device not named*, which is what the state files now say in four places.** **`the whole of` is 2 in this block and not 1, at Chapter 146 line 83 and Chapter 147 line 35, and both survivors are the flood and the drowning.** **And two more, found here and not by the review: `held` is 18 in the block and not 19, and `state/chapter-summaries.md`'s Chapter 140 entry still said the Marches is the sixth of the six, which the Batch 0014 review corrected in the page to the sixth of the eight and did not correct in the summary — the phrase *the sixth of the six* belongs to Chapter 118 and to the county's six terms and to nothing else.**

**One finding judged and not changed, and one contradiction deliberately left open.** **`chapters/volume-02/chapter-0052.md` lines 21 and 117 are the same paragraph word for word, and they are not a defect: line 21 is the third line of a four-line letter and line 117 is the keeper of records sitting in a quiet room thinking about the third line of that letter, which she says she has read eleven times, and a woman quoting a line back to herself in full is the beat. It was inspected and left alone, and a later duplicate scan over the whole manuscript will hit it and should not delete it.** **And Chapter 138's dating of the guild's plan to the twelfth rolling, against Chapter 139's ninth, is untouched and is the volume close's to decide.** It would have to be fixed in four places in two chapters and would have to choose a rolling before the Water, which is a plan-level decision and not a repair pass's; the close must decide it and record which way it went. **The Volume 03 close has decided it: the plan is of the twelfth rolling of the year before, and it is the page at all five places, and the close's own reason is that `chapter-0131.md` already implied it in a character's mouth. See continuity item 381 and `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` §5. This pass's forecast of four places was one short: it is five, because `chapter-0145.md`'s first column had the same error and nobody had found it.** **Nothing else in this block is open, and the six things the review called verified correct — the volume totals before this pass's own change to them, the continuity count, the bench asked four times, the fair copy at 408 words with the working on a second sheet, 1,752 and 1,272 on the page in Chapter 141, and Chapter 150 ending on neither the span nor a man — are verified correct and are not re-opened.**

## Volume 03 close — volume close record and Volume 04 outline

**This is the close record for the phase and it is not a batch. No chapter prose was written. Fifty chapters were read as a finished book and audited against the cards, the fifty-eight recorded deviations, and the page. The close document is `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` and the Volume 04 outline is `outline/volume-04.md`, and this entry is the short record of what the phase changed, what it corrected, and the question Chapter 151 opens on.** Cross-referenced to continuity items **381–392** and to **Threads the Volume 03 close carries into Volume 04** at the foot of `state/open-threads.md`. **The next phase is the Volume 04 Batch 0001 prompt, Chapters 151–160, and that phase creates its own prompt. This close created no Chapters 151–160 prompt and no second volume-close prompt, and it wrote no controller file: not `scripts/`, not `.github/`, not `.opencode/`, not `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, or `state/phase-ledger.json`.**

**What the volume turned out to be.** **A promise made in a field, and the fact that in a farm county a promise and a boundary are the same object, and a man who could see that and had no standing to say so until the last day of the year, when he was asked for nothing and gave something away.** Its most consequential page is a list of twenty-nine thumbs and its most consequential figure is a name. **Its answer to its own question — *what does a promise owe the people who keep it, when keeping it is the harm?* — is a door and not a cure, and the door is a promise that can be broken on a day named in advance, and a door is a thing you can be killed in, and the door is made of the same material as the thing that was killing the county, because a release is a document and a document names a body and a body can be served.** And its finding, which is the volume's real discovery and which is worse for the protagonist than the problem: **an oath renewed in public by men who are still alive is the only durable consent a rural county can produce and also the only document in the countryside that names a body, and that is why the records gather where the oaths are. It is not because oaths cause anything. It is because an oath is a name.** The Marches is now a place a record gathers in, and nobody in it chose that, and it entered the danger at the head of its own list and did it anyway.

**The five-block audit, in one line each.** **Block 1, the figure and the stone:** the card's six terms landed in Chapter 104 and not 114 and fixed the wording for the volume; two faces withheld to hold the name budget; one Ash figure spoken aloud in a room and deleted at review; and the block's real cost is a paragraph under the three rules, in the same ink, saying that **a rule about what is written down is not a rule about what is done** — the volume saying on the page, in its first block, that its own central device does not work. **Block 2, the river changes course:** the card's crossing out improved into a crossing that does not open at all, which nobody can tell from a refusal and which nobody says the word about; the drift figure written as a projection on faith because the return is in another country's chapter; and the refusal in one question asked in about four seconds, and the word *refused* spent fifty times in ten chapters as ordinary vocabulary so that Chapter 124 can land in it unremarked. **Block 3, the first third:** the mechanism once, in trade terms, and never again; the named working refused on a date and a community and a forged witness, unexplained, for Volume 06; and the block's best page unasked for, a two-line correction down a line in a hand that is not a clerk's from a woman four hundred miles off, **and the county does not know he knows.** **Block 4, the second third:** the guild proved better than the north without a stranger's mouth, by a shoulder and three pegs and a line that came down two fingers to the left; the flood as the third answer and not a curse, with the date given and the reason withheld; the marks on the walls found twice and read neither time; and **the volume's answer, in a room, in Chapter 140** — the eight stones are not eight of the same instrument and a column of counties is not a cause. **Block 5, the last third:** the delegation that cannot arrive beating a card in which it did; the hand-over to the crew's authority carrying the guild's with the brother in a document and not in the room, which discharges §13's open question on the page; the span, the nine hours, the limp at the fifth hour in the leg he chose; a fair copy cut to 408 words with the working on a second sheet after a first pass claimed 406 over 1,117; and **an ending on a road, four hundred yards of it, and it is there, which is better than the image the outline fixed and is not a sentence about a man.**

**What this close changed, and it is four things and three of them are one-line corrections.** **First, the plan's date.** The guild's plan is **of the twelfth rolling of the year before**, at all five places across two chapters, and the reason is that `chapter-0131.md` already implied it in a character's mouth for a bridgewright's reason — a plan written in a rolling when the water is sitting where it always sits has to carry a stop in it — and it sits before the Water, which is what the four stops are for. **This was the one defect four review passes and two repair passes left alone, correctly, because it is a fact about the calendar and not a sentence.** Three consequences bind: **Aren arrives in the eighth rolling of the current year**; **no chapter states an arrival rolling and none may be inferred from a chapter that does not say it**; and the plan's date moved, the day map did not. **Second, a fifth place of the same error that nobody had found:** the first column of the fair copy in `chapter-0145.md` had dated *the new work* to a single rolling, in the document the volume's climax turns on, and it now reads **the sixth, the eighth and the tenth**, which is what Chapter 149's own working says. **Third, a garble in Chapter 138 line 57** — *the fourth rolling of the fifth rolling* — now **the fourth week of the fifth rolling**, against Chapter 138's own line 31, and the word count is unchanged by that fix. **And fourth, `chapter-0138.md` line 11's bare *the seventh* after *the twelfth rolling of the year before*, which a reader would have parsed as the seventh of the year before, now reads *the seventh of this year*, which is the only thing in the chapter anchoring the arrival and was the one clause a repair pass could not safely touch while the date itself was still open.** No scene, no paragraph and no dialogue was rewritten, and no plot beat, date, money figure, cast name or calendar was moved.

**What it corrected in the state files, against the page.** **The figures: Batch 0011 31,648 · Batch 0012 29,130 · Batch 0013 16,315 · Batch 0014 35,340 · Batch 0015 34,128 · Volume 03 146,561 · manuscript 601,642**, per chapter **4316 / 3952 / 3156 / 2975 / 3959 / 2943 / 2978 / 2928 / 3724 / 4409** and **3408 / 3368 / 2794 / 3183 / 2916 / 3014 / 3380 / 3634 / 5136 / 3295**, footed at 146,561 and 601,642. **Four state files and one prompt were carrying 35,318 / 34,113 / 146,524 / 601,605, which was right before this close's own fixes and is wrong after them, and the Batch 0014 and Batch 0015 entries above are annotated with the supersession rather than silently edited, because the record of what was wrong is the useful part.** **And four stale claims about the plan's date are corrected: this file's Batch 0014 *what the block changed* and *the plan that was accepted instead*, the Batch 0015 first deviation, and `state/current.md`'s last-change paragraph.** `state/continuity.md` **item 375 is annotated as superseded on the dating by item 381 and is not renumbered**, and item 383 supersedes the totals in items 372, 374 and 380 by name.

**What it did not repair, and did not invent.** **The promise's age, Chapter 70's "eight weeks" for the Sedge Reach scour, the Chapter 10 column, and Mudmonth's length all stand as recorded defects, for the reasons the Volume 02 close gave, which have not changed: a defect that is recorded is a fact about the book, and a defect that is inherited silently becomes a canon with a footnote.** And **the Batch 0012 entry has no repair-pass record and this is a real gap in the record, not an omission in the work** — its own *Landed* line claims one and four traces of it survive inside its deviation list — **and this close does not manufacture a repair pass for a batch it did not review.** And the tile's prohibition is corrected from *not named* to **not explained and the device not named**, **because a prohibition built on *not named* would have forbidden the last line of Volume 03**, and that correction is the one most likely to save a Volume 04 batch from breaking the volume's best object.

**What Volume 04 is, and the two places it departs from the series outline, both flagged.** **A frozen valley that has kept a nine-generation promise, physically stable and politically trapped, in which about two hundred people are on a roll of carriers from birth in a hand nobody alive can name, and the volume's question is whether they are owed an asking, and the first asking is refused, correctly, and the refusal is the engine.** The volume is built on `outline/volume-03.md` §11's question and **not** on `outline/series.md`'s next-volume cell, and the reconciliation is written down: **the hinge question is paid inside the volume as a fact about a record rather than as a mechanism explained, and why-the-Gatehouse-was-rewritten stays Volume 11's.** It reaches Threadkeeper by qualification in Block 5, with **a seam in a stone course in a bridge in the current world as the cost, and no second bodily cost, because the Many-Handed stage's worsening of the limp belongs to Volume 15.** It spends **six names of thirty with twenty-four unfilled, one fewer than Volume 03's seven**, and it forbids an apology, and it forbids healing the brothers. **Two plan conflicts are recorded in `outline/volume-04.md` §16 rather than absorbed: `outline/series.md`'s Volume 4 escalation line and knowledge-control row require Edric's name in older administration and this close's guard forbids writing it in Volume 04, so the volume puts an office's hand on a two-hundred-year-old form instead and the series row is recorded as not satisfied and carried forward; and the pressure-rotation table's Volume 4 relationship work of *Aren and Edric's last private warning* is replaced by the breach and Mara, the brothers, and the two hundred on a roll.** Both are deviations and not improvements, and no later phase may read the outline as having satisfied either row.

**The question Chapter 151 opens on.** Not the valley. **The twenty-fifth of Hearthmonth, YR 313, a Saturday, a market day, the fourth hour, the day after the volume ends — and a man who was paid in ninety-six bushels of salt comes up two flights of a stair in Weir Street and reads a fair copy off a board under the bench, because anybody coming up two flights may read it, and asks for it to be copied, and nobody has to be asked.** And the copy goes four hundred miles to a bench of nine that has been asked four times and has said yes four times and is not a bench, and asks it a fifth question, and the answer is no, and the reason is in the fair copy, and the person who put the fair copy where anybody coming up two flights may read it is not the man who wrote it. **The road that opens is made of paper and carries nobody, and the man who cannot open it is the man who built it.**

---

## Batch 0001 — Volume 04, Chapters 151–160, "The Relief"

**Landed.** Chapters 151–160 in `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0151.md` to `chapter-0160.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes. **Main world for the twenty-fifth of Hearthmonth to the eighth of Goatmonth, YR 313, thirteen days elapsed, and then path time.** **33,045 words** by `wc -w` including title lines **after the Batch 0001 review repair pass**, per chapter **3766 / 3512 / 3281 / 3311 / 3388 / 3078 / 3003 / 3213 / 3265 / 3228**, which is over Batch 0011's 31,648 and under Batch 0014's 35,340, and no chapter is under 3,003. **The block as first written was 32,831 at 3766 / 3502 / 3281 / 3311 / 3276 / 3055 / 2993 / 3178 / 3265 / 3204, and both figures are recorded because the manuscript total is 634,687 and not 634,473, and a later phase that foots anything must use the repaired one.** **This is the length the prompt asked for and not Batch 0013's length**, and the reason is `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` §10 item 3: Volume 03's middle ran short and it showed, and a reader who is being asked to care about a council in a valley he has never been to cannot be short-changed in the chapters that take him there. **Ten `---` section rules, one per chapter, against a first pass of thirty-eight.** **Forty-six bolded line-openings, the manuscript's own way of marking a change of voice, and not one of them is a System line.** **One Gatehouse-voice line in the whole block, in Chapter 160, unattributed, in his own perception, with no hour against it, and he does not write it down** — and it is a different line from the four he has had, and it is the one line in this batch that says something true about the volume. **No System panel.** **Zero semicolons and zero em-dashes**, against Volume 03's last block's zero and zero and Batch 0014's three and zero.

**Cast.** **One new name and five slots unfilled, exactly as `outline/volume-04.md` §13 allows: Iven Sarr**, 58, who has kept the winter book of a valley four hundred miles off for nineteen years and has wanted it read by somebody who was not him for six years and has had two readings and one of them lasted a night. **He is named in prose once, in a letter, in Chapter 154, and by his own account of himself: *I am Iven Sarr, and that is my own name, and it is in the book in my own hand, and I have never once had to explain it to anybody.*** He is the batch's want in his own voice, all three parts: the original oath found and read in the room it was made in, the ninth generation told what it signed, and the reason he has never been able to make anybody ask him. **Volume 04's running total is one name of six and twenty-four of twenty-four slots unfilled, and the cap is thirty and this batch has spent one of it.** **Nine people are in this block's valley and none of them is named**: a woman of about forty at the Low Holm with a yoke, a man of about eighty-one who has cut withies since he was nine, a woman of about thirty with a slate, a man of about thirty-four with sixteen men and no work, a woman of about sixty-six at four looms, a girl of about nine at a gate, a man who makes nails and cannot sell them, and a man who sells nails. **The girl of nine refuses to give her name and offers a place-name instead, and it is a child's version of the whole of the volume's argument, and it is the first page of the valley.** Returned faces and no new ones: Mara Vey, Alish Renn, Sarn Oyelaran, Lysa Fenn, Aldis Fenn, Bram Ottery, Rowan Petch, Aled Rhys, Tovan Kest, Sabin Dain in a document and nowhere else. **Sian Pugh's name is not spoken once in the ten chapters**, and Edric Hunn is not written anywhere and is not in a column and is not in a room.

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so that no writer re-derives them as errors.**

1. **The prompt's frame and the prompt's "six weeks" disagree, and the day map is binding.** The frame gives the twenty-fifth of Hearthmonth to the seventh of Goatmonth, YR 313, and the crossing out on the eighth, and `outline/volume-04.md` §8 Block 1 and §17's chain agree with the frame exactly. **That is thirteen days elapsed, not six weeks.** The chapters are dated off the chain: **Hearthmonth 25 Saturday market, 27 Monday, 29 Wednesday, 30 Thursday market, Goatmonth 1 Friday, 4 Tuesday, 6 Wednesday, 7 Thursday, 8 Friday not a market day at the fourth hour.** **Judgement: the prompt's "six weeks" is a slip and taking the day map was right**, and this is the third time in this project that a figure has been carried into a prompt instead of counted, and the standing rule from the Volume 02 close's §10 item 3 is that a later phase states a figure, checks it, and records the check.
2. **Iven Sarr's letter arrives on the fourth of Goatmonth and not on the fourth of Longlight.** **Longlight falls outside Volume 04's span in both directions** — the whole of Longlight YR 313 is before the volume opens on the twenty-fifth of Hearthmonth, and the volume ends on the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth YR 314. The letter is dated **the fourth of Goatmonth, YR 313, a Tuesday, not a market day, four days in front of a road.** **Judgement: necessary, and it improved the block.** A letter with a deadline four days in front of it is a better document than a letter with a comfortable one, and it is why he is on the Old Toll sill at the fourth hour on a Friday that is not a market day instead of wandering in from a season away.
3. **"Ch 152–154 · the county post, the hearing, the count, and the letter" includes the hearing, and the hearing may not be staged.** **Judgement: the hearing is referred to twice, once in Chapter 156 and once in Chapter 154, in a sentence about a room that will be shut, and it is not held and not adjourned and not refused and not staged, and nobody is sent for.**
4. **The prompt's requirement 1 says "Term four is the price," and the Marches' fourth term is *The stone is not to be renewed in the north's presence*, which is not a price.** **All six terms are printed in Chapter 151, verbatim, in order, and no line is re-worded**, and the room names the first as the reason a delegation cannot arrive, the second as the reason the man who built the road may not write a name at the north end of it, and the sixth as the door. **Judgement: the binding instruction is the verbatim print and it is met, and the parenthetical appears to be a carry-over from the fifth terms of the County of Ash, where the fourth is about a person struck being able to answer. It is recorded here so that no later writer reads it as an error and no line of the six is re-worded to make it true.**
5. **The four-line notice in Chapter 152 is a second notice and not the one that came down in the ninth rolling.** **Judgement: better than the card, and the only way to make the prompt's "not an answer to anything, because there was nothing to answer" true is that no question was ever put.** The resolution of the twentieth of Hearthmonth says no person is to be sent to ask anybody in that county anything, and this county kept it, and about four hundred people in a county that is not on any map can be entirely certain that nobody came, and that is the one thing this case has been able to give them. The seed letter went into a bag in that county addressed to a county four hundred miles off a shut road and has not been delivered and is not going to be inside the year, and the notice does not mention a jar, a seed, a wheat, a price or a keeper, and nobody in that county is in default of anything.
6. **The mail sack is opened by Rowan Petch, on the county post at his own cost, and the Archive is not the woman who opens the envelope.** **Judgement: correct, and it is what makes the chapter work.** The post cuts the band because he is the post and he has a bag, and a bag is not a place a thing is decided about. The envelope comes back **cut along the left edge with a knife**, which is the office's own method and the lesson of the last eleven months of this case, so the answer a refusal got was a knife and not a letter.
7. **Aren is not in the room when the refusal is opened.** The prompt says getting it out is a party receiving a document and not a scene in which he explains himself, and the page went further. **Judgement: better than the card.** He is four hundred yards away on a bridge for the whole of Chapter 153 and does not know that it came back cut, and Mara Vey learns of it from a document, which is the only instrument in this case that can carry a fact about a man to a person who was not in the room, and nobody apologises to anybody and there is nothing in the four hundred and eleven words that a person could apologise about.
8. **The return stitch is prepared in the worst possible place on purpose, in daylight, in front of about twenty men, on the outside face of a public parapet, at the height of a thumb, on the side the weather gets at, and the volume does not say why until Block 4.** **Judgement: correct, and the not-securing is visible in the preparation and is not explained.** A girl of about eleven comes up off the near bank with rushes and puts her hand flat on the mark to see whether it is warm. The last line of Chapter 155 says what a thing left out in daylight is, and it does not say why he did it, and nobody in that yard saw a stranger cut a mark in a bridge at the ninth hour on a Wednesday. **Block 4's parapet is now already on the page and does not have to be invented.**
9. **The valley's word for a roadkeeper is *the hand*, said once, in Chapter 159, and not argued.** **Judgement: right, on the Volume 03 close's own terms — three counties, three arrivals from three ends of the world, and a writer who argues it has misread what it is.** A man of thirty-four says *We have had a hand at every wall in this valley for nine generations, and there has not been one road*, and nobody in the room remarks on anything.
10. **Nine valley people and no name among them.** **Judgement: right, and the name budget is one of six for the volume.** It also means the batch's biggest new fact is carried by a man of fifty-eight and the batch's last beat is carried by a woman of about sixty-six who is *not* named and is not Batch 0002's Nev Treen, 66, of the eighth generation, and the two are different people and no later writer may merge them.
11. **The count of carrier obligations is beaten with the form's own construction, and the form is the Crown's and not the county's seven-column form, and the chapter says so.** **Judgement: correct, and it is the first time the county's own seven-column form and a Crown form of the same series have been on the page in the same room, and saying *this is not the county's own form, which is ruled in seven* keeps the seven where it belongs.**
12. **The office's reply is a figure and only a figure: 2,640, dated, in a woman's hand, with no seal, no number, no returnable date, no signature and no name.** **Judgement: better than the card and it is the volume's argument in one number.** The figure is larger than the district, so it is a number of entries and not a number of people, and the only way anybody in that county can find out which is to count the district, and counting a district is a thing a Crown's hand is for. **No name in a Crown's hand for the second time in two volumes, and there is still not one since the twenty-seventh of Fallowmonth.**
13. **Chapters 158 to 160 are about six weeks in the valley and the ninth generation is not asked anything in any of them.** **Judgement: correct and it is the hard part of the block.** The first asking is Block 2's and its refusal is Block 2's engine. What this block does instead is put a word about him in a valley where two hundred people are on a roll from birth and where **there is nowhere to enter it**, and let him not ask what the word means, and let the not-asking cost him a day and the valley a fact it cannot get out of and cannot prove it ever had.
14. **`the whole of` was at 37 in the first pass of this block and is at 10.** **Judgement: the first pass had found a tic and this is a prose repair made before the block was finished and not after.** The figure a later grep gets is **10**, against a house baseline of 2 in Chapters 141–150 and 3 in Chapters 131–140, so it is still three to five times the house figure and it is recorded here rather than hidden, and two of the ten are a document's fourth line printed twice because it is read twice.
15. **The `---` section rule was at 38 in the first pass and is at 10, one per chapter, against Volume 03's Block 4's four in 35,340 words.** **Judgement: the first pass had made a metronome into a device at roughly seven times the volume's precedent. One per chapter is a metronome and it is the number a later phase should carry.**
16. **The money beat, and what it was allowed to touch.** The prompt required "the sixth of the four hundred miles" and forbade re-deriving the contract. **Chapter 152 cites the contract whole and recomputes none of it** — three thousand two hundred and forty pence, two hundred and eighty-eight bushels of salt at nine pence the bushel and two hundred and sixteen bushels of seed at three pence the bushel, and the two together are the contract and not two payments — **and foots a new Merefen column on the page before any total is said**: a carrier at ten pence a day is ten and a horse's keep at four is four, ten and four is fourteen a day, fourteen nine times is a hundred and twenty-six, four sheets at twopence is eight, a hundred and twenty-six and eight is **a hundred and thirty-four pence, which is two marks and three shillings and twopence, and he has paid it himself.** The keeper of records moves the figure off the wrapper onto the same page as the list, and the reason is that a wrapper goes to a filing room. **The sixth of the four hundred miles is carried in the same chapter as a marginal note against the eleventh line of a list of things sent**, where the second copyist records that eight of eleven counties in a column from the summer have a stone in a field, that this county is the sixth of the eight, that the finding is against nobody and carries no opinion, and that the keeper has entered it once and is not going to enter it again, because a second entry of a finding against nobody is a habit and this office is against habits. **The one fifth, 648 pence, is named as payable on the return, given up in a gateway, and out of the pocket of a yard four hundred miles off, and the same figure is the price of two hundred and sixteen bushels of seed in the same line of the same account, and the text says in one sentence that the two are the same number and are not the same money.** No figure from the Marches is set in a column beside a figure from Merefen and the 4,632 and the 1,752 and the 480 are not on the page and are not re-footed.
17. **The `about N seconds` count is 9 and `cut` is 14, and all fourteen are ordinary.** **The fourteenth is Chapter 156's: the mark is on the parapet of the third bay and was cut into it at the ninth hour on Wednesday, and the same repair that put the bell back at four hundred yards from that parapet is what added it.** The seconds are the finding in Chapter 152, the row of figures in Chapter 155, the man who will not be asked and the two who look, and the gate. 18. **Chapter 154 dated the fourth of Goatmonth a Tuesday, and seven places in the chapter leaned on it, and this entry did not declare it. — DECLARED AT THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW.** `outline/volume-04.md` §17's chain, counted from the twenty-fourth of Hearthmonth, YR 313, a Friday, on the carried thirty-day month, gives Goatmonth 1 as a Friday and **the fourth as a Monday**, and the block printed a Tuesday in the chapter's first line, in its own bolded section head, in the keeper's account of the county council sitting, in the reason the second copyist read a letter, and in the entry on the wrapper. **It also leaned on the error twice in substance and not only in wording:** the council's Saturday was *four days* away when the fourth of Goatmonth is a Monday and the next Saturday is five, and the returnable date on the Crown's paper was *the seventh of Thawmonth, YR 313, a Monday* when the first of Thawmonth is a Sunday and the seventh is a Saturday and the year must be YR 314, because YR 313's Thawmonth is behind a Goatmonth. **Judgement: the slip was the batch's worst and it is entirely the review's finding and not a judgement of the writing. The reason a block gets a date wrong is that it dates from the prompt's frame instead of from the chain, and the reason this entry did not catch it is that the entry was written from the chapter and not from §17, and both of those are now instructions in `workspace/volume-04/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, which prints the chain and the three dates it produces.** The page is repaired and the repair is in `chapter-0154.md` at seven places and in `chapter-0155.md`, `chapter-0156.md`, `chapter-0157.md`, `chapter-0158.md` and `chapter-0160.md` at the other date and figure findings, and none of it changed a scene, a beat or a character.

**The fourteen `cut`s are a stick cut out of a fence rail three times, a seal cut with a knife twice, a county cut off, a strip of cut paper, a mark cut into a parapet, a mark cut with a cold chisel, a mark cut in a bridge, a peg cut out of a fence rail, a lintel cut by a grandmother, and withies cut for thatching. Not one of the fourteen is the working, and the working is not named, priced, scheduled or described as available anywhere in the block.**

18. **Chapter 154 dated the fourth of Goatmonth a Tuesday, and seven places in the chapter leaned on it, and this entry did not declare it. — DECLARED AT THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW.** `outline/volume-04.md` §17's chain, counted from the twenty-fourth of Hearthmonth, YR 313, a Friday, on the carried thirty-day month, gives Goatmonth 1 as a Friday and **the fourth as a Monday**, and the block printed a Tuesday in the chapter's first line, in its own bolded section head, in the keeper's account of the county council sitting, in the reason the second copyist read a letter, and in the entry on the wrapper. **It also leaned on the error twice in substance and not only in wording:** the council's Saturday was *four days* away when the fourth of Goatmonth is a Monday and the next Saturday is five, and the returnable date on the Crown's paper was *the seventh of Thawmonth, YR 313, a Monday* when the first of Thawmonth is a Sunday and the seventh is a Saturday, and the year must be YR 314, because YR 313's Thawmonth is behind a Goatmonth. **Judgement: the slip was the batch's worst and it is entirely the review's finding and not a judgement of the writing. A block gets a date wrong by dating from the prompt's frame instead of from the chain, and this entry did not catch it because it was written from the chapter and not from §17, and both of those are now instructions in `workspace/volume-04/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, which prints the chain and the three dates it produces.** The page is repaired, in `chapter-0154.md` at seven places and in `chapter-0155.md`, `chapter-0156.md`, `chapter-0157.md`, `chapter-0158.md` and `chapter-0160.md` at the other date and figure findings, and none of it changed a scene, a beat or a character.

**Filler counts for the next writer's baseline, counted as whole words and not as strings, so that `stood` at 13 is not `understood` at a higher number: `nodded` 0, `held` 11, `wrote` 37, `stood` 13, `plain` 2, `counted` 8, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** Against Volume 03's Chapters 141–150 baseline of 0 / 18 / 38 / 12 / 1 / 10 / 0 / 0, in thirty-four thousand words. **`the whole of` 10 and `about N seconds` 9, both after the repair: the reviewer counted `the whole of` at 9 against the 10 this entry claimed, and the repair made the claim true by adding one in Chapter 155, where a keeper says that was the whole of the difference.** Zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, ten `---` rules, forty-six bolded line-openings, one Gatehouse-voice line, no System panel.

**What the block changed.** A fair copy of a county's account, which had been addressed to a shelf in a press and unread, went onto a board under a stair in a guild hall where anybody coming up two flights can put a hand flat on it, **because a water-barge hand of twenty-seven from that county read it standing in the rain and asked for a copy, and nobody had to be asked.** A keeper of records read all four hundred and eight words of it aloud twice before she let it leave the room, and the second reading was for a man from a county of clay who was in the room and is not the person the book is about. **Two copies went out of the room: one down the stair to a bench of nine and one four hundred miles on the same barge the man came in on.** A bench that has been asked the same thing five times and has said yes four times was asked the fifth time and said no, in about a minute and a half, and the reason is a board of six lines in a county that is not on any map, **and the sixth of those lines is a door, and the reeve of Merefen put into her own county's minute that a door is a thing you can be killed in.**

A four-line notice came down a road and answered nothing, because there had never been a question, and a list of things sent was ruled in four columns with no fifth, and there are two inches of bare paper at the right-hand edge of it. **A refusal of four hundred and eleven words came back cut open along the left edge, and the clause that landed was the number of persons the offer had been made out to, which is not stated on the sheet, and an office that will not print a number of persons cannot show that any person has not been instructed — which is the other half of the same arrangement the Archive has had in its press for four years and a half in a column headed *Keeper* in a hand that is not the man's.** A count was beaten in nine minutes with a construction nine words long, and an office with no seal on it answered a legal argument with a figure of 2,640 and no name.

A man told a keeper of records on the eleventh hour of the sixth of Goatmonth that he was going out of the county on the eighth at the fourth hour, and he gave her the numbers before she asked, and **for the first time in four years and a half there was no morning in it, because there is no day over there that anybody keeps, and the not-asking stood at nine and was not made a tenth, and the reason is that a keeper who asks that is asking a person to be able to do a thing.** A letter arrived from a valley four hundred miles off that is in nobody's book, from a man of fifty-eight, asking for a man who can read a hand and not a man who can cross, **and the man who can read a hand was in the room and it was a keeper of records and not the road warden, and she was asked to go and did not, and said why in four words: *A keeper does not travel.*** A county council read the letter out on the Saturday and minuted that nothing was done with it, and the man who had come about it was already four hundred miles off.

**A road was made properly for the third time in the manuscript and the page is about the difference between a road that is prepared and a road that is agreed, and the difference is one line in a notebook and three of nine questions with no figure in them.** Nine questions in nine minutes with the hour against each of the nine, and a witness at this end who is a man of forty-seven on a bench because he was elected, who said before he did it that he would be able to say that a man he knows was on a bridge and would never be able to say what he did there. A mark cut into a public parapet at the ninth hour in front of twenty men at the height of a thumb, on the weather side, where any child can get a hand to it. A bell rung by a boy and a carpenter on a Friday that is not a Monday, and the arrangement not his. And a crossing out at the fourth hour of the eighth of Goatmonth, from a sill with no glass in it and no third seat, with a lintel on the far side with **nothing on it at all**, and a girl of about nine at a gate who told him the whole of the valley's position in nine words and a sentence.

**And the valley is not starving.** The Burn runs at about four feet a second under old ice with a lot of water in it. There are four seed houses and two families who count them and have never compared their counts in a book. Eleven children were born in the last twelve months in eleven steadings and a town of about four hundred people, and a woman with a slate comes up the bank about once in ten days and reads the numbers out to whoever is in the room, and nobody has ever argued with her about one. Nobody is hungry, nobody is cold, nobody is in a hole, and **there is not one yard of made road in a valley of four hundred people, and a woman with a yoke could not say what the rest of her gate is for.** A man of eighty-one said it out loud: nine generations of somebody standing up and asking this valley to keep the winter in the room over the cross in the cold, and it has never once been refused and it has never once been asked whether keeping it is the same as agreeing to it. He said he would have said no, and that he is eighty-one, and that nobody ever thought to want to hear it.

**And a man of fifty-eight keeps the only roll in a valley nine generations deep in a room over a byre, and about two hundred people are on it because they were born, and nobody who has ever kept it is in it, not one, and the man who wrote the first line about that wrote it about himself and is not in the book.** He told a stranger his whole want in about a minute and said it badly and said why he said it badly, and in the telling he used a sentence out of his own handwriting that he has not looked at in nine years, and **nobody in that room and nobody in this project has said where that sentence came from and no character may ever tell him.** And a young man of the ninth generation stopped in a doorway because a word was used about him in an argument about a gate, and a man standing nine feet away with a cup he had not drunk from had never heard the word and did not ask.

**And at the fourth hour of a morning in the ninth week of a winter in its ninth generation, a woman of about sixty-six at four looms said *You are the carrier off the road*, and it came out in a gap in a sentence about a gate, and she had not decided to say it, and he said *I am not a carrier* in about a second and a half before he knew what the word meant, and she did not ask him what he was.** By the sixth hour of that morning four people in that valley were using the word about him and none of them had asked him anything, **and the roll in the room over the byre has columns for the day and the mark and the hand and a name in the older spreads, and there is nothing in it for a word somebody said at a gate, and that is not an oversight, that is the design.**

**The cost, entered.** A road made of paper that carries nobody, and a man who built it may not write a name in the line's book at this end of it. A bench asked five times and said yes four times and has a fifth answer in its minute. Two inches of bare paper at the right-hand edge of a list of things sent, and a strip of cut paper in a press that weighs nothing and is the only object an office has answered a person with. A figure of 2,640 with no name in it, and the only way to check it is to count a district. A keeper of records who has a hole in her book with a shape to it and did not ask. **A mark cut into a public parapet at the height of a thumb, where any child can get a hand to it.** A lintel with nothing on it and a girl who would not give her name. **A word said about a man in a gap in a sentence at a gate, and a valley that has no form on which to enter a word anybody said at a gate, and a man who did not ask, and no character in the volume has noticed that he did not ask.**

**The question Chapter 161 opens on.** **A stone room over a byre and a roll nine generations deep and a hand in it that is not any hand anybody alive can name, and a man of fifty-eight who has had two readings of that book and one of them lasted a night.** The ninth spread is about two hundred lines that begin at the top of a page and go down in one run, and every one of them has a day against it and a mark against it, and a day against a line of that kind is the day the person was born, and the man has kept it for six of the nine years and did not write any of it, and he will not read a name to a stranger tonight and he will not read a name tomorrow either, and he has said that there will be a fifth thing and it is the last thing he has got and he is keeping it for a man who asked.

**What the next phase inherits.** `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` §8, §9, §10 and §12 first, then `outline/volume-04.md` §8 Block 2, then this entry and continuity items 393 onward. **The Marches' six terms are printed in full and verbatim in Chapter 151 and no line may be re-worded.** The county's form is seven columns and no volume may call it six. The `cut` string is greppable and all thirteen hits in this block are ordinary. **No main-world month, no real-world month and no main-world weekday is spoken or written in Chapters 158 to 160 in any register including a notebook, and the one conversion attempted was refused in his own notebook and is the second refusal of a conversion in this case and the first time he has had a number and no frame.** The tile on the coping is named twice more as an object, is not explained, its device is named by nobody, and the road to that end of the line is closed in four lines of writing and was not reopened. The marginal instruction was cited in a return and refused about in a gateway and was not explained and is not in anybody's mouth. **Stage 3 is not reached, not attempted, not offered as a plan, not priced and not described as available, and no lineage is read, and the cut is not named.** The hearing stands and was not held. The letter for a jar is not answered, was not delivered, and no living holder of the other end was found. The three marks on the outside of the seed vault are not mentioned and the keeper's decision is not taken. Sian Pugh's name is not spoken. The breach is open, the not-askings stand at nine, and no apology and no mistake has been made available to anybody in these ten chapters.
