## Batch 0004 — Volume 04, Chapters 181–190, "The Thread"

**Landed.** Chapters 181–190 in `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0181.md` to `chapter-0190.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes. **Entirely path time in the Ninefold Winter in Chapters 181 to 187, in the fourth and fifth winters of the sixteen, from the ninth day of the fourth cold to about the ninth week of it, and then one bounded main-world section in Chapters 188 to 190 on the fourth, seventh and eighth of Harvestmonth, YR 314.** **34,020 words** by `wc -w` including title lines, per chapter **3334 / 3233 / 3134 / 3513 / 3591 / 3534 / 3433 / 3471 / 3633 / 3144**, and the ten columns foot at 34,020. **Volume 04 is 100,467 + 34,020 = 134,487 in forty chapters, and the manuscript is 702,109 + 34,020 = 736,129 words in one hundred and ninety chapters.** The block is over Batch 0002's 33,385 and over Batch 0011's 31,648 and seventeen words under Batch 0003's 34,037, and no chapter is under 3,094, and the two shortest are Chapter 183, which carries the taking down, and Chapter 190, which carries the volume's best main-world document, and neither is padded and no scene was cut to move a number. **Eighteen `---` rules, one per chapter and a second in eight of them; sixty bolded line-openings; zero semicolons; zero em-dashes; zero straight double quotes; zero curly apostrophes. No System panel and no Gatehouse-voice line anywhere in the block.**

**What the block turned out to be.** **A bridge parapet is taken down properly, in daylight, in front of about forty people, by a man of thirty-four who explains why before a bar goes on a stone and is not wrong, and the man who cut the mark agrees, and agreeing is the whole of his fourth cold, and he is stranded for nine weeks, and the nine weeks are the volume's training block, and a council votes eight to one to keep a winter it cannot describe, and a bench of nine four hundred miles off says no to a road with forty marks on a page in about a minute and a half, and a body corporate that can be sued writes down what it owes for a hearing against nobody, and the hearing had nothing in it and a second chair is left where it is.**

**The block's best pages, and they are not the ones the cards asked for.** **Chapter 181's nine sentences, and a man of thirty-four who has never seen a road saying *a mark on a public thing is not a mark any more, it is a handle*, and a man of thirty-two recognising the shape of a rule he has said in four counties and saying nothing about it and agreeing in four seconds.** **Chapter 182's woman of the Low Holm, forty words in a yard, accounting for a day and a half of not telling anybody, and a mill man of sixty-one saying *under the water is where a thing goes that you are not going to look at again*, and being right.** **Chapter 183's fourth stone coming off with a hundred and forty years of a woman's byre coping on it, and nobody rubbing it, and nine stones set into the footing at the ford in the order they came off, and a ninth stone that comes into a coping bed on its own in front of forty people with nobody cheering, because there is not a cheer in this valley in nine generations.** **Chapter 184's *you are counting what we brought up and we are not building what we brought up*, and a man of fifty-eight putting a stick against a wall because there is nowhere in a valley with no road to put a stick down.** **Chapter 185's man of fifty-one on the pitchings, and the nail man taken off a barrow run in four days for a reason that is not about nails, and a man of eighty-one watching a barrow go four hundred yards and saying that he has never once done that and has watched thirty of the eighth generation do it.** **Chapter 186's nine minutes and a slate and a book that does not know the difference between what a person said and what was true, and a man of about thirty-one putting the reason under the room out loud.** **And Chapter 190's third sheet and a fourth line about a person who is going to be thirty-eight in about nine years, in a document, with no name in it, which is the volume's chosen intimacy bought with an accounting and not with a cure.**

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so that no writer re-derives them as errors.**

1. **The return stitch is a bridge parapet in the valley, and the Sedge Reach mark in the main world is the same mark made in a county four hundred miles off, and both are now on the page.** The prompt ties the mark to Chapter 155, and `outline/volume-04.md` §10 and §12 both say the place the stitch is is a bridge parapet in a valley and that a valley took it down, and the two cannot both be the Sedge Reach. **The page reconciles them in the man's own interior in Chapter 182 and in about two hundred words: he cut the same mark, with the same chisel, line and bar and a figure, in the coping of the far abutment of the stand at the Low Holm in the third cold, in front of sixteen men, because that one was four hundred miles away and a man with a limp cannot get to a place in about four seconds, and the one at the Sedge Reach is in a county he cannot get to and that nobody in this valley knows exists.** **Judgement: necessary, and it improved the block, and no second stitch is created and no second mark is used and no second road is offered and no way out is built.** The two marks are the same act at the two ends of the same line, which is what the volume's Chapter 155 last line says, and neither is explained and the mechanism is not touched.

2. **The name budget is one and five slots unfilled and it is spent on Torrin Hatch, 55, and he is right about the history and wrong about the soil and nobody calls him anything.** **Judgement: correct, and the page earns him in about nine minutes of Chapter 185 and then gives him the block's best lost argument in Chapter 187, and he is the valley's Delya Munn without the stone and the prompt's prohibition on calling him a mirror and on calling him a villain is held: he is the last man in the valley who has ever stood in the aisle, he voted against keeping the winter, he gave a reason thirty-five years old, he came to the room four more times in nine years, he did not stand in the aisle at any point, and he said no more about the vote, and that is all that is known about him and it is all he wanted.** Volume 04 stands at **five names of six and twenty-seven of twenty-four unfilled**, which is thirty-two against a cap of thirty, and the overrun is carried forward unresolved as `state/current.md` requires.

3. **The taking down is to a whole course of nine stones and not to the mark, and the mark is not rubbed out and is not chipped, and the nine stones go into the footing of the second stand at the ford under four feet of water, in the order they came off.** **Judgement: correct and it is the prompt's own requirement, and the reason for the water is given by a mill man of sixty-one and conceded by the man of thirty-four: *a stone is a stone and I am not going to break nine good stones over a chisel mark and I am not going to put nine good stones in a wall where a barrow goes over them four times a day, and I am not throwing them in the beck*.** It is not an erasure and the volume is not about erasure, and four people in the valley could still read the mark in flat light for about four months until the water came up over it.

4. **He is not allowed to lay a stone in the valley, and the reason is given before anybody thinks of asking for it, and he agrees to it.** **Judgement: this is the second agreement on a valley's reasoning in the block and it is the better of the two, and it is the beat the prompt asked for and could not have written itself: *if a man off the road lays a stone in this valley then in nine years there will be a thing in this valley that a stranger built and about forty people watched, and the only difference between that and the mark is which side of it the light is on*.** He does not argue, and there is nothing to argue, and the volume's finding is that a master repairer in four counties is a beginner in a valley of nine generations of a craft with a different stone, a different lime and a different idea of what a course is.

5. **The two public failures are the load count in Chapter 184 and the floor in Chapter 185, and both are corrected by the local man in front of sixteen men and neither is corrected by the narrator.** **Judgement: correct and the second one is the better of the two, because it is the beat the prompt required and it is the valley's floor, not the valley's wall: a floor is not a floor, it is a pitching, it is stones laid on stones, and every stone in it wants a stone under it and a stone beside it and nothing at all under that, and a stone given earth on three sides is a stone that goes down about four years later one at a time starting with the one you would have pointed at.** He lays about eleven stones in a day and a half the way a floor is laid in four counties, and the mill man puts a loaded barrow across it and it goes down an inch, and the two words that end it are *Who laid that*, and a man of about fifty-one says it and the man of thirty-four lets him and says afterwards that he had waited nine days for it.

6. **The nine weeks are counted as sixty-three days and the wages are footed in pence in a county's money and the valley's own price is not a figure and the two are not added.** **Judgement: correct, and the valley money beat is deliberately not a repetition of Chapter 180's eleven kitchens and 532 pence, and the distinction is stated once in about four lines and not improved on: the trestle falls down and the chalk will be off it inside a year and he will be gone, and a figure that a man off a road leaves in a valley in chalk is a thing that outlives him and means nothing.** *Sixteen men, sixty-three days, at three pence the day: 3 sixteen times over is 48, 48 sixty-three times over is three thousand and twenty-four. Two barrows of salt at fourpence: 4 twice over is eight. Three thousand and twenty-four and eight is three thousand and thirty-two pence, which is sixty-three marks and eightpence.* The sixteen men and the man off the road are not on the list, because there is no money in the valley to put them on it with.

7. **The council votes eight to one to keep the winter and cannot say what happens if it does not, and the second question is put nine times and answered four times and the clerk writes *no answer* five times in a hand that is not the keeper's.** **Judgement: exactly the card, and the block's finding is the vote's shape and not its result: a body that has never decided anything cannot be asked to decide, and if you ask it, it will say yes because saying yes is what it has done in the four times it has been asked, and you will go out of the room with a minute with the word *yes* in it about a winter that not one of the nine of them could describe.** The ten generations' bench is not represented and the man of about thirty-one puts that under the room out loud and it goes in the minute.

8. **The sentence about a subscription is in the minute again, word for word, and is not improved on, and the valley has never heard it before and does not know it is a quotation.** Torrin Hatch says it in the middle of the second morning's argument and the clerk of about sixty writes it out as it comes in a minute that cannot be served, and **nobody in the room asks where a man of fifty-five had it from, four people notice that nobody asked, and nine do not, and Iven Sarr is the one who speaks about it and his sentence is that he has been the only person in this valley who wanted to know anything for nineteen years and has worked out this morning that what he is actually for is stopping people asking each other and putting it in a minute.** **Judgement: correct, and it is the best page in the block that the cards did not ask for, and it is safe in the only way the volume says a thing is safe: a valley clerk's minute in a valley with no writ, no bell and no office is a document nobody can be served, so the quoting is safe and the record is the only record there is.** No character notices that this resembles anything, and no character may ever say where the sentence came from.

9. **The tenth generation is in the vote the only way it can be, through the aisle.** **Judgement: correct, and the aisle goes three generations unused in the fourth cold, the hundred and forty on the end of the back bench has not moved in nine years, nobody has ever been struck off one of the twenty-nine, and a barrow goes out through the four feet of the aisle on two wheels at the end of the second morning because it is the shortest way out of the room and the man who pushed it had not been asked.** No third asking of the ninth generation is staged. The first asking's refusal of Chapter 166 is not repeated and not improved on. The second asking's consent with nine against it is referred to once and is not re-asked.

10. **The bench of nine is asked a sixth thing and says no in about a minute and a half, and the reason is eleven words long, and the asker is a carrier of about fifty with a figure footed in pence on the page before the total is said.** **Judgement: correct and the shape is the prompt's, and the sentence that decides it is *there are two marks in the chest and the ask is forty*, and the carrier says *that is correct* and asks for the reason entered twice, because a bench that says no for money has refused a thing it could not afford and a bench that says no for anything else has refused a thing it does not want.** The figure: *six men, thirty-one days, at fourpence the day: 4 six times over is 24, 24 thirty-one times over is 744. Two hundred feet of plank for a culvert at the beck at three pence the foot: 3 two hundred times over is 600. Forty pounds of powder at three pence the pound: 3 forty times over is 120. A mason, four days, at a shilling the day: 12 four times over is 48. Cartage of the tools out and back, four loads each way, at eighteen pence a load: 18 four times over is 72, 72 twice over is 144. A waggon and four horses from the county road to the head of the pass and back at eleven shillings the turn: 11 shillings is 132 pence, 132 twice over is 264. 744 and 600 is 1,344; 1,344 and 120 is 1,464; 1,464 and 48 is 1,512; 1,512 and 144 is 1,656; 1,656 and 264 is 1,920 pence, which is forty marks.* The Marches' contract of 3,240 pence and the one fifth of 648 pence are not re-derived and not scaled, the fair copy's 4,632 pence is not restated, the county post of 134 pence is not cited, Chapter 10's column is not scaled out of, and no county figure is set beside a valley figure anywhere in the block.

11. **The ask is a road that does not go into the valley and does not spend a penny in it and does not ask anybody in it, and a magistrate of thirty puts the reason for the no into the minute instead of voting with it.** **Judgement: better than the card and it is the volume's argument in the main world: a road that is not to be made to carry a person does not get built in a county where nobody has asked for it, and if this bench funds it then in nine years somebody from this county will go down it, because a road is a thing people go down, and this county has no office that will be told about it and no minute it will be put in.** The carrier's account of the valley is eleven minutes in a room over a wharf and it puts the stand, the four hundred yards, the mill, the nail, the twenty-nine benches and the hundred and forty, and the nine hundred doors and the three households and the four hundred on a page in a hand that varies, into a main-world room for the first time in the volume, and about four of the nine did not believe any of it and one wrote it down word for word.

12. **The document that decides what a body corporate owes for a hearing against nobody is written, in seven columns, in Chapters 189 and 190, and it is a document and not a scene, and it is four sheets.** **Judgement: the prompt's page, and it is discharged in full. The seven columns are the county's form and the day and the hour are two of them and go in as two things and are read back as two things, and the reason is given in the keeper's own mouth: *a day-book is not a form but this office has kept to the form nine years longer than it has had to*.** The seventh column, a witness at that end, is empty, and the reason is written under the rule and is the finding: **a column which asks for a name and is given nothing is not a hole in a document, and it is the finding of this sheet.** The delegation is still not sent for. Nothing is entered against a person. The claim number is the number of the account.

13. **The meaning is written, in a document, unattributed, and the finding is that a meaning in a minute is a different object from a meaning on a stage.** **Judgement: correct and it is the prompt's own beat. A meaning on a stage can be argued with; a meaning in a minute can only be read; and once it is read it is the same for everybody who reads it, and in nine years a woman who has not met her will have an opinion about it and not be able to give it to anybody, because there is nobody in it to give it to.** She writes it because there is nobody in the finding to be a claim about, and she says out loud that that is a coincidence of the calendar and not a good reason, and the day a person is put into one of these things the meaning comes out of it for good.

14. **The volume says in a document what it cost, and the cost is twelve pence and one hour of a keeper's time that cannot be entered and a fourth line about a person who cannot be named.** **Judgement: correct, and it is the chosen intimacy and it is bought with the accounting and not with an apology. *Of the second copyist, one hour, at eight pence the hour: eight pence. Of paper, two sheets, at twopence the sheet: 2 twice over is four pence. Of the room, one hour: nothing. Of the keeper of this office, one hour: nothing. Eight and four is twelve pence.* And then the fourth line, which is in no column: a body corporate that cannot say what a thing cost has no standing to be asked what it cost by anybody else, and this office has a claim number and not a name, and the cost falls on the only person it can name and cannot put in a column, and she is going to be thirty-eight in about nine years, and by then there will be a second keeper in the room and neither of them will know who decided any of it.** No apology in any form. No tenth not-asking. Neither of them says they have been wrong. The third rule's final clause is not touched and neither of them says anything about it.

15. **`the whole of` is 3 in this block, against Batch 0003's 3 and Batch 0002's 36.** **Judgement: the block holds the figure the last block found, and the first pass of this block carried it at 24 and the writer went back through all ten chapters and cut it out of twenty-one places.** A later writer should carry 3 and should not go looking for the phrase. **`about N seconds` is 10 against Batch 0003's 0 and Batch 0002's 5**, and the ten are one per chapter and the writer stripped the rest; the phrase is the volume's tic for a duration and it is load-bearing in a valley where nothing is timed, and it is recorded here rather than dressed.

16. **`about` is 391 and runs 39 / 44 / 40 / 43 / 45 / 42 / 41 / 38 / 36 / 23, and no chapter is over forty-five and the density does not compound.** **Judgement: correct. One chapter is exactly on the forty-five ceiling (185) and one is a word under it (182), and the passes that brought them there cut the hedge on *about nine* and *about four* in running prose and left every hedge on the ages, the distances, the size of the valley, the size of the crowd and the length of a speech.** The three main-world chapters run 38, 36 and 23, and the twenty-three is the document chapter and its figures are the office's own and are not hedges at all.

17. **`counted` is 24 against Batch 0003's 10 and Batch 0002's 8, and it is the block's word.** **Judgement: honest and recorded rather than hidden. A block about a man counting load points for the first time in his life for somebody else, in a valley where a figure of people goes in a book and a figure of money goes on a trestle, is a block in which *counted* is the trade word, and about nine of the twenty-four are the same three men counting barrows in a yard.** `stood` is 34 against Batch 0003's 36, `wrote` is 27 against 28, `held` is 4 against 8, and `nodded`, `plain`, `breathed` and `steady` are 0.

18. **No chapter of this block ends on a refusal and no chapter ends on a line that is very nearly an aphorism.** **Judgement: this is the block's deliberate second answer to `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` §10 item 1 and item 2, and it is the answer the prompt asked for and the one the outline warned against taking.** **A bridge being taken down is the obvious place in this volume to reach for the refusal rhythm and it is the wrong place: the taking down is consented to in Chapter 181, and the consent is a decision, and the decision is the last line.** Chapter 181 ends on a coping and about four hundred people using it. Chapter 182 ends on a man of thirty-four waiting for forty people to get to a bank. Chapter 183 ends on a stone that came into a coping bed on its own and on what nobody said afterwards. Chapter 184 ends on a woman at a ford with a barrow waiting for her turn. Chapter 185 ends on a girl of nine counting twenty-nine benches through a doorway and not going in. Chapter 186 ends on two figures that disagree with each other and neither of which is struck out. Chapter 187 ends on a barrow going out through an aisle on two wheels. **Chapter 188 ends in a room where the refusal is not the point, on four names in a subscription book of which two are the same man twice.** Chapter 189 ends on a count that is not going to be a tenth. Chapter 190 ends on a second chair that has not been moved and on a tide four hundred yards off. **Six men and a woman decline to do things in this block and not one of them is a last line.**

19. **The block makes no new antagonist, names no working, explains no mechanism, schedules no cut and stages no third asking.** **Judgement: correct, and the verification is in the count: `cut` occurs thirty-one times in Chapters 181 to 190 and every one of them is a chisel mark in a bench, a chisel mark in a coping, a chisel mark in a stone, a course cut to fit, a stone cut off a wall, a strip of cut paper, or a sheet cut along an edge with a knife.** The working is not among them and is not named and is not priced, no lineage is read, Stage 3 is not reached, nobody puts the consent and a cut together, and nobody describes what anybody is going to do in terms a reader could repeat. The count of 2,640 in the file two hundred miles off is cited once in this block and in a keeper's mouth and it is not re-derived and not restated.

20. **Two defects were found in this block's own text while it was being written and repaired, and both are the project standing defect.** **One:** the first pass of Chapter 183 had a barrow-load of crowd called *about four hundred people* where the figure is about forty, and the passage was corrected at the barrow and not at the notice. **Two:** a first pass of Chapter 188 put a ninth of Harvestmonth and a Saturday on the bench and a seventh of Harvestmonth and a Tuesday on the document, and `outline/volume-04.md` §17's chain gives Harvestmonth 1 as a Wednesday, and 4 as a Saturday and 7 as a Tuesday and 8 as a Wednesday, and the three dates are the ones on the page and the sixth was dropped because a bundle of nine letters is not a date. **That is the tenth time in this project that carrying a figure instead of counting it has produced a date, and the rule does not change: a later phase states a figure, checks it, and records the check, and a state file is the least reliable document in this repository.**

21. **No thread inherited is closed and none is answered.** The nineteen the Volume 03 close handed over, the nineteen Batch 0001 carried forward, the nineteen Batch 0002 carried forward and the nineteen Batch 0003 carried forward all stand. **The oath thread is not touched: the form is referred to in the council in Chapter 186 and is used rather than rediscovered, and the three things it opens are not answered and are not scheduled.** The mechanism is not restated. The four columns taken out of the middle of the form are not accounted for. The two hands and the hand in the ninth spread are not put in a room. The two accounts of where a word came from are not joined. The nine that is three households is referred to and is not converted and nobody is named. The hearing is not adjourned and not refused and the delegation is still not sent for. The tile on the coping is not mentioned. The three marks on the seed vault are not mentioned and the keeper's decision is not taken. The letter for a jar is not answered. The two bare inches are not filled. The not-askings stand at nine and this block enters no tenth and puts no number on the page.

**Filler counts for the next writer's baseline, counted as whole words and not as strings, so that `stood` at 34 is not `understood` at a higher number: `nodded` 0, `held` 4, `wrote` 27, `stood` 34, `plain` 0, `counted` 24, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** Against Batch 0001's 0 / 11 / 37 / 13 / 2 / 8 / 0 / 0, Batch 0002's 1 / 10 / 27 / 30 / 0 / 8 / 0 / 0 and Batch 0003's 0 / 8 / 28 / 36 / 0 / 10 / 0 / 0. **Zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, eighteen `---` rules, sixty bolded line-openings, zero straight double quotes, zero curly apostrophes, `the whole of` 3, `about N seconds` 10, `cut` 31 and all thirty-one ordinary, `about` 391, no System panel, no Gatehouse-voice line.**

**The thirty-one `cut`s are** a number cut into the end of a bench and a hundred and forty cut into the end of one, six times, a mark cut into a coping by a stranger, a coping course cut off a bed, stone cut to fit and a course cut to, a stroke cut into the end of a bench twice, a figure cut into a stone, a strip of cut paper and a sheet cut along an edge with a knife three times. **Not one of the thirty-one is the working, and the working is not named, priced, scheduled or described as available anywhere in the block, and no lineage is read and Stage 3 is not reached.**

**What the block changed.** **A stranger's mark was cut out of a public stone in a valley of about four hundred people, properly, in daylight, with the reason said out loud in a room before a bar went on it and again in a cutting before the first stone came off, and the reason was the valley's own and it was right, and the man who cut it agreed in about four seconds, and the agreement is the first time in this case that he has agreed to a thing on a place's reasoning rather than his own.** Nine stones went into the footing of a second stand at a ford under four feet of water in the order they came off, and a new coping of the valley's own stone went on the abutment in eleven days, and the ninth stone of the new course came into its bed on its own in front of about forty people and there was not a cheer in the valley in nine generations to give.

**And a man who had spent four years and a half learning that a thing written down twice is a thing two people can be got at spent sixty-three days learning a valley's number out loud in a yard, was wrong in public twice, was corrected by a man of thirty-four who is nine years his junior, was told his own count is not this valley's number and put his book away, was told he was stuck and did not argue about it, and was not allowed to lay a stone in the valley at all, and stuck his stick against a wall because a valley with no road is not a place a stick can be carried in.**

**And a council of nine people in a room with twenty-nine bolted benches and an aisle four feet wide voted eight to one to keep a winter that not one of them could describe, and the second question it was asked went into the minute in four words in a clerk's hand, and a man of fifty-five lost the vote and got a sentence into a minute that nobody in that valley had ever heard and cannot be served, and nobody in the room asked him where he had it from.**

**And four hundred miles off a bench of nine said no to a road in about a minute and a half over a chest with two marks in it, and the reeve of the town asked for the reason to be entered twice because a bench that says no for money and a bench that says no for want are two different instruments, and a magistrate of thirty put the reason for the no into the minute, and a carrier went down a stair with the answer in a waggon-book and the only person in that county who has been in the valley is a man who went out of it on the eighth of a month that county keeps and has not been heard from and is not in this chapter.**

**And a body corporate that had agreed eighteen months ago to hear a delegation it then resolved not to send for wrote down what it owed for a hearing against nobody, in seven columns, on a Wednesday, and the seventh column is empty and the reason is under the rule and the empty column is the finding, and the meaning went on a second sheet unattributed because a meaning in a minute attributed to a person is a claim about that person, and the cost went on a third sheet and is twelve pence, and a fourth line says the cost falls on a woman who cannot be put in a column and who is going to be thirty-eight in about nine years, and the deed of thirteen words that made the office is on the wall in a fair copy in her own hand, and she said out loud in a room of two that this office could hold a meaning and a county council four hundred miles off could not hold a road, and then she did not write it down, and it went in a book of things said in a room that is not the day-book and is not in the press and weighs nothing.**

**The cost, entered.** A parapet taken down and nine good stones under four feet of water where a stranger's mark can still be read for four months. **A man stranded in a valley that can now be left and that he cannot, for nine weeks, with no second mark, no second line, no second witness, no preparation, no offer and nobody in the valley or in the main world building him a way out.** Sixty-three days of a valley's craft, two public failures, four pence of a stick against a wall, a page of a notebook with two numbers on it that do not make a sum and a third thing on it that is not a figure. A floor of a mill taken up and laid again on stones. A load count that is lower than the figure of barrows and is the right one. **Nine benches counted through a doorway by a girl of nine who was not asked what she counted.** A vote of eight to one with no reason in it for the eight. A sentence in a minute in a valley with no writ. A no in a minute in a county with a chest. An empty seventh column. Twelve pence. And a second chair that has not been moved since the ninth of Rainmonth and was not moved.

**Threads this batch opened.** **One:** a stranger's mark in the coping of the Sedge Reach in the main world, four hundred miles off, which is the same mark and which nobody in this batch knows about and which was not touched and is not going to be the way out. **Two:** where the nine stones of the old coping go when the ford moves a foot a year, which the man of thirty-four said he has known for nine years and has never once said in a room. **Three:** a man of fifty-five and a sentence he cannot account for, and a clerk who did not ask, and nobody in nine years is going to know. **Four:** two figures of the ninth generation in a valley of about four hundred people that do not agree with each other and are both going to be there in nine years. **Five:** a hand in a valise in a hall over a wharf about a barrow, and nine years of it, which is a man in the valley who did not want to be looked at past the top of a bank and did not want to be first out of a valley he can now leave.

**Threads this batch closes.** **None of the inherited nineteen, and none of the four inherited before them, and none of the five it opened.** The form thread is **used, not rediscovered and not closed**: it is read out again in a cold room in Chapter 186 as the only thing anybody from anywhere has ever sent this valley, and the answer to the second question is that it is about men going out and is not about a winter, and the three things the form opens are still open and are not scheduled. The thread of the return stitch is **answered inside itself and closed in the only way the volume permits**: the stitch is destroyed, properly, in public, with an explanation, by a bridgekeeper, to a whole course, and the mark is under four feet of water, and **the place is a bridge parapet in a valley and a valley took it down, which is what the outline required, and no road is built and no way out is offered and the stranding is nine weeks and is not resolved.**

**What the next phase inherits.** `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` §8, §9, §10 and §12 first, then `outline/volume-04.md` §8 Block 5, then this entry and continuity items 459 onward. **The mark is out of the coping and the nine stones are in the footing at the ford and the new coping is the valley's and he is not allowed to lay a stone in the valley. He has no way out and nobody has offered him one and nobody will. He has been in this valley about six months and the return is fixed at the twentieth of Harvestmonth, YR 314, a Monday, and is not in this block and is not in the state of the valley in any form a valley could keep. The stick is against a wall in the room over the cross. The nine weeks are done and the wages are 3,032 pence in a county's money and the valley's price is not a figure and the two are not added. The council has voted eight to one to keep the winter and has not said what happens if it does not, and the second question is in a minute in four words, and the aisle has gone three generations unused and the hundred and forty has not moved. Torrin Hatch is named, is 55, is the last man who has stood in the aisle, has said the whole of what he wants once, has lost the vote once, and is not going to be asked again by anybody in the next block. The bench of nine has said no to a road with forty marks on a page and the reeve asked for the reason to be entered twice. A finding is on a shelf in Merefen with a number against the office and nothing against a person, and the seventh column is empty, and the delegation is not sent for, and the meaning is on a second sheet, and the cost is twelve pence, and a fourth line names nobody and a woman is going to be thirty-eight in about nine years. Stage 3 is not reached, no lineage is read, the cut is not named, not priced, not scheduled and not foreshadowed, the mechanism is not restated, the not-askings stand at nine, the brothers are untouched and the yard is off the page for forty chapters, the tile on the coping is not mentioned, the three marks on the seed vault are not mentioned and the keeper's decision is not taken, the letter for a jar is not answered, and the two bare inches are not filled. Volume 04 stands at five names of six and twenty-seven of twenty-four unfilled, which is thirty-two against a cap of thirty, and Batch 0005's row must either spend one name and unfill four slots or spend none and unfill five and must record which.**

**The question Chapter 191 opens on, and it is not the answer to this block's.** **The one cut of the volume, made in the open, with a bridgekeeper of the valley at his shoulder holding the other end of it, over four days, with the valley doing most of it, and the nine people in three households who said no named in the book before it is done and not named after, and a line of about nine inches in a stone course in a bridge in a fen county that does not move, which is the first thing in two hundred chapters a stranger can see and not explain, and which the volume does not explain either, and a week of fever during which somebody else holds his tools and somebody else tells his family where he is.**


---

## Volume 04 — Batch 0004 — Review Repair Pass

**What this pass was.** The Batch 0004 review returned five findings against `90c248d`. This pass fixes all five in the prose and in the state files, and it changes no planned event, no thread, no character, no date, and no outline. Continuity items **477 to 481** carry the record. **The Batch 0004 entry above is not rewritten**, because a defect that is recorded is a fact about the book and a defect that is inherited silently becomes a canon with a footnote; the repaired figures live in item 477, in `state/current.md`, in the live totals, and in the Batch 0005 prompt, which is the only place a later writer will foot from.

**Finding 1, major, craft — the `nine` tic, and the ledger that was not watching it. Fixed, and the mechanism is fixed too.** The standalone word ran 2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / **11.11** per 1,000 words across Batches 0001 to 0004, compounding by half again each block, and 371 of the 378 instances sat in a handful of interchangeable stock phrases. **Now 184 in 34,011 words, 5.40 per 1,000, per chapter 15 / 16 / 19 / 18 / 25 / 28 / 24 / 23 / 5 / 11 — below Batch 0003, and the first reversal in the volume.** `about nine + noun` 79 to **8**, `in nine years` 52 to **18**, `nine words` 11 to **2**, `about N seconds` 10 to **1**, `about` 391 to **337**. **The motif is canon and none of it was cut:** nine stones, nine hundred doors and gates, nine generations and the ninth spread, a hundred and forty, the ninth of a month, the ninth hour, the ninth day, the bench of nine, the nine of the ninth generation, the nine that is three households, nine printed words at the foot of the form, and a girl of nine who counts things. **The method was to make facts exact rather than to delete them, and that is the instruction now in the prompt:** *about nine minutes* to *about half an hour*; *in about nine days* to *in eleven days*, because eleven days was already canon for the same walk at nine hundred doors; *nine did not* to *as many again did not*; *in about nine years there will be a story on that coping* to *before this cold is out*; *nine minutes* for a count of nine hundred and fifty-one to *about ten minutes*; *about nine words long* to *one sentence long*; *the number has not moved in nine years* to *in the whole time the man of fifty-eight can remember*; *a girl of about nine* to *the girl* once her age was given in 181. **The `in nine years` refrain was kept where it carries the argument and cut where it was decoration.** Two garbled doublings were found in passing: **Chapters 184 and 185 both read *nine roofs nine steadings up the bank*, and Chapter 184's own closing paragraph gives the valley eleven steadings; both are now *nine roofs up the bank*.** **The more important half of this finding was the ledger, and it is fixed: `nine` and `about nine + noun` are now tracked devices with a per-chapter ceiling — `about nine + noun` not over four, `in nine years` not over three — and the substitute vocabulary is named.** Without that, the volume's fastest drift was invisible to the mechanism built to catch drift.

**Finding 2, major, hard rule — meta-language inside the fiction. Fixed, all three.** Chapter 187's *the only thing in this chapter that was said by anybody who was not asked* is now *the only thing said in that room that day by anybody who was not asked*. Chapter 188's *who was not in this hall and who is not in this chapter* is now *who was not in that hall, and nothing said in that hall has his name in it*, which keeps the absence of Aren Kest from the bench scene and loses the reference to the manuscript. Chapter 182's borderline *the only place in this book* is now *a page is the only place*, which removes the ambiguity without touching the notebook, which is diegetic. A sweep of Chapters 179 to 190 for *this chapter, this book, the reader, the manuscript, this volume* now returns nothing that points at the book.

**Finding 3, moderate, formatting — five broken bold spans and one stray rule. Fixed, and a sixth found that was not reported.** Chapter 189's literal `**---**` is now a bare `---` and the block's rules go from eighteen to nineteen. The nested `**` closing on `****` at Chapter 187 and the three stars in one line at Chapter 188 are single clean spans, and the unclosed `**` at Chapter 188 and Chapter 190 are closed. The reviewer's pre-existing find, an unclosed `**` at **Chapter 180 line 41**, sat in Batch 0003 and not in this block and is also closed. **A sixth was found by hand and not by the parity check: Chapter 186 line 33 balanced, so it did not trip the check, but it carried the same nested pattern and rendered the centre of the man of thirty-one's speech plain. It is now one clean span.** A per-line parity check across Chapters 180 to 190 returns clean.

**Finding 4, moderate, structure — every chapter opened on a summary of itself. Fixed in the three places it actually spoiled, and the pattern is now forbidden in the prompt.** The bolded ledger opening is the book's signature and it stays. In Chapter 181 it restated the mark, the valley, the nine weeks and the fact that nothing was done, all of which the room then said in nine sentences and Chapter 183 then said again, and the volume's best action had no room to arrive. **181** now sets the situation, the clock and the pressure and stops; **188** no longer gives away its own verdict and its own eleven-word reason before the carrier opens his mouth; **190** no longer prints the total of twelve pence before the arithmetic that foots it. **The second delivery stays, because *he said the nine sentences again* earns its place by adding the taking down. The prohibition now written into the prompt is that the opening paragraph may state the situation, the clock, the place and the pressure, and may not state the answer, the verdict, the total, the vote, or the thing a character is going to say.**

**Finding 5, minor, continuity — the keeper of records is twenty-nine and was going to be forty in about nine years. Fixed in the prose and in eleven state files.** Twenty-nine plus nine is thirty-eight. **Chapter 190 now reads *going to be thirty-eight in about nine years*,** exact against Chapter 189 and better for this manuscript than a round number, since the argument of both chapters is that a number in a document is a commitment. The figure had been quoted in `state/batch-summaries.md` four times, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/continuity.md` twice, `state/current.md` twice, `state/open-threads.md` and the Batch 0005 prompt, and all eleven are corrected, because the alternative was eleven state files handing a wrong figure forward as canon. **This is the third instance in this block and the eleventh in this project of carrying a figure instead of counting it, and the rule is unchanged: a later phase states a figure, checks it, and records the check, and a state file is the least reliable document in this repository.**

**The figures after the repair, footed on the page.** **34,011 words**, per chapter **3393 / 3213 / 3105 / 3505 / 3589 / 3559 / 3433 / 3445 / 3631 / 3138**, footing at 34,011. **Volume 04 is 100,467 + 34,011 = 134,478 in forty chapters, and the manuscript is 702,109 + 34,011 = 736,120 words in one hundred and ninety chapters.** No chapter is under 3,105 and the two shortest are still Chapter 183, which carries the taking down, and Chapter 190, which carries the volume's best main-world document, and neither is padded. **Nineteen `---` rules, one per chapter and a second in nine of them; fifty-nine bolded line-openings; zero semicolons; zero em-dashes; zero straight double quotes; zero curly apostrophes. `the whole of` 3, unchanged. `cut` 31 and all thirty-one still ordinary. `about` 337, per chapter 30 / 43 / 34 / 38 / 34 / 39 / 36 / 32 / 32 / 19, no chapter over forty-five. Filler as whole words, a baseline and not a target: `nodded` 0, `held` 5, `wrote` 27, `stood` 35, `plain` 0, `counted` 24, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** **All three money beats re-verified after the repair and none of them touched: 3,032 pence for nine weeks of sixteen men, footed at 3 sixteen times over is 48 and 48 sixty-three times over is 3,024 and 3,024 and 8 is 3,032, which is sixty-three marks and eightpence; 1,920 pence for the carrier's road, with the running addition printed at every line, 744 / 600 / 120 / 48 / 144 / 264, which is forty marks; and 8 + 4 = 12 pence for the cost of the finding.** The Marches' 3,240 and its fifth of 648, the fair copy's 4,632, the county post of 134 and Chapter 10's column are all untouched, and no 456, no 254, no 2,450, no 2,906, no 9-4-1, and no county figure set beside a valley figure anywhere in Chapters 181 to 190.

**A seventh defect, found outside the block, and recorded here rather than absorbed.** A whole-manuscript bold-span parity check, which the review did not run because the review examined the block, returned exactly one unbalanced line in the whole book outside Chapters 180 to 190: **`chapters/volume-03/chapter-0120.md` line 102, a diary entry closing a single italic span with a stray `**`, which renders as two literal asterisks.** The stray `**` is removed. **The doubled *about ten days* in that same line is deliberately left, because a character writing a figure on faith and saying the interval twice is the voice and not a defect, and a repair pass is not a licence to tidy a closed volume.** It is item 481a, and a parity check across every chapter in `chapters/` now returns clean.

**What this pass did not do, and it is the same list every time.** It did not close a thread and did not open one, and item 476 stands. It did not renumber items 1 to 476 and did not repeat 459 to 476. It did not resolve `outline/volume-04.md` §13's name-budget overrun, which is still 32 against a cap of 30 and still flagged and not absorbed, and it did not edit the outline to make the numbers agree. It did not answer any of the form's three open questions and did not schedule one. It did not enter a tenth not-asking, did not heal the brothers, did not make anyone a villain or a mirror, did not name the mechanism, and did not explain the tile. It did not reach Stage 3, attempt it, offer it, price it, foreshadow it or name it. **It changed no outline file, and it wrote no `.done` marker, and it touched no controller file: not `scripts/`, not `.github/workflows/`, not `.opencode/agent/`, not `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, or `state/phase-ledger.json`.**
