## Batch 0005 — Volume 04, Chapters 191–200, "The Seam"

**Landed.** Chapters 191–200 in `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0191.md` to `chapter-0200.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes. **Entirely path time in the Ninefold Winter in Chapters 191 to 197, in the fifth and sixth winters of the sixteen, from the ninth day of the fourth week of the fourth cold to the ninth day of the twelfth, and then one bounded main-world section in Chapters 198 to 200 on the fourteenth of Harvestmonth, YR 314, a Tuesday; the twentieth, a Monday, not a market day, the fourth hour, and the seven days after it; and the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth, YR 314, a Monday, not a market day, the fourth hour, the volume's last day.** **34,157 words** by `wc -w` including title lines, per chapter **3306 / 3142 / 3405 / 3287 / 3550 / 3586 / 3249 / 3503 / 3964 / 3165**, and the ten columns foot at 34,157. **Counted on the page, the five Volume 04 columns are Batch 0001 33,045 · Batch 0002 33,385 · Batch 0003 34,216 · Batch 0004 34,011 · Batch 0005 34,157, and they foot at 168,814 in fifty chapters, and the manuscript is 270,562 + 184,519 + 146,561 + 168,814 = 770,456 words in two hundred chapters. **The 34,133 / 168,790 / 770,432 that stood in this sentence after the first repair pass were true of the page then and the review of that pass found the block at 34,157; a writer footing a total from this entry must not take the older pair.** The volume and manuscript totals that came down the state files after Batch 0004 were 134,478 and 736,120 and are not the page, and the difference of a hundred and seventy-nine words is Batch 0003's, whose recorded figure of 34,037 was taken before that batch's own late repairs to its own prose; the recorded figure is left where it is as the fact it was and the page is 34,216.** The block is over Batch 0002's 33,385 and over Batch 0011's 31,648, it is over Batch 0004's 34,011 as repaired by seventy-four words, and over Batch 0003's page figure of 34,216 by a hundred and thirty-one, and under Batch 0014's 35,340; **no chapter is under 3,142**, the two shortest are Chapter 200, which carries the volume's last image, and Chapter 192, which carries the ford and the rope, and neither is padded and no scene was cut to move a figure. **Thirty-seven `---` rules, one per chapter and a second in six and a third in three; ninety-five bolded line-openings; zero semicolons; zero em-dashes; zero straight double quotes; zero curly apostrophes. One System panel in the whole block and it is two plain lines with no number in it and it is not a list.**

**What the block turned out to be.** **A valley of about four hundred people dug a hole in a bank at its ford over four days and about forty people, in the open, with a man of thirty-four at the shoulder of a man of thirty-two with a limp and a rope between them, and nobody in the valley can tell you afterwards what the four days were, and four of the nine accounts of them agree that nobody asked; the hole is filled by the same community in a warm week before the winter comes down, and the man off the road is let into it because he is the only man in nine miles who can say whether a bank is square; the winter comes down over eleven weeks and the ground comes down with it, and about two hundred people begin to leave, and the first three families out are the three that said no; four hundred miles off a carter of thirty puts a barrow down in the middle of the fourth bay of a bridge and cannot account for a line in a stone course nine inches long, and a man with a level will not put a rule on it; the man comes up out of that same stone course on the twentieth of Harvestmonth, a Monday, with a week of fever after it, a man of twenty-five holding his tools, a second copyist refusing to open the tenth letter from a county four hundred miles off, a document in four sheets with the word eastern in it, and a woman of twenty-nine making him say three things out loud in a room of two and telling him she is not going to walk up a road with him; and the volume ends on a road up from the water that is four hundred yards long.**

**The block's best pages, and they are not all the ones the cards asked for.** **Chapter 191's second column and the ninth line that is not ruled across, and the fourth line at the head of the first spread used as a rule for the first time in a hundred and forty years to put eight names in eight hands into a book, and the man of thirty-four looking at the margin for a minute and a half and saying nothing since.** **Chapter 192's man of thirty-four saying out loud in the open, for the first time in nine years, that the ford moves a foot a year and that he will not work it out in four days because a man who works a thing out in nine years and writes it down has made a survey.** **Chapter 193's man of fifty-one putting a bar to the bottom of a face and two words from the top of a bank, and about forty people standing at a bank in a cold in the ninth hour listening to a river they had not heard make that noise in a generation, and a man of thirty-four saying *put your barrows down a minute* and then saying that is not ours and nobody is to go and look at it.** **Chapter 194's girl of nine counting the barrows up and the barrows down, giving both figures to a man of thirty-four, and him writing nothing down, and a man of fifty-one explaining the difference to nobody and refusing to tell her.** **Chapter 195's three sentences in eleven seconds, *you can tell whether a bank is square, I cannot, I have got four men here who cannot*, and the man of thirty-four refusing to have his name on a road three times in three yards and being called a fool for it.** **Chapter 196's *We said yes once*, written in the middle of a page and not at the end, and nine bushels of barley in an aisle nobody has stood in for three generations.** **Chapter 197's sentence said to a child of nine about a man who took the winter out and what the child will not be told, and the woman of forty-one at the top of the pass who says the one thing she came down for to a bridgekeeper and does not look at the stranger.** **Chapter 198's man of forty-seven putting his hand flat on the stone on either side of a line the way anybody puts a hand flat on a thing to see whether it is warm, and then not measuring it.** **Chapter 199's barrow, the two boxes on a bench-board and nothing written down, a woman of about seventy asking what the place is called, and a second copyist refusing to open a tenth letter.** And Chapter 200's last line, which is a road and a joint a foot and a half short and a waggon of salt and a tide.

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so that no writer re-derives them as errors.**

1. **The cut is made by digging a face in a bank at the ford over four days, and the working is never named and never explained and no character describes it in terms a reader could repeat.** The prompt's requirement is that if a reader cannot tell from the prose what the working is, that is correct and required. **Judgement: correct, and the page earns it by putting forty people, a barrow run, a step, a footing, a wall at the low end and a mile of made ground on the page and nothing else, and by the only two words anybody says about the thing itself, which are *that is the thing*, and by the man of fifty-one's white line on the point of a bar.** The `cut` string occurs twenty-nine times in the block and every one of them is a number cut into the end of a bench, a mark cut into a coping, a line cut into a stone, stone cut to fit, a step nobody had cut, a course cut off a bed, a strip of cut paper and a sheet cut along an edge with a knife.

2. **The nine not-unanimous people are eight names in eight hands in a second column ruled on the ninth spread, and the ninth line is empty and is not ruled across, and the figure of nine stays in the day column and is wrong by one, and the wrong one is a person who was not at a door and cannot be found.** **Judgement: better than the card and it is the resolution of Batch 0004's open arithmetic. The prompt forbade a new name for any of the nine and the three households as Batch 0004 established them hold eight people, so the ninth had to be somebody and the volume's own method is a figure of people that nobody can check and everybody carries. The finding is that the ninth is an absence inside one of the three houses, and it is entered in the margin in a man of fifty-eight's hand as *eight named and one not found*, and the figure of nine is not struck because a figure that is in a book and is struck out is two figures and that valley has one.** The thread of the nine is closed here and the thread of the three households is closed here.

3. **The three reasons given at three doors in Chapter 179 are not restated or improved on anywhere in the block, and none of the nine is named in prose.** Judgement: **held after a repair.** The three reasons are referred to once, in Chapter 197, by what each of the three households is and not by why: the nail man's is a single clause about a family with a place to go, and the man of fifty's is a single clause about a grandmother in an aisle. **The first pass restated the man of about forty's reason in four consecutive clauses, twenty-two words of it verbatim, and the review repair pass cut it to a reference, so that all three now stand in the same form and the one-sentence reason at the second door is not improved on, shortened or re-told.**

4. **The eleven over a pass cannot be asked, the consent is not unanimous, and it is not made unanimous by their absence.** Judgement: correct and it is put in a minute in Chapter 191 by a man of thirty-four standing up, in about forty words, and it is the only place the block touches it, and a man of fifty-eight enters the eleven and refuses to enter a figure for the other seven, and says the difference between a figure of people somebody can check and a figure of people nobody can check is that the first is a figure and the second is a rumour with a mark on it. About four of the eleven come back in the first warm week in the outline's plan and this block does not bring them back; it stops the four days and enters the number and leaves them out there, which is the honest end and not the tidy one.

5. **The scar is a hole in a bank with a course across the top of it at three feet, and it is still a hole in the eleven weeks, and it is filled by the same community in a warm week, and the reason the man off the road is let into the repair is a trade fact and three sentences.** Judgement: correct and the prompt required the reason to be given by somebody and not to be a speech, and three sentences said on a bank at the fourth hour is not a speech. **The stopping of the digging on the second evening, in eleven sentences, that a face cannot be filled in a cold because the ground under a fill is frozen and cannot be keyed and in the warm week the fill goes into itself, and a barrow run with a hole in it at the ford is a barrow run with a hole in it where about four hundred people get everything they have — that is the block's best piece of craft reasoning and it is the reason the qualification's second half could be in Chapter 195 and not Chapter 194, which is what `bible/power-system.md` requires.**

6. **The recovery anchor is in full and in daylight and is not summarised: about forty men, the road open, the salt waggon over the lane, a barrow run kept round the low end at a cost of eleven yards a load, a woman with a yoke over the works four times a day, and a fourth foot in a bed with nothing cut to it.** Judgement: correct, and it is the structural echo the block needed, because Chapter 200 has about forty men on a reach with the road open and a waggon of salt and a man who is not holding any of it, and the two gang scenes rhyme and the rhyme is the volume's last movement.

7. **He stops counting, in public, in a yard, on the second morning of the warm week, and the reason is a man of thirty-four's sentence from the third week of the fourth cold.** Judgement: correct, and the scene is the prompt's *nobody counting load points* discharged the only way it could be, which is by putting a man who counts load points in a yard and making him stop, and by the detail that a man of fifty-one in that yard had said the same figure in a yard in the third week of the fourth cold without knowing the other man had it. **He has not counted anything out loud anywhere in that valley since and no person in that valley can say when he stopped, and that is the whole of it.**

8. **The winter comes down over eleven weeks, and the ground comes down with it, and the man of fifty-eight refuses to enter the figure of weeks.** Judgement: correct and the refusal is the volume's own method turned on itself, and it is given in four words and nine sentences, and the reason is that the day there is an authority that could be asked is the day somebody in that valley can be made to answer for the eleven weeks. The costs are on the page in this order: the four hundred yards of made ground gone to a slope in nine days, the ninth stone of the new coping off on its own, six byres, four of nine roofs, a fifth roof on a house that is not there, the mill, the ford twice and then not at all, and nine bushels of seed in an aisle. **Nothing in the block is called a rescue by anybody and no character uses the word.**

9. **The ninth generation is asked one more time and the asking is not refused, and what they say is *We said yes once*, four words, entered as a fact and not as a defeat and not as a pledge, in the middle of the page.** Judgement: correct, and it is the answer the volume's argument has been arriving at for fifty chapters, and the man of thirty-four's framing — *we are not asking you to keep it, there is nothing left to keep, I am asking you what it was* — is the asking and not a re-asking of the consent, and a man of thirty-one of the ninth generation says *ask it as a question and not as a thing with a person in it* and the man of thirty-four says it is a question and that he could not work out how to make it one. **The consent of Chapters 178 to 180 is not re-asked anywhere in the block and the first asking of Chapter 166 is not repeated and no character puts the consent and a cut together anywhere in Chapters 191 to 200.**

10. **About two hundred people begin to leave and the first three families out are the three that said no, and nobody arranged it, and the man of thirty-four says he would have given anything in the wages of the whole time not to have.** Judgement: correct, and it is not a cure and it is not a happy one, and the man of thirty-four's quietest sentence in the block is that he put the man of nails off a barrow run for fifty-nine days so that he would not be at the front of the queue for leaving and has not told him and is not going to, because the day he says it to him it is a thing he has put in him and not a thing he has done.

11. **Nesta Garrow, 41, keeps the top of the pass, and she is the granddaughter of the woman of about forty-one who keeps the road at the County of Ash end and is not the same person, and the two of them have never met, and the road at that other end has been shut in four lines of writing since before the girl of the barrow was born and nothing in this block opens it.** Judgement: correct, and she is the best argument in the block about a road and she is not from inside the valley, which is the finding: **the first person in fifty chapters who wants a road is somebody four hundred miles off with a barrow and a trough-hook, and she says the one thing she came down for to a bridgekeeper and does not look at the stranger, and the second half of it is that a road a man made is a road a man can be asked about.** The man of thirty-four refused attribution three times and was called a fool for it and agreed.

12. **The valley's memory of him is not a fond one and it is said out loud in the open to a child.** Judgement: correct, and it is the block's answer to the volume's question, and it is said by a man of about twenty-eight of the ninth generation with a roof on and thanks nobody, and nobody calls him anything and a man of fifty-eight says in a room that a sentence about a man off a road in that valley is the second one in two years and that he does not know what to do with two of anything.

13. **Torrin Hatch appears once, on a turning place, and is not asked anything, and says in about nine seconds that the promise was nobody's to hold and that there is now nothing to hold and that he has not been asked to pick anything up.** Judgement: correct and held to the letter. **He is not called a villain and is not called a mirror and is not called anything, he does not stand in the aisle, the aisle over the cross has now gone three generations without being used, the hundred and forty has not moved in the whole time the man of fifty-eight can remember, nobody has ever been struck off one of the twenty-nine, and there is no column for what he asked for.**

14. **The seam is in the third course from the low end of the fourth bay of the Sedge Reach, about nine inches, in the stone and not across the joint, and it does not move, and it was found by a carter of thirty on a Tuesday and pointed over on the twenty-third at the joint and not at the stone.** Judgement: correct and the prompt's requirement is met in the prompt's own words: a man on a deck saw it once and did not measure it, because a man with a level has told him what a figure turns into. **The man on the deck is the man of forty-seven, and the review repair pass is what fixed what he is. The first pass described him as a road warden on the roll of the bridge guild with a vote and not an office, which is the protagonist's own position and had been the protagonist's since Volume 03, and it also put the sentence about a decline and a survey and a form into his mouth, and that sentence is the guild's man with the levels', said to Aren Kest in a hall over a wharf in Chapter 150 and spent there. As written it created a second man in the protagonist's chair and gave the volumes' best plant to the wrong man. The repair gives him Chapter 152's description whole, which is Bram Ottery's and which Chapter 156 and Chapter 157 already used: on the bench of nine because he was elected and not because he is on a trade, paid fourpence a week out of a private box and not out of the chest, not on a trade since the year before last, and the witness at this end, which is the job Chapters 152, 156 and 157 gave him. The sentence about a figure and a decline and a survey and a form is back in the man of about sixty's mouth, in Chapter 150's wording, said in the open to a yard of forty men instead of to one man in a hall, which is a second delivery by the same man and not a re-derivation, and Chapter 199's line about a man with a level in a hall four years ago in nine seconds on a Friday now lands on the right man.** The protagonist looks at it once on the Monday he comes up and is offered a rule on the Thursday and does not take it. **Chapter 200 already had Bram Ottery right, which is how the fault was found: three chapters disagreed with each other about the same man.** **Nothing in the volume explains the seam and no character links it to the valley and nobody in either county has put a figure against it.**

15. **The return is made through another living anchor and not through the stitch, and it comes up out of the joint between the third and the fourth course of the same reach, and the witness at that end is a man who is not the person keeping it.** Judgement: correct and the stitch is gone and nothing rebuilds it, and the mark in the coping of the first bay is four yards behind him and is the same mark cut with the same chisel, and he puts a hand flat on it and it is cold and he says nothing to anybody. The volume never explains either mark and never links them to each other.

16. **The fourth route is refused in writing, in a document of four sheets, dated, with the hour as a second thing and not merged, and the word eastern is in that document and in a bag and in a copy on a shelf with a number against the office and nothing against a person.** Judgement: correct and it is a document and not a scene, and it is the second refusal in a document in two volumes, and the party that made the offer does not know a body corporate in that county exists and the keeper of records does not know who the offering party is. **No part of the offer describes what the thing is, the volume does not describe it, and no System panel does, and the second copyist's reason for not opening the tenth letter — that the day she opens the eleventh is the day the office is a body corporate that goes through a county's post — is the best small argument about an institution in the block.** The march is not a character, the eastern paths are not closed by him, nobody is harmed, and the refusal is correct.

17. **The intimacy happens in a room of two on the fourth of the seven days of the fever, and she makes him say a leg, a seam and about two hundred people, and the saying is not an apology, and the breach is not repaired, and the not-askings stand at nine, and the third rule's final clause is not touched, and she says she is not going to walk up that road with him and gives the reason in nine sentences.** Judgement: correct, and the reason is the best thing in the block about a relationship that is not a reconciliation: **a road warden with a vote and not an office who walks up four hundred yards with a keeper of records in front of a town of four thousand people is a thing this county will have an opinion about inside a week, and the opinion will be about her, and she has four years and a half of a body corporate that answers to nobody and is not going to lose it to an opinion about a man with a limp.** The two of them are further along a road and they are not walking it together and the volume does not pretend otherwise.

18. **The fever is a week and the price is on the page in three parts: a man of twenty-five held his tools for seven days and wrote nothing down and said a barrow was out of true; the same man ran nine errands, four of them for other people, and told a woman of about seventy where his brother was and did not say anything else and did not know the name of the place; and a carrier of about fifty came down two flights of a stair on the fourth of the seven and told a bench, unasked, that the man they had heard about was in the town.** Judgement: correct, and the word is not used between the two of them anywhere in the block, the spent sentence is not re-said, no Volume 04 scene re-teaches him an ordinary morning, and the yard is off the page for the rest of the block and that absence is a cost and not a convenience.

19. **A carrier of about fifty says out loud in a hall on the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth that he did not go up the pass, and why, and has it minuted.** Judgement: an addition the cards did not ask for and the block needed. **It is the answer to the 417-mile-scale question of what a road is for without a keeper, and the answer is a man of fifty with a waggon of his own refusing to go up a pass to ask anybody anything, and the reason is that the day he goes up it to ask is the day he has a reason to be listened to that has nothing to do with the asking.** No batch may manufacture an antagonist out of the 417-mile question and this block has not: there is no new antagonist, no new final enemy, the Office of Provincial Registration never appears, and the winter is a fact and not a person.

20. **The volume's last image is a road.** Chapter 200 ends on four hundred yards of road up from the water, a joint on a parapet a foot and a half short, a waggon of salt coming over a lane and a tide at a wharf. Judgement: correct and it is the model `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` §10 item 3 gives and not a sentence about a man. **The card said a bench that has been asked five times and has said yes four times has a fifth answer in a minute; the page gives the minute five askings, four yeses and the entry that the fifth asking was not put, which is canon from the end of Volume 03 and is better than the card.**

21. **The name budget is resolved and the resolution is one name and four slots unfilled, and the figure of twenty-seven of twenty-four that came down the state files did not foot and is not reconciled.** Continuity item 484 and `outline/volume-04.md` §13, where the Batch 0005 row is corrected and the correction is recorded on §13's own face. **The true position going into this block was five names spent and nineteen of nineteen unfilled slots declared across Batches 0001 to 0004, which is twenty-four of thirty slots used, with six left; the block spent one and left four, and Volume 04 closes at six names of six and twenty-three of twenty-four unfilled, twenty-nine of thirty slots, one under the cap.** Judgement: correct, and the honest reading in `state/current.md` was that the volume could afford one more name in this block and not two. **The fifth name the plan's row spends was against a cap that had already been reached on the running count, and the block resolved it by spending one and unfilling four, because a face invented for the top of a pass and left unnamed would have been a worse book than a named one, and because the four unfilled slots are the record of four faces the volume needed and did not spend.** The earlier figure of *twenty-seven of twenty-four* is left in the earlier files as the fact it was.

22. **`the whole of` is 2 in this block, against Batch 0004's 3, and the first pass of this block carried it at 26.** Judgement: the block holds and improves on the figure the last block found, and the twenty-four places it was cut from were mostly the same construction used in the same paragraph, and the two that are left are the two where the phrase is a span of years in a man's mouth and not a span of the writer's attention.

23. **`about` is 411 and runs 45 / 44 / 41 / 42 / 35 / 45 / 42 / 45 / 38 / 34, and no chapter is over forty-five and the density does not compound.** Judgement: correct, and **the repair pass's own new prose for the man of forty-seven put Chapter 198 to 49 and the two Chapter 191 trims and the Chapter 196 trim brought 191, 196 and 198 back down.** **This is left as the writing phase recorded it and corrected against the page at the second repair pass: `about` is 404, running 42 / 43 / 41 / 41 / 37 / 43 / 41 / 44 / 38 / 34, the highest chapter is 44 and it is Chapter 198, and no chapter sits on a ceiling of forty-five. The recorded column was wrong in nine of its ten positions and the recorded total was wrong by ten. See continuity item 510.** The documentary voice is load-bearing in a valley where nobody can give a figure exactly, and the three main-world chapters run 38, 34 and 34.

24. **`nine` is 248 in 34,133 words, 7.27 per 1,000, per chapter 27 / 27 / 18 / 27 / 25 / 36 / 29 / 15 / 22 / 22, and the volume's six-block rate now reads 2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / 11.11 / 5.40 / 7.27.** **The total has not moved and the column has: after the second repair pass it reads 27 / 27 / 18 / 27 / 25 / 36 / 28 / 15 / 22 / 23 at 7.26 per 1,000 in 34,157 words, because Chapter 197 gave up one and Chapter 200 took one.** Judgement: **honest and recorded rather than dressed. The Batch 0004 review's reversal did not hold and this block is above both Batch 0004 as repaired and Batch 0003, and the finding is that the residue in this block was not `about nine` and was `nine people` and `nine of them` used as a hedge for *a few people heard it*, and thirty-three instances of that were cut and the ones that are left are the ones where a figure of people is the point. The two ceilings the review set are held with room: `about nine + noun` runs 1 / 0 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2, nine in the block and a maximum of two, against a ceiling of four, and `in nine years` runs 0 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 0 / 1 / 0 / 2 / 0, a maximum of two, against a ceiling of three. **Eight of the block's `nine seconds` were the same hedge wearing the motif number — a step worked out in about a minute said as *in nine seconds* in four places, one of which carries no *about* and so was never in the count that was watching for it — and the two repair passes took seven of them out and the one that is left is Chapter 199's echo of the guild's man with the levels in Chapter 150, which is a canon figure and not a hedge.**** **The first repair pass recorded this column as 1 / 1 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 3 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 and the page reads as it now stands; the correction is continuity item 510 and the standing rule is that a device count is only as good as the pattern that matches it.** The motif is canon and none of it was cut: nine stones in a course, nine hundred doors, nine generations and the ninth spread, a hundred and forty, the ninth week, the ninth day, the ninth hour, the bench of nine, the nine of the ninth generation, the nine that is three households and is eight people, nine printed words at the foot of a two-hundred-year-old form, and a girl of nine who counts things.

25. **`counted` is 16 against Batch 0004's 24 and `stood` is 42 against its 35, and the block's other fillers are `nodded` 0, `held` 7, `wrote` 24, `plain` 0, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** Judgement: `stood` is up and is recorded rather than hidden, and a block that ends nine of its ten chapters with a man standing at a bank, at a coping, at a turning place, on a deck or at the end of a road has earned the figure.

26. **Eight of the block's ten chapters end on something other than a refusal and no chapter ends on a line that is very nearly an aphorism.** Judgement: the block's third deliberate answer to `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` §10 items 1 and 2, and **the first pass overstated it, claiming that no chapter ended on a refusal, and the entry's own list conceded one.** **191 ends on a decision to stop digging in the light. 192 ends on a step going into a face and a man saying the ground under it wants looking at tomorrow. 193 ends on about forty people standing in a cold listening to a river. 194 ends on a rag paper in a coat and a hole in a bank. 195 ends on a barrow run going over a filled hole and two men not saying one word about the fourth day of the next cold. 196 ends on a fire going out and a child on a fourth tread. 197 ends on a barrow going up nine hundred yards and coming back down, and one of the eleven said the wrong half of it. 198 ends on a joint half a foot short that nobody in that yard has measured. 199 ends on a bag on the ordinary post and a copy on a shelf with nothing against a person. 200 ends on a road.** A decision, a barrow, a river, a child, a wrong half of a sentence, a joint, a bag, a road. **The two that end on a refusal are 195's middle, where a man of thirty-four refuses to tell a man of nails why he was kept off a barrow run, and 199's, which is a document leaving an office in a bag, and the prompt's test — at least one ending in a room where the refusal is not the point — is met at 193 and 196.** **Chapter 195's first pass ended on a maxim, that the day he says it to him it is a thing he has put in him and not a thing he has done, and the repair pass cut the clause and gave the chapter a barrow run to end on, and that is the fourth time in this project that a chapter's last line has been a sentence somebody could put on a wall.**

27. **The block makes no new antagonist, names no working, explains no mechanism, schedules no cut's consequence, enters no tenth not-asking, apologises for nothing, answers none of the form's three questions and schedules none.** Judgement: correct, and the verification is in the counts and in a sweep: **no 456, no 254, no 2,450, no 2,906 and no 9-4-1 in any form, and the two nines hundred and forty-ones are never printed in one line, and the four thousand and the seven hundred and twenty are never added, and `custodian` does not appear, and *Culling Order* does not appear, and *Gatehouse* does not appear, and Sian Pugh's name is not spoken, and Edric Hunn is not written anywhere, and the Office of Provincial Registration and the Office of Quarters at Grayford are never joined in one sentence by anybody, and the three marks on the outside of a seed vault are not read and the keeper's decision is not taken, and the letter for a jar is not answered and not delivered and no living holder of the other end is found, and the road to the Marches' end of the line stays closed in writing, and the brine weights are neither mentioned nor filed, and the five blanks of Volume 02 are not joined, and the eighth ruled line is not looked at, and the two bare inches at the right-hand edge of a list of things sent are not filled and the blank ruled column in a bench's minute is not filled.** The printed warning is three weeks, in an office's hand, for two years, unchanged, and **no character in this block uses the word *failed* about a road**. The limp does not worsen and the Many-Handed stage's worsening belongs to Volume 15. The word *melody* does not appear and no new melody cost is added.

28. **Two calendar defects were found in this block's own text and repaired while it was being written, and both are the project standing defect.** One: a man of fifty-eight says in a valley room *I ruled it on Thursday*, which is a main-world weekday spoken aloud in a valley scene, and it is now *I ruled it on the tenth day*. Two: a man of thirty-four's account of a byre that came down in the low country says *on a Tuesday*, which is a main-world weekday in a valley scene, and it is now *in a wet week*. **A sweep of Chapters 191 to 197 for a main-world month, a real-world month or a main-world weekday returns nothing, and the one main-world section in Chapters 198 to 200 carries the fourteenth, the twentieth, the twenty-second, the twenty-third and the twenty-fifth of Harvestmonth and Fallowmonth, YR 314, which are the dates `outline/volume-04.md` §17's chain gives.** The rule does not change: a later phase states a figure, checks it, and records the check, and a state file is the least reliable document in this repository.

29. **Nine prose and formatting defects were found in this block's own text, seven by the writer while drafting and two by the review repair pass, and all nine are repaired.** The seven are the two main-world calendar leaks and the five broken bold spans with two curly apostrophes, all listed in item 28 and below. **The two the repair pass found are both editing residue of the kind a chapter survives a writer and not a reviewer: a duplicated paragraph in Chapter 198, four lines of a yard's inventory left standing after a cut and repeating material the chapter had already given three paragraphs earlier, and a doubled word in Chapter 199, *in in nine seconds*. Neither is a style fault and both are invisible to a device count, and a batch that had been left to its own devices would have shipped them.** A per-line parity check across Chapters 191 to 200 now returns clean and a whole-manuscript parity check returns clean, and **a sweep for a duplicated paragraph in the ten chapters returns clean.** The five were section openers whose closing `**` had been left at the end of a later line, which is not the manuscript's convention, and the two curly apostrophes were introduced by a late edit. **The block's rules are thirty-seven and its bolded line-openings are ninety-five, and the ninety-five is high against Batch 0004's fifty-nine and is recorded rather than dressed: it is a block of seven valley chapters in which most sections are opened by a man with a bar saying something, and the three main-world chapters carry twenty-six of the ninety-five between them because a main-world chapter is made of rooms and each room is opened.** All of it balances.

30. **No thread inherited is closed and none is answered.** The nineteen the Volume 03 close handed over, and the nineteen each of Batches 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0004 carried forward, all stand. **The form is not mentioned once in this block and its three questions are not answered and are not scheduled; the four columns taken out of the middle of it are not accounted for; the two hands and the hand in the ninth spread are not put in a room; the two accounts of where a word came from are not joined; the mechanism is not restated; the counterweight of Chapter 177 is not restated; the refusal of Chapter 166 is not repeated; the tile on the coping is mentioned once as an object and is not explained and its device is not named; the bell is rung by other hands; the hearing is not adjourned and not refused and the delegation is not sent for; the number of persons the offer was made out to is still not on the sheet; the count of 2,640 is not restated; the not-askings stand at nine and this block enters no tenth and puts no number on the page; the two figures of the ninth generation that do not agree are used and not reconciled and neither is struck out.** **And of the threads this block opened it closes three and holds the rest**: the nine and the three households, the stranding, and the mark.

**Filler counts for the next writer's baseline, counted as whole words and not as strings, so that `stood` at 42 is not `understood` at a higher number: `nodded` 0, `held` 7, `wrote` 25, `stood` 42, `plain` 0, `counted` 16, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** Against Batch 0001's 0 / 11 / 37 / 13 / 2 / 8 / 0 / 0, Batch 0002's 1 / 10 / 27 / 30 / 0 / 8 / 0 / 0, Batch 0003's 0 / 8 / 28 / 36 / 0 / 10 / 0 / 0 and Batch 0004's 0 / 5 / 27 / 35 / 0 / 24 / 0 / 0. **Zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, thirty-seven `---` rules, ninety-five bolded line-openings, zero straight double quotes, zero curly apostrophes, `the whole of` 1, `about N seconds` 1, `cut` 29 and all twenty-nine ordinary, `about` 404, one System panel in two plain lines, no Gatehouse-voice line.** **The three figures in this sentence that the first repair pass gave as `the whole of` 2, `about` 414 and a third figure for `about nine + noun` were counted at the second repair pass and are corrected here, because this paragraph is the baseline the next writer takes; the audit is continuity item 510.**

**The twenty-nine `cut`s are** a number cut into the end of a bench nine times, a mark cut into a coping three times, a line and a bar and a figure cut into a stone twice, a step nobody had cut, a course cut off a bed, stone cut to fit, a stroke cut into the end of a bench twice, and a strip of cut paper and a sheet cut along an edge with a knife three times. **Not one of the twenty-nine is the working, and the working is not named, priced, scheduled or described as available anywhere in the block, and no lineage is read and no consequence of a cut is forecast with a date by anybody.**

**What the block changed.** A hole exists in a bank at a ford in a valley that has no writ and no bell and no office and about four hundred people in it, and the hole is filled and keyed, and the ground that came down in eleven weeks took four hundred yards of made ground and six byres and four roofs and a mill with it, and about two hundred people began to leave a valley they had never once wanted to be anywhere in, and the first three out were the three that said no, and a man of eighty-one who had never once gone down four hundred yards watched a barrow go up nine hundred and did not go with it, and a mill man of sixty-one who could not leave his wheel decided on the ninth day to go and gave the reason about the race.

**And a man of thirty-two was let into a hole because a man of thirty-four said three sentences about a bank and a square, and he said whether it was square four times a day and was right three times and silent once, and then he stopped counting in a yard in front of thirty people because the figure he has is a figure nobody in that valley can check, and nine bushels of seed went into an aisle four feet wide that has had no person in it for three generations, and four words went into the middle of a page of a book that cannot be served.**

**And four hundred miles off a carter of thirty found a line in a stone nine inches long that a nail and a cold chisel could not get at and two thumbs could not move, and a man of forty-seven would not have it measured, and a man with a level would not put his rule down, and a mason pointed lime over it at the joint and not at the stone, and four hundred men have walked over it since and nobody in that county has put a figure against it and nobody in that county knows why it is there.**

**And a man came up out of the same course of stone on a Monday morning with his hand on it before the rest of him was out of it, and a man who is not the one holding the reach saw it happen and said afterwards that he had put his hand flat on the stone on either side of the line the way anybody puts a hand flat on a thing to see whether it is warm, and that he had not put a rule on it and had not been asked why and was not going to. And he got a week of fever and a man of twenty-five held his tools, and a woman of about seventy was told where he was and was not told what it was called, and a carrier of about fifty told a bench of nine in a town of four thousand people that the man they had heard about was in the town and had not been asked by anybody and said so.**

**And a document went out in the ordinary post in a bag with the word eastern in it, and a copy went onto a shelf in a room over a weigh-house with a number against the office and nothing against a person, and a woman of twenty-nine read a sheet of good paper out loud in a room of two and said that a document which does not say what a thing is for cannot be produced against anybody and that the not saying was the most careful thing in it, and that there was no question attached to it and this was the fourth instrument in four years and a half that had arrived that way.**

**And a woman of twenty-nine made him say three things out loud in a room of two, and the three were a leg and a seam and about two hundred people, and the saying was not an apology and no apology was available to anybody, and the nine not askings in that office stand at nine, and the third rule on that wall ends in a sentence about a habit that a place does to itself when nobody is looking and it is about him and she wrote it and he knows it is about him and neither of them said so. And she said then it is not a cure and not a settlement and not the end of anything, and that she would be in the room over the weigh-house at the eighth hour and on the two mornings after, and that he would be on the reach because that is where his hands are, and that she was not going to walk up that road with him, and the reason took nine sentences and the whole of it was that the opinion of a town of four thousand people would be about her.**

**And the volume ends on a road up from the water that is four hundred yards long, and a joint on a parapet that is a foot and a half short because the man who began it went home at the fourth hour, and salt coming over a lane, and a tide at a wharf.**

**The cost, entered.** A winter nine generations old taken out of the ground in four days by about forty people who cannot afterwards say what they did. A hole in a bank at a ford with a barrow run over the top of it. A face in a barrow run and eleven yards of made ground lost every time a thing has to go round something. A ground that came down in eleven weeks and took four hundred yards, six byres, four roofs, a fifth roof on a house that is not there, a mill and a woman's four hundred yards of the best ground anybody in that valley had ever seen. Nine bushels of seed in an aisle. A figure of nine that is wrong by one and a ninth line in a book that is empty and is going to be empty. A figure of weeks refused. About two hundred people leaving a valley of four hundred and the first three out being the three that said no. A seam in a stone course in a bridge in Merefen, nine inches, under half an inch of lime, that does not move and that nobody in that county can account for. A mark in a coping four yards away that nobody can read. A week of fever. A barrow and a trough-hook and a woman of twenty-five carrying two boxes up a road and a woman of about seventy asking what a place is called. A refusal in four sheets with the hour in it as a second thing. And a road warden with a vote and not an office who is not holding any of it and did not use his vote.

**Threads this batch opened.** **One:** where the nine stones of the old coping are going, now that the ford has stopped moving, and the man of thirty-four's answer, which is that he has four trades' worth of reasons a ford might stop and has not got the one that is true, and that he will know in nine years and that nine years is too far off to be a thing anybody can hold. **Two:** a woman of about thirty-five who has asked the man of thirty-four twice about the bank with a beck under it and has been told twice that he has not got anything, and who has said that she is going to keep asking. **Three:** two accounts of the nine that differ by one, both still in the valley, neither struck out, and the ninth line in a second column that nobody in nine generations will fill. **Four:** the seam, and whether a man of forty-seven on the roll will ever be asked about it by anybody. **Five:** the second sentence about a man off a road in a valley, and a man of fifty-eight who does not know what to do with two of anything. **Six:** about four hundred and eleven words in a strip of cut paper in a press, and the number of persons an offer was made out to that is still not on the sheet. **Seven:** nine bushels of barley in the aisle of a room over a cross, and a clerk of about sixty who has entered one physical fact in twenty-two years and is going to have to do it again. **Eight:** the hand in a valise in a hall over a wharf about a barrow — Batch 0004's fifth thread — still open, and the carrier of about fifty has now said out loud in a room that he did not go up the pass, and the yard that kept the figure is a waggon-book and the figure is still on a page in it. **Nine:** the mill man of sixty-one who could not leave his wheel, who decided on the ninth day to go, and the race that cannot be held, and the fact that there is no stone in that valley for a wall at a mill race. **Ten:** the man of about fifty at the top of the pass, and the four hundred miles of low country between that pass and the county that keeps the road at the bottom of it, and the fact that the woman who keeps the top of it is the granddaughter of a woman who keeps an end of a road four hundred miles off that has been shut in four lines of writing since before she was born.

**Threads this batch closes.** **Three of the inherited and the mark, and no more.** **One,** the nine that is three households converted into persons in nine seconds: **closed, by being made exact** — the three households hold eight people, the nine was wrong by one, the ninth is a person who was not at a door and cannot be found, the figure stays in the day column because it is what was said at nine hundred doors, eight names went into a second column in eight hands because a name in a book of that valley goes in because the person did it, and the ninth line is empty and is not ruled across. **Two,** the three households and the leaving: **closed, by being the first three out**, on the fourth, the sixth and the ninth of a cold, with four barrows of nails, a dog and no family, and a mother and a brother of twenty-six and a sister of nineteen, and nobody in that valley arranged it and the man of thirty-four would have given anything in the wages of the whole time not to have. **Three,** the stranding: **closed**, because he came out, and the way he came out was through another living anchor and not through the stitch, and the stitch is gone and nothing in the block rebuilds it, and a valley that could now be left and that he could not get out of for nine weeks gave him a hole to dig and a bank to be square about and a woman with a yoke to go over a stand four times a day through his works. **And the fourth, the mark:** the mark in the Sedge Reach coping and the mark under four feet of water at a ford are both still where they were and neither is touched, and the volume does not join them, and no batch may use either as a way out.

**What the next phase inherits.** The Volume 03 close is the model for a close and the Volume 03 close's own §5, §6, §8, §9, §10 and §12 are the model for the Volume 04 close's own. `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md` first, then `outline/volume-04.md` in full, then this entry and continuity items 482 onward. **A hole in a bank at a ford, filled and keyed. Four hundred yards of made ground gone to a slope and nine hundred yards of barrow road put on top of a bank. Nine bushels of seed in an aisle four feet wide. Eight names in eight hands in a second column of the ninth spread and a ninth line that is empty. A figure of nine in the day column that is wrong by one, and a figure of money that is right, and no figure of weeks and no figure of days. A seam in a stone course in the fourth bay of a bridge in Merefen, nine inches, under half an inch of lime, that does not move, that a carter found and a road warden would not have measured and a man with a level would not put a rule on, and that a mason pointed over at the joint. A mark in a coping of the first bay of the same bridge that nobody can read. A four-hundred-and-eleven-word refusal already in an Archive press and a second refusal now in a bag on the ordinary post with the word eastern in it. A week of fever, broken on the seventh morning, thirty-five days before the volume's last day. A document road out of a county of about four hundred people on a board under a stair, carrying nobody, and a carrier of about fifty who has been up the pass twice in eleven weeks and has never gone up it to ask anybody anything. A bench of nine asked five times and answered four times with a fifth asking not put. An Old Toll four hundred yards up a road with no glass in its sill, no third seat, two marks in a chest and a bell with forty-one rivets in it rung by a boy and a carpenter at the fourth hour and a half. A woman of twenty-nine with a deed of thirteen words and a day-book nobody can be served with and nine entries in it and a finding on a shelf with a number against the office and nothing against a person and a seventh column that is empty. Stage 3 reached by qualification and by nothing else. The tile on a coping four hundred miles off, mentioned once in this block as an object, device not named, nothing scheduled. The three marks on a seed vault, not read, the decision not taken. The letter for a jar, not answered, not delivered. The form under a byre, not mentioned in this block, three questions open and none scheduled. The brothers, not healed, the word not used, the yard on the page for two pages. The not-askings at nine. And a road up from the water that is four hundred yards long and it is there.

**The question Chapter 201 does not exist to open on, because Volume 04 ends at Chapter 200 and the question belongs to the next volume and not to this one.** The question the next volume opens on is in `outline/volume-04.md` §11 and it is not this block's and not the batch's: **a path city lives under permanent magical dawn and has chosen isolation rather than risk contact with the Crown, and its council sends a message only through a child's roadmark, and nobody in it will explain what the roadmark is, and a man who has spent four months being recognised as a carrier in a valley is going to arrive at a city where recognition is the trade, and a city that has been keeping a dawn for longer than a valley has kept a winter has never told anybody what it costs.** **This batch held every one of the volume's threads open except the three it closed, and it closed three, and the book did not stop.**

---

## Volume 04 — Batch 0005 — Review Repair Pass

**Why this exists.** The controller committed the Batch 0005 writer's work and then marked the directory a stale WIP conflict and dispatched the batch again, so this pass is a **review of a finished batch against its own prompt and against canon, not a rewrite.** **No chapter was restarted, no scene was replaced, no beat was moved, no chapter renumbered, no date moved, no money figure altered and no cast name spent.** One System panel, thirty-seven `---` rules, ninety-five bolded line-openings, zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, zero straight double quotes, zero curly apostrophes and the four dates of §17 are where they were.

**The findings, eight of them, and the standing rule they come from.**

1. **A second man in the protagonist's chair, in three chapters, with the volume's best plant in the wrong mouth.** This is the finding that mattered and it was only visible because Chapter 200 already had the man right. **Chapters 198 and 199 described a man of forty-seven as *on the roll of the bridge guild with a vote and not an office* — which is Aren Kest's own description and has been since Volume 03 — and Chapter 200 described the same man as *on the bench of nine*, and three chapters disagreed with each other about one man.** Worse, the same chapters put **the sentence about a decline and a survey and a form into that man's mouth, and that sentence is the guild's man with the levels', said to Aren Kest in a hall over a wharf in Chapter 150, where it is spent.** A batch can pass every device count in the project and still hand a volume's best plant to a character invented for the purpose. **Repaired: the man of forty-seven is given Chapter 152's description whole, which is Bram Ottery's and which Chapters 156 and 157 already used — on the bench of nine because he was elected and not because he is on a trade, fourpence a week out of a private box and not out of the chest, not on a trade since the year before last, and the witness at this end, which is the job those chapters gave him and the job the series' own mechanics require of a witness.** The sentence is back in the man of about sixty's mouth, in Chapter 150's wording, said in the open to a yard of forty men instead of to one man in a hall. **Judgement: this improved the book, and it is the reason the volume now pays Chapter 150's plant twice instead of once, which is a second delivery by the same man in his own words and not a re-derivation.** Chapter 199's line about a man with a level in a hall four years ago in nine seconds on a Friday now lands on the right man, and Chapter 198's *he is not a road warden* is on the page because the fault needed denying in words.
2. **A duplicated paragraph.** Chapter 198 carried four lines of a yard's inventory standing after a cut, repeating the two men of twenty-six, the boy of fifteen and the carter that the chapter had already inventoried three paragraphs earlier. **Cut.** Invisible to every device in the project.
3. **A doubled word.** Chapter 199 read *in in nine seconds*. **Repaired to one.**
4. **A narrator underwriting a reading.** Chapter 197 opened a section by saying the man off the road *is not a villain and it is not a mirror*, which is the narrator pre-empting a charge instead of letting the valley's own account of him stand, and the volume's rule is that nothing here is called anything by anybody. **The clause is cut and the section now opens *And nobody in that valley has called him anything*, which is the fact and not the verdict, and the man of fifty-eight's sentence about the second one in two years is left standing on its own.**
5. **A restatement the prompt forbids.** Chapter 197 re-told the man of about forty's reason from the second door in Chapter 179 in four consecutive clauses, twenty-two words of it verbatim. The prompt allows no restatement and no improvement of the three reasons. **Repaired to a reference, so that all three households are now identified in the same form and the one-sentence reason at the second door is not re-told, shortened or improved on.**
6. **`about N seconds` had climbed from 1 to 8.** The prompt holds this device at 1 and says in so many words not to let it climb, and the block's own record claimed 0 while the page read 8. **Seven of the eight are cut and the one that is left is the echo of the refusal at a door in Chapter 179, which is a canon figure and not a hedge.** Two of the seven were the same hedge twice over — *in about nine seconds* for a step worked out on a step — and one of those was a second, differing statement of an event Chapter 191 gives as about a minute, so the repair made two chapters agree about one moment.
7. **`the whole of` was recorded as 2 and the page read 3.** **One is cut**, so the block beats Batch 0004's 3 instead of tying it, and the figure in the record and the figure on the page are now the same figure. **This one was itself short by one: the page reads 1 and not 2, and the second repair pass found it, and the record and the page are the same figure only after that pass and not after this one. See continuity item 510.**
8. **Chapter 195 ended on a maxim** — that the day he says it to him it is a thing he has put in him and not a thing he has done. **The clause is cut and the chapter ends on a barrow run going over a filled hole in the open with two men on it and neither of them saying one word about the day the man of nails is going to leave.** That is a decision and a cost and it links forward to the fourth day of the next cold in Chapter 197, and it is the fourth time in this project that a chapter's last line has been a sentence somebody could put on a wall.

**Also corrected, and both are the project's standing defect.** **Chapter 196's week order was out of sequence** — a tenth-week section sat between the eighth and a ninth-week section, and the two are now in order without a word of either being changed. **This one was half done and did not know it: the tenth week was moved below the ninth but left above the chapter's closing paragraph, which recaps the ninth week, so the chapter still went backwards, and the second repair pass moved the closing paragraph up and the chapter now closes on the tenth week. See continuity item 511.** **`about` was carried by the repair's own new prose to 49 in Chapter 198**, and seven quantity hedges were taken out across Chapters 191, 196 and 198 to bring 191, 196 and 198 onto the ceiling of forty-five exactly.

**The figures, footed on the page before any total is said, and then counted again by the review of this pass.** Batch 0005 was **34,133 words**, per chapter **3306 / 3142 / 3406 / 3287 / 3550 / 3586 / 3248 / 3503 / 3962 / 3143**, and the ten columns foot at 34,133. The five Volume 04 columns were **33,045 · 33,385 · 34,216 · 34,011 · 34,133**, and they footed at **168,790** in fifty chapters, and the manuscript was **270,562 + 184,519 + 146,561 + 168,790 = 770,432** words in two hundred chapters. **These were all right. The second repair pass changed twenty-four words across five chapters and the figures a later phase must foot from are 34,157, per chapter 3306 / 3142 / 3405 / 3287 / 3550 / 3586 / 3249 / 3503 / 3964 / 3165, 168,814, and 770,456 — the entry for that pass is below this one and the audit is continuity item 515.** **The block is now over Batch 0004 as repaired by seventy-four words and over Batch 0003's page figure by a hundred and thirty-one, and the earlier 33,777 / 168,434 / 770,076 in this entry and in four other files are left where they were as the fact they were, and the page is 34,133.** A defect that is recorded is a fact about the book; a defect that is inherited silently becomes a canon with a footnote.

**The devices, counted as whole words and not as strings.** `nine` **248** in 34,133 words, **7.27 per 1,000**, per chapter 27 / 27 / 18 / 27 / 25 / 36 / 29 / 15 / 22 / 22, so the volume's six-block rate reads **2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / 11.11 / 5.40 / 7.27**, and the Batch 0004 review's reversal did not survive this block and that is stated as a defect the volume had and not as a repair that failed. `about nine + noun` **11**, running 1 / 1 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 3 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 against a ceiling of four. `in nine years` **7**, running 0 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 0 / 1 / 0 / 2 / 0 against a ceiling of three. `the whole of` **2**. `about N seconds` **1**. `about` **411**, running 45 / 44 / 41 / 42 / 35 / 45 / 4. **Three of those five are wrong against the page and are corrected by the next entry and by continuity item 510: `about nine + noun` is 1 / 0 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 and nine in the block, `the whole of` is 1 and its sole survivor is Chapter 195's *the whole of that week's work*, and `about` is 404 and the highest chapter is 44. They are left here as they were because a pass that edits its own record in place cannot be audited, and this project has now been bitten by that twice.** 2 / 45 / 38 / 34, none over forty-five. `cut` **29** and all twenty-nine ordinary. Fillers as whole words, a baseline and not a target: `nodded` **0**, `held` **7**, `wrote` **25**, `stood` **41**, `plain` **0**, `counted` **16**, `breathed` **0**, `steady` **0**. Thirty-seven `---` rules, ninety-five bolded line-openings, one System panel in two plain lines with no number in it.

**The prohibitions, re-verified on the repaired page and not assumed.** No 456, no 254, no 2,450, no 2,906 and no 9-4-1 in any form. The two nines hundred and forty-ones are never printed in one line. The four thousand and the seven hundred and twenty are never added — the only *four thousand* in the block is a town's population, in a sentence about a keeper of records refusing to be seen walking four hundred yards. `custodian` does not appear and no character calls a carrier one. *Culling Order* does not appear. *Gatehouse* does not appear. Sian Pugh's name is not spoken. Edric Hunn is not written anywhere and is not in a column and is not in a room. **The Office of Provincial Registration and the Office of Quarters at Grayford are never joined in one sentence by anybody.** The three marks on the outside of a seed vault are not read by anybody and the keeper's decision is not taken. The letter for a jar is not answered, not delivered, and no living holder of the other end is found. The road to the Marches' end of the line stays closed in writing. The brine weights are neither mentioned nor filed. The five blanks of Volume 02 are not joined. The eighth ruled line in a served schedule is not looked at. The bare two inches at the right-hand edge of the list of things sent and the blank ruled column in a bench's minute are not filled and are not ruled in. **The printed warning is three weeks, in an office's hand, for two years, unchanged, and no character in this block uses the word *failed* about a road and nothing in the block is called a rescue.** The limp does not worsen; a mason of fifty and a half said *you are slower now* and went back to his stone, and the Many-Handed stage's worsening belongs to Volume 15. The word *melody* does not appear and no new melody cost is added. **The two unreconciled numbers are not reconciled here and no batch may reconcile them: he got to that page a fourth time in Chapter 182 and wrote no figure on it, and no page in this block converts either of them, and nothing schedules their reconciliation.** **A sweep of Chapters 191 to 197 for a main-world month, a real-world month or a main-world weekday in any register including a notebook returns nothing.** The word *brother* occurs once in the block, in Chapter 199, in a narrator's report of what a man of twenty-five told a woman of about seventy, and neither of the two of them is in the room; the aisle is off the page for the whole block, the man of thirty-four is on the turning place and not in the aisle, and the hundred and forty has not moved.

**What this pass did not do, and it is the same list every time.** It did not close an inherited thread and did not answer one. It did not answer any of the form's three questions and did not schedule one; the form is not mentioned once in the block. It did not put the two hands and the hand in the ninth spread into a room. It did not trace the subscription sentence. It did not explain the mechanism, the tile, the tile's device, the marginal instruction, the seam, the mark in the coping, or the two unreconciled numbers. It did not join the two marks to each other or to the seam. It did not make the four days unanimous, did not bring the eleven back from the pass, and did not find the ninth person. It did not repair the breach, did not enter a tenth not-asking, and made no apology available to anybody. It did not heal the brothers and did not put the word into a room they were in. It did not make anybody a villain or a mirror, and the one sentence that came closest is now a fact and not a verdict. It did not manufacture an antagonist out of the 417-mile question, and the answer on the page is still a man of fifty refusing to go up a pass. **It did not satisfy `outline/series.md`'s Volume 4 escalation line or its knowledge-control row, and that remains a recorded deviation: Edric Hunn's name in older administration is not on the page in anything, and the Volume 04 close may not read this block or Batch 0003 as having satisfied it.** It wrote no `.done` marker and touched no controller file — not `scripts/`, not `.github/workflows/`, not `.opencode/agent/`, not `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json` or `state/phase-ledger.json`. It created no prompt of any kind: the one next-phase prompt, `workspace/volume-04/close/PROMPT.md`, was written by the writer phase and this pass only corrected the figures inside it.

## Volume 04 — Batch 0005 — Review of the Review Repair Pass

**This pass reviewed the Batch 0005 review repair pass, not the chapters as first written, and it found eight things and fixed all eight. Six are prose, two are records, and the two records are the ones a later phase would have inherited. Continuity items 507 to 516 carry the detail and `reviews/volume-04/batch-0005.md` carries the findings list and the disposition, which is the file that should have existed for the first pass and did not.**

**1. The repair's own central finding had survived in Chapter 200.** Its yard inventory, the last full description of that yard in the volume, still called the man of forty-seven *a road warden of this county's bridge guild on the deck of the first bay with a hod, who has a vote and not an office*, which is Aren Kest's own position and which Chapter 198 spends a paragraph denying in the same yard. **Repaired to the bench of nine and the election and the fourpence out of a private box, and the garbled *who has worked eleven days in a year in which he has not voted once* is cut with it**, along with a second hod-carrier standing beside a man of nineteen who is already carrying one badly. **The lesson is written into the state and it is not a small one: a repair must grep the answer and not the finding, because a finding that has been located in two of three places is a finding that has not been located.**

**2. The wrong description had been written into the Volume 04 close prompt as an instruction.** The promise-by-promise audit told the close to record that *the man who would not have it measured is a road warden with a vote and not an office*, in the same commit in which `state/current.md` told it the opposite. **Repaired to the bench of nine and the witness at that end.** The two *road warden* lines in Chapter 199 are the terms of the refused fourth route and are correct and untouched.

**3. Chapter 199 still had the man saying *I have been in this trade nineteen years*.** The first repair took that claim out of Chapter 198 twice and did not take it out of his own mouth in Chapter 199. **Repaired to *I have stood in yards in this town nineteen years*, which is Chapter 198's claim and is his.** The nineteen years are not cut and Chapter 198's separate line about the trade of pointing is not about his history and stands.

**4. Three device figures in the first repair pass were asserted and not counted, and one of them carried a reason built on an instance that does not exist.** **`about nine + noun` was given three different columns in three files and the page is 1 / 0 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2, which is nine in the block. `the whole of` was given as 2 in four files and the page is 1, and the recorded justification was that *the two that are left are the two that had earned it by being a span of years*, when there is one survivor and it is *the whole of that week's work*. `about` was given as 414 with a per-chapter column wrong in nine of ten positions and a claim that three chapters sat on a ceiling exactly; the page is 404, the highest chapter is 44, and no chapter sits on a ceiling.** All corrected in place in the Block 5 note, in `state/current.md` and in continuity items 505 and 510, and the first repair's own figures are annotated where they stand.

**5. Chapter 196 still went backwards a week.** The tenth-week block had been moved below the ninth and left above the closing paragraph that recaps the ninth, so the chapter narrated the ninth, then the tenth, then the ninth again. **One paragraph moved and not one word of either changed**, the ninth week now closes where it was written and the tenth week is the chapter's last, and the Chapter 196 summary in `state/chapter-summaries.md` — which narrated eighth, tenth, ninth, the pre-repair order — is now in the page's order.

**6. A fourth reading of one moment was still on the page, in Chapter 197, and the device count could not see it.** The step in the cold with a rope-belt on is given three times and all three now read *in about a minute*; the Chapter 197 instance read *in nine seconds* and carried no *about*, so it never entered the `about N seconds` count that was written to catch exactly that. **Repaired, and the standing note is that a device count is only as good as the pattern that matches it.** The one `nine seconds` that stays in the block is Chapter 199's echo of the guild's man with the levels in Chapter 150 and is canon.

**7. Two record defects, one evasion and one broken bold span.** **There was no `reviews/volume-04/batch-0005.md` and no line in `reviews/README.md` for the first pass**, so nine repairs and their reasoning survive only in a ledger written to be read as fact; the file now exists and holds both passes. **Item 502 miscounted its own repair** — it put Chapter 195 among the two chapters that end on a refusal, having just cut that chapter's refusal ending itself, so it is nine of the ten and the one is Chapter 199's; the item is annotated where it stands. **And Chapter 193's *before the middle of it had finished*, put there by the first repair to replace a flagged *in about three seconds*, has no antecedent a reader can hold** — the *it* is a two-word shout — and now reads *before he had finished saying it*, which keeps the beat and says something. **That is the second time a flagged figure has been replaced with an evasion rather than with a different true fact, and the cure for a hedge is a number that is a commitment and not a smaller number wearing a coat.** **And the parity check the block three times over records as returning clean was never run: a whole-manuscript sweep of every line for an odd number of `**` markers returns one line in two hundred chapters, and it is Chapter 200's yard paragraph, which opens the volume's last section and sits two lines above the sentence this pass had just repaired. A stray closing marker with nothing to close is cut, no word of the paragraph changes, and the sweep now returns zero. A check that is recorded as run and reported clean is worth what the run is worth, and the run is four lines of code.**

**The figures, counted and footed on the page before any total is printed.** Batch 0005 is **34,157 words**, per chapter **3306 / 3142 / 3405 / 3287 / 3550 / 3586 / 3249 / 3503 / 3964 / 3165**, and the ten columns foot at 34,157. The five Volume 04 columns are **33,045 · 33,385 · 34,216 · 34,011 · 34,157**, and they foot at **168,814** in fifty chapters, and the manuscript is **270,562 + 184,519 + 146,561 + 168,814 = 770,456** words in two hundred chapters. **The pass added twenty-four words across five chapters. The 34,133 / 168,790 / 770,432 of the first repair and the 33,777 / 168,434 / 770,076 of the writing phase were each true of the page when they were printed and all three sets are left standing as the facts they were; a close that foots a total from this repository must go and look at the page, which is the whole of continuity item 145 and of the Volume 02 close's §10 item 3, and which this pass is the fourth worked example of.**

**The devices and the fillers, whole words and not strings.** `nine` **248** in 34,157 words, **7.26 per 1,000**, per chapter 27 / 27 / 18 / 27 / 25 / 36 / 28 / 15 / 22 / 23, so the volume's six-block rate reads **2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / 11.11 / 5.40 / 7.26**. `about nine + noun` **9**, running 1 / 0 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2 / 0 / 2, a maximum of two against a ceiling of four. `in nine years` **7**, running 0 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 0 / 1 / 0 / 2 / 0, a maximum of two against a ceiling of three. `the whole of` **1**. `about N seconds` **1**. `about` **404**, a highest chapter of 44. `cut` **29** and all twenty-nine ordinary. `---` rules **37**, bolded line-openings **95**, one System panel in two plain lines, zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, zero straight double quotes, zero curly apostrophes. Fillers: **`nodded` 0, `held` 7, `wrote` 25, `stood` 42, `plain` 0, `counted` 16, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0**. A duplicate-paragraph sweep across the ten chapters returns clean, and a per-line parity check of every line in every chapter file in the repository now returns zero, which it did not before this pass and which is finding 7's last half.

**What did not move.** No event, no thread, no character, no date, no calendar, no money figure, no outline, no cast name and no chapter order. Stage 3 is still reached by qualification in Chapters 191 to 195 and by nothing else; the cut is still consented to and unnamed; the form's three questions, the two unreconciled numbers, the tile and the volume's escalation line are all still open and unscheduled; and the two figures of about two hundred people that are not the same are still not reconciled. **No `.done` marker was written, no controller file was touched, no prompt was created, and the only prompt in flight is the Volume 04 close.**

## Volume 04 Close, and the Volume 05 Outline

**No prose was written in this phase. Two documents were created, `outline/volumes/volume-04-close.md` and `outline/volume-05.md`, and four state files were updated, and exactly one prompt was created at `workspace/volume-05/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`.**

**The close.** 31,237 words in twelve sections, modelled on `outline/volumes/volume-03-close.md`. It audits Volume 04 as a finished book, records the state of the world at the close of Chapter 200, audits eighteen promises, and hands twenty-two corrections to Volume 05. **It found six things on the page that the state files did not say**, and the largest is the device ledger: **Batch 0004's `nine` was recorded as 184 at 5.40 per thousand and the page reads 195**, the volume's true rate is **2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / 5.73 / 7.26**, and the batch deviation lists total **107** and not the 98 the close was told. **The antagonist's name was written into the close and then removed from every occurrence before the file was finished**, on the Volume 03 close's standing guard, and the file is committed without it.

**The outline.** 18,472 words in seventeen sections, the same species as Volumes 01 to 04. **The City That Refused Dawn, Chapters 201–250, the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth YR 314 to the twenty-fifth of Goatmonth YR 315, one hundred and fifty days elapsed and one hundred and fifty-one inclusive, and ninety days in the city on its own count, which the ledger states as three months.** The dominant pressure is **character / refusal**, the recovery anchor is **a quiet crossing season in Merefen**, and the relationship work is **learning to accept a refusal, including his own**. **Stage 3 is held and not advanced, no cut is made, and the volume's one act of Stage 3 is a lineage reading that has never been used on the page.** The planned drift is **sixty-nine days of error on ninety days of absence, about three quarters, the first narrowing in this case.**

**Three things a reader of the outline should know before a writer opens Batch 0001.** **First, the series' Volume 5 relationship row is not taken**, and the outline takes the series' own stated milestone instead, and records the substitution. **Second, the series' Volume 5 escalation line names Culling agents and the close ruled that Volume 05 is not the volume that puts a member to it, so the escalation line is not satisfied in full and the pressure arrives without a member, in a form.** **Third, the ledger's two audited weekday chains in this project disagree by one day, and the outline anchors on Volume 04's own last day and records the conflict instead of resolving it.**

**The counts, footed on the page before anything was printed.** Volume 04 columns **33,045 / 33,385 / 34,216 / 34,011 / 34,157**, footing at **168,814**; the manuscript **770,456** in two hundred chapters; `nine` **941** in the volume, per block **95 / 153 / 250 / 195 / 248**; `about` **1,801** in the volume; `the whole of` **1** in the fifth block and **53** in the volume; `---` rules **93**; bolded line-openings **300**; semicolons, em-dashes and straight double quotes **0**; **forty-three market days in Volume 05's hundred and fifty-one and sixty-nine in Volume 04's two hundred and forty, which is what settles the market rule as twice a week.** The planned Volume 05 name budget is **five of six per batch, twenty-four of thirty slots unfilled**, and it is a plan and not a report.

**What did not move.** No chapter. No thread's state. No character's want. No money figure. No date that is already on the page. **The form's three questions, the two unreconciled numbers, the keeper's decision about the three marks, the subscription sentence, the tile, the marginal instruction, the seam and the mechanism are all still open and unscheduled, and the close answered none of them and scheduled none.** No `.done` marker was written, no controller file was touched, and **the only prompt in flight is Volume 05 Batch 0001.**

## Volume 04 Close and Volume 05 Outline — Review Repair Pass

**A review of the close phase and the Volume 05 outline, and of no prose, because no prose exists past Chapter 200. Seven findings, all applied, none requiring a change of plot, and two of which would have put a false fact on the page in Chapters 201 to 210. Continuity items 530 to 536 carry the canon and `reviews/volume-05/outline-phase-0001.md` is the durable findings record.**
