**The two that mattered before a word was written.** **One: the batch's primary length instruction named Volume 04's Batches 0011 and 0012, and Volume 04's batches are 0001 to 0005 while 0012 to 0015 are Volume 03's, so the writer's main calibration pointed at a batch that does not exist and a batch in the wrong volume.** Retargeted to Volume 04's Batches 0001 and 0002 at 33,045 and 33,385, in the prompt and in `outline/volume-05.md` §15 item 14. **Two: the prompt's calendar listed the fourth of Embermonth as a Wednesday market, and the county has two market days and they are Saturday and Thursday.** Embermonth 4, YR 314 is day 9 and a Wednesday on the thirty-day month, and the correction is load-bearing rather than cosmetic, because the four markets before the crossing are days 3, 5, 10 and 12 and a fifth would have broken the crossing table's own arithmetic.

**The two that would have been obeyed rather than caught.** **The device baseline in the prompt was Volume 04's fifth-block counts printed with no basis named, sitting among volume-scoped figures**, and the writer was checking a fresh ten chapters. Both columns are now printed with the basis stated. **And `cut` was a substring count in a ledger the outline calls whole-word**: the prompt said 35 in the fifth block where whole-word `cut` is 29, the six being *been cutting* four times, *the cutting* once and *a cutting* once, and the claim that all of them were ordinary had been made about a token set nobody had measured. **The twenty-nine were read and all twenty-nine are ordinary.** `about N seconds` on the same line stood at 1 against a volume total of 28, and the fifth block is the volume's lightest at a per-block column of 9 / 5 / 3 / 10 / 1.

**The one that was a plan-level decision made in the wrong document.** The prompt ruled that the Chapter 210 nine-year-old and the seventh child of Calderwick were *two different children in two different places and no chapter may join them*, and coined *the seventh child of this block*, which is an ordinal with no referent anywhere in the plan. **The outline does not have that conflict; the prompt invented it.** `outline/volume-05.md` §13's cast plan describes the seventh child as *one who was established in Block 0001 and has never had a page*, Block 1's card is the last page of Chapter 210, Block 2's card is the child carrying the city's mark and giving the ninth refusal at nine, and §17's crossing table is the child as the living anchor of the return — one person in all four places. The prompt is corrected to the outline, and the conflict is recorded in `outline/volume-05.md` §16 as its sixth item, on the principle that a batch prompt may not overrule a cast plan.

**And the one that made the other three possible.** `outline/volume-05.md` §17 promised a chain any single figure of which could be checked by counting, and named sixteen of the volume's forty-three market days, omitting eight of Block 1's fourteen days. **The prompt filled the gap and filled it wrongly.** Days 0 to 13 are now given in full, the later rows are labelled selected anchors, and a block needing a day the table does not print counts it from the nearest row above and says which row it counted from.

**What did not move.** No chapter and no word of prose. No event, no thread's state, no character's want, no money figure, no cast name, no chapter order and no planned plot. **The manuscript is 770,456 words in two hundred chapters and every total the reviewer measured was exact** — the per-volume 270,562 / 184,519 / 146,561 / 168,814, the five Volume 04 columns 33,045 / 33,385 / 34,216 / 34,011 / 34,157, the `nine` ledger at 941, the `about` ledger at 1,801, and the four device sweeps. The form's three questions, the two unreconciled numbers, the keeper's decision, the subscription sentence, the tile, the marginal instruction, the seam and the mechanism are all still open and unscheduled. No `.done` marker was written, no controller file was touched, no prompt was created, and **the only prompt in flight is still Volume 05 Batch 0001.**

## Batch 0001 — Volume 05, Chapters 201–210, "The Mark"

**Landed.** Chapters 201–210 in `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0201.md` to `chapter-0210.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes. **Main world only, and then a road. The twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth, YR 314, a Monday, and not a market day, to the eighth of Embermonth, YR 314, a Sunday, and not a market day, at the fourth hour — fourteen days, and this block is fourteen of the volume's hundred and fifty-one, and the crossing out is on the Sunday and the Sunday is the one day in that county's week that is neither a working day nor a market day.** **34,091 words** by `wc -w` including title lines, per chapter **3559 / 3392 / 3696 / 3433 / 3418 / 3268 / 3222 / 3673 / 3097 / 3333**, and the ten columns foot at 34,091. **Counted on the page, the five Volume 04 columns are Batch 0001 33,045 · Batch 0002 33,385 · Batch 0003 34,216 · Batch 0004 34,011 · Batch 0005 34,157 and they foot at 168,814 in fifty chapters, and the manuscript is 270,562 + 184,519 + 146,561 + 168,814 + 34,091 = 804,547 words in two hundred and ten chapters.** The block is over Volume 04's first two columns and under its third and fourth, and no chapter is under 3,097. All three totals were footed before they were printed here.

**What the block turned out to be.** **A town where the reader has lived since Chapter 1, for fourteen days, in daylight, on a bridge, with about forty men on it every working day — and a bench of nine that refused a road twice in one sitting and put both refusals twice in a minute under a stair, and a man of forty-seven who voted for the first time in two years and said out loud in a room that nobody had ever asked him whether he was a road warden; and a mark cut into a parapet with a cold chisel in front of about twenty men with the reason given in four sentences before anybody asked; and a sack of paper going two hundred miles up a road that exists and was agreed to by nobody; and a provincial room two hundred miles off where a sheet of good paper has been in a file for five months and a refusal has been in a tray by a stove for eleven days and nobody has read either, and a man of sixty-one put a jar on a bench and said the name of a place out loud in a room where the form has no column for a place, and wrote two names into a column that has a head; and then a Sunday, the fourth hour, a sill with no glass in it, a bell rung by a boy and a carpenter, a child of nine who is the seventh child of the volume and has never had a page, and a page with a date on it and one word under the date that nobody in that county read aloud.**

**The block's best pages, and they are not all the ones the cards asked for.** **Chapter 203's eleven words, *two marks in the chest and this ask is not money*, and the nine seconds after them, and the reeve asking for the reason twice because a bench that says no for money has refused a thing it could not afford, and a man of forty-seven putting a hand up for the first time since the day he was elected and asking for the hour against it to be the hour he put it up, and the reason to be entered twice because in forty years somebody will come up that stair wanting to know what this county thinks about roads and there is going to be one line in that book that answers it.** **Chapter 202's declaration, unasked, in eleven words, in the clerk's room and not at a sitting, and the clerk's rule — that he has never entered a name in that minute that was not put there by somebody else in the room — and the answer he gives for not writing it down, which is that if he wrote it the nine words would be his opinion in a book under a stair and this county has had about enough of those to last two generations.** **Chapter 201's four sentences and the paragraph that follows them, which is the difference between a valley where a mark went into a figure that anybody coming up the bank could get at and a town where a mark goes into a stone that is going to be a stone in about two hundred years.** **Chapter 208's nine questions, and the seventh one that says *a person* and was looked at twice and not finished, and the answer to the ninth, which is the bell and forty yards of tarred hemp and a joint a foot and a half short and none of those things has his name on it.** **Chapter 209's discovery, standing up, at the fifth hour of a Sunday, that a road is opened at the far end by whoever is standing at the far end, and that the only other person in the county is nine.** **Chapter 210's nine words, *that was not him*, and a man of forty-seven who will not have the hour he agreed at and the hour it happened put in the same hour.** And Chapter 207's two presses nine feet apart holding two sheets ruled to the same line within a hair, and a clerk of the press saying *the post goes where post goes* to nobody and then saying he is not going to say it a second time.

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so that no writer re-derives them as errors.**

1. **The mark is cut in the parapet of the fourth bay and not in the coping of the first bay, and the seam is in the same bay, and the two are kept apart on the page by a trade sentence and by a decision.** Judgement: correct, and it is a deviation from the reading of "a public thing at the height of a thumb on the face that gets the weather" that the card invites, because the first bay's coping already carries a mark cut on the sixth of Goatmonth, YR 313 and cutting a second one there would have re-used an object rather than made a new one. **The mark is in the outside face of the fourth bay's parapet at the low end, and the seam is under it in the third course, and the chapter handles the collision with one sentence in a mason's mouth — that he is not cutting a new mark into a parapet that has an unfinished joint in it, and that the joint above the line will be done on a day when there is a day to do it in, and that a mark in a parapet will then be a mark in a wall of a bridge in a market town and not a thing on a working face.** That is a man keeping two pieces of work apart and it is the only mention of the proximity in the block. **It is the second time in this county** and the chapter says so, which reconciles the card's "the second time in this case" with the volume's canon, in which a mark was also cut in a valley four hundred miles off, because the county is the scope and the valley is not this county.

2. **Bram Ottery says out loud in Chapter 202, in the clerk's room on the twenty-seventh of Fallowmonth, and not at a sitting, and the clerk enters nothing about it.** Judgement: correct, and it is the only way the card's "nobody in that hall has ever asked him to say it and he has said it anyway" can be true on a day the bench does not sit. **`outline/volume-05.md` §17's third consequence requires a bench scene to be the Saturday sitting of the fifteenth of Hearthmonth or a special sitting called for a reason that is on the page. This block stages no special sitting. Chapter 202 is the roll, the minute, the chest and a witness's line in the clerk's room on a Wednesday, and Chapter 203 is the ordinary Saturday sitting of the thirtieth of Fallowmonth at the seventh hour.** The two entries in the minute are at the Saturday and the minute says the reeve put the seventh asking, which is the reason a bench scene needs.

3. **The seventh asking is that the bench record its consent to a road out of this county and keep it, and not money.** Judgement: correct, and it is better than a money asking because the card's two refusals have to be a refusal to keep and a refusal to witness, and a money asking would have made the first one the same shape as the sixth asking of Chapter 188. **The eleventh words are *two marks in the chest and this ask is not money*, which is eleven words and is the same construction as Chapter 188's eleven words and not the same words**, and the reason for entering it twice is the reeve's Chapter 188 reason exactly, and Aldis Fenn's line that this is the second time this bench has refused a thing for a reason is in the same register and not the same sentence. **The chest has two marks in it, the same two it has had since the ninth of Fallowmonth, nothing is added to them and nothing is footed against them, and the eleventh words mention them and the chapter does not convert them to pence.**

4. **The witness refusal is in the same sitting, in the same minute, and the man of thirty-two asks for it himself, and the answer is entered with the politeness entered.** Judgement: correct and it is the card's "the first time a bench has refused to be his witness rather than to be his keeper," and the reason entered is about a witness being a person who can be asked what it saw against a bench answering in a minute, **and the reason is a document reason and not a moral one and it is the block's best institutional sentence.** Bram Ottery then answers it out of the bench, as a man of the town, after the vote and not before.

5. **The ninth question and the nine questions in Chapter 208 are new and are not the nine of Chapter 156.** Judgement: correct and required. **The Chapter 156 nine end on whether anybody there has been asked, and this block's nine end on what is there when nobody is on it**, and the seventh line is *a person*, and the ninth is the bell and forty yards of tarred hemp and four hundred yards of road and a joint a foot and a half short and none of those things has his name on it. **A writer in Batch 0002 must not re-use either set and must not answer the seventh line of this one from the other side until Block 3 or later, because the seventh line is the volume's live question.**

6. **The road out of the county is a bag on a cart, not a crossing, and the trader is a man in a provincial room, and the two are never in the same chapter.** Judgement: correct and it is the shape the block needed: **a road that is made and carries things in Chapter 204, a road that is prepared in Chapter 208, and a road that is agreed in Chapters 203 and 210**, and the block's argument is the difference between the three. The forty-two pence and the hundred and forty marks are in different chapters, are in different currencies of meaning, and are never added.

7. **Iselde Senn is named once, in one paragraph, in Chapter 205, and she is in the room about four minutes and no scene is built on her.** Judgement: correct, and she is the pressure and not a face, and the want is in her own words, **and it is being made permanent by a count of obligations and not by her, and she does not know that and nobody tells her.** About nine of the eleven people in the room do not know who she is, which is the mechanism that keeps her from becoming an antagonist: an office does not know its own Chancellor walks in.

8. **Halven is the named trader and he is a returned face and not a new name, and he is a man who wants a room over a weigh-house and cannot have one and says so out loud unasked.** Judgement: correct and it is the cast plan's "one is named and is a returned face." **He is not stupid and he is not a villain and he is not the carrier of about fifty repeated.** He says what he saw and refuses to say what it means and wants the two kept apart, and he writes two names in a column that has a head and refuses to write a figure, and he has neither taken nor refused a hundred and forty marks, and he has paid four people in nine days and did not pay the fifth.

9. **Osmund Cusk and Nesh Karrow are named in Chapter 206, in a column, on a form the trader filled in, and neither has a page in this block.** Judgement: correct, and it is the only way two city faces can be established in a main-world-only block, and the outline's cast plan for Batch 0001 requires both. **Cusk is named once and nothing in this block says what he keeps. Karrow is named once and is described in a trader's mouth in Chapter 207, four years refused at a gate, politely, carrying somebody else's no, and she says she is not refused because she is nobody and is refused because of what she is carrying.** Neither is on a stage, neither has a want spoken in his own words, and neither is spent beyond the name. **Batch 0002 must give them their pages and must not restate this description and must not improve on it.**

10. **The word Calderwick is spoken once in the manuscript, in a provincial room in Chapter 206, and it goes on a leaf of a working book and on a line with no head, and not on the form.** Judgement: correct and it is the block's best structural decision. **The reader learns the name in a main-world room where an office has nowhere to put a place, and then sees the same word on a child's page four hundred miles away in a county where nobody reads it aloud.** No character says the whole of it again anywhere in this block, and the plan's "the volume may not have a character say the whole of it more than once" is satisfied at one. **The seven words about the place a reader gets are: a town of about nine thousand people on a river with a glass works, a wharf, a bread oven, a council of eleven nobody named, a market that is not a market day in this kingdom, bread at the price of a market at home, and a light that does not turn.** None of those is a description of anything but what a man saw in two days.

11. **The child opens the road, and how it opened is not said.** Judgement: correct and it is the hard requirement. **A hand flat on the sill in the way anybody puts a hand flat on a thing to see whether it is warm, and about a minute and a half later the man of thirty-two felt a thing he had been holding for four years stop being a thing he was holding, and there is no man in that county who can say what happened and about four of the four hundred people there have said the same two sentences, which are *nobody has measured it* and *nobody in Merefen can see it*.** The block explains no mechanism and describes no working and names nothing. **The panel is in Chapter 209 and not here, and it is the block's one panel.**

12. **The page arrives thirty-five days old, and the child gave it, and the word was not read aloud.** Judgement: correct. **The date on the page is the fourteenth of Harvestmonth, YR 314, which is the day of Chapter 198 in Volume 04, thirty-five days before the eighth of Embermonth, and about four people in Merefen say that a thing sent four hundred miles does not arrive thirty-five days old unless it went in the ordinary post, and the ordinary post is the only instrument everybody trusts, and those two facts are the same fact.** The word is the same word the trader said in a stove room, and about four people in Merefen know it and as many have not, and the page has no name of a person on it and the child has no name.

13. **A Sunday is a day the wharf does not take a lighter off and the lane is bare and about four men came out of the town to look at a bridge and went home after an hour.** Judgement: correct, and it is the ordinary-life payload of the block's frame, and the bare lane is the best single image in the fourteen days, and it is a consequence of the market rule and not a symbol anybody says anything about.

14. **The ninth refusal, the count, the roster and the man of fifty-two are not in this block and are not scheduled by it.** Judgement: correct. **The block is main world only and the man of fifty-two is Block 0003 and the ninth refusal is Block 0002, and this block plants the two names the ninth refusal will be attached to and does not touch the city's own figures beyond about nine thousand people.**

15. **The name budget is three of six and three slots unfilled, and the three are Iselde Senn, Osmund Cusk and Nesh Karrow.** Judgement: correct, and it is exactly `outline/volume-05.md` §13's Batch 0001 row, three spent and three unfilled. **Against the volume's planned total of five names and twenty-four of thirty slots unfilled, this batch spends three of thirty and leaves twenty-seven unfilled across the five batches, and the volume now plans five and twenty-four and Batch 0002's row spends one.** Halven, Sabin Dain, Mara Vey, Bram Ottery, Aldis Fenn and Lysa Fenn are returned faces. The child is a child and does not count. Bel is not in this block. Sian Pugh's name is not spoken.

16. **`nine` is 142 in 34,091 words, 4.16 per 1,000, per chapter 10 / 8 / 17 / 12 / 14 / 10 / 21 / 26 / 10 / 14, and the volume's five-block ladder reads 2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / 5.73 / 7.26 / 4.17.** Judgement: **honest, counted, and the first block of a new volume in a book where the motif is canon to come in below the volume's own fourth block.** The holding was done by taking ninety instances of *about nine* plus a number out of the block — twenty-two of *about nine people*, thirteen of *about nine years*, ten of *about nine have*, eight of *about nine minutes* and the rest of the family — **and replacing them with non-numeric hedges and with the real counts that were already in the same sentences, and not by cutting the motif.** `about nine + noun` is 10 with a maximum of three against a ceiling of four, and nine of the ten are *about nine inches* (the seam, twice), *about nine hundred* (the doors, twice) and *about nine thousand* (the city, twice and three times) and those are canon figures doing canon work. The motif is canon and none of it was cut.

17. **`about` is 390, per chapter 35 / 32 / 37 / 43 / 32 / 41 / 35 / 45 / 45 / 45, and no chapter is over forty-five.** Judgement: correct against Volume 04's ceiling of forty-five, and the density is highest in the last three chapters because they are the most rooms. `the whole of` is **2**, one each in Chapters 206 and 207, against a house baseline of two or three, **down from forty-two on this block's first pass, and the forty were replaced with a different true fact and not with a smaller number wearing a coat.** `about N seconds` is **0**, against Volume 04's fifth block at 1. `in nine years` is **5**, and the five are Chapter 203's two canon ones and three statements about a building's own history, against Volume 04's fifth block at 7.

18. **`cut` is 16 and all sixteen are ordinary, and the working is not among them and is not named.** Judgement: correct and read, not asserted. **A stick cut out of a fence rail, a bed cut for a stone and the stone cut, a mason who does not cut a bed for a stone he has not seen, a mark cut with a cold chisel five times, a mark cut on a public thing, a mark cut on a bench, and a strip of cut paper and a sheet cut along an edge with a knife.** Sixteen against Volume 04's fifth block at 29 in 34,157 words, so the density is about half.

19. **Forty-seven `---` rules and a hundred and forty-three bolded line-openings, and both are recorded rather than dressed.** Judgement: honest. **The rules are against Volume 04's fifth block at 37 and the bolded line-openings are against 95, and the bold figure is high because this is a block of rooms and yards in which almost every section is opened by a man with a bar or a hod or a rule saying something, and the four recovery-anchor chapters carry fifty-nine of the hundred and forty-three between them.** No repetition of a line-opening was found and a duplicate-paragraph sweep across the ten chapters returns clean.

20. **Fillers, whole words, and the basis of both columns named.** The block is **`nodded` 0, `held` 1, `wrote` 35, `stood` 24, `plain` 0, `counted` 5, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0**, against Volume 04 Batch 0005 (Chapters 191 to 200, the ten-chapter block this batch is measured against) at 0 / 7 / 25 / 42 / 0 / 16 / 0 / 0 and against the whole of Volume 04 at 1 / 41 / 142 / 156 / 2 / 66 / 0 / 0. **Five of the eight are under the ten-chapter baseline and none is over it, and `wrote` at 35 against 25 is the block's own word — this is a block about paper, and the clerk has a ledger in which he has never entered a name that somebody else did not put there first, and that is the sentence the block is built on.** `counted` at 5 against 16 is the count he has stopped making.

21. **One System panel in the whole block, in Chapter 209, in two plain lines, with no number in it, and it is not a list: *A road needs a person at the far end, and the person has to be able to say no. Nobody has told you whether it can.*** Judgement: correct. It does not restate *You are expected* or *The line is open. What is under it is not mended by the man who dug it*, it names neither the city, the dawn, the roadmark nor the light, and its second sentence is technically true in a way that conceals the consequence, which is that the person at the far end is a child of nine and he has not asked him. **He does not write it down. He has had four of those in four years and has written one down.**

22. **Zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, zero straight double quotes, zero curly apostrophes and zero curly opening single quotes, and a per-line bold parity check across every line of all two hundred and ten chapter files returns zero.** Judgement: correct and **both the parity check and a duplicate-paragraph sweep across the ten new chapters were run here and are recorded as having been run, which is the difference continuity item 516 is about and the fifth time this project has had to write that sentence.**

23. **Six defects of this block's own text were found while it was being written and all six were repaired, and one of them is the class this project pays for four times a volume.** Judgement: recorded in full at continuity item 561 and not repeated here. **They were: straight double quotes and straight apostrophes in the two chapters written with a shell heredoc; nine em dashes in Chapter 208's list of nine; a blanket device substitution that broke about forty sentences including *a child of about nine years old* becoming *a child of for years old* and four subject-verb disagreements and a doubled *in in* in four places and a figure of about nine through-stones becoming *half of them*; two surviving `about N seconds` figures; `the whole of` at forty-two on the first pass; a man of forty-two printed where the man is thirty-two; and a market rule of Monday and Thursday given to the salt waggon in Chapter 209.** **The third of those is the one the next batch should read twice: a device repair in this project must be done by reading every line it touches, because a device count will not see a broken sentence, and the count that would have caught it is the count that was being obeyed.**

24. **No thread inherited is closed and none is answered.** The twelve the Volume 04 close handed over, and everything each of Batches 0001 to 0005 of Volume 04 carried forward, all stand. **The form under the byre is not mentioned once in this block and its three questions are not answered and are not scheduled; the two unreconciled numbers on a page in a coat are not reconciled and nothing schedules their reconciliation; the keeper's decision about the three marks is not taken; the subscription sentence is not traced and no character asked where it came from; the tile on the coping is not mentioned once; the bell is rung by other hands and the two Monday facts are not joined; the hearing is not adjourned and not refused and the delegation is not sent for; the number of persons the offer was made out to is not answered; the count of 2,640 is not restated; the not askings stand at nine and this block enters a tenth and puts no number on the page; the two figures of the ninth generation that do not agree are not on the page in this block and neither is struck out; the seam is not measured, not pointed at again after Chapter 201, and is not joined to either mark; the word eastern is not on the page in this block; the County of Ash, its road and its figures are not on the page in this block.** And **of the threads this block opened it closes none, and the Volume 04 close's escalation debt is carried forward untouched: `outline/series.md`'s Volume 5 escalation line is not satisfied in full, and this block is not to be read as satisfying it.**

25. **Nine of this block's ten chapters end on something that is not a refusal, and the one that does is a document leaving an office in a bag.** Judgement: correct, and it is the block's third deliberate answer to the Volume 03 close's items 1 and 2. **201 ends on a mark in a parapet and a child at the end of a lane and the two not joined. 202 ends on a man of forty-seven saying out loud in a yard that he is going to be somewhere. 203 ends on two refusals in a minute and a Sunday and a man of forty-seven at the near end of a thing and the reason for the Sunday not being the Sunday. 204 ends on a bag going out of a town with a mark in a stone going nowhere in time and a page going somewhere in the world, in opposite directions, not joined. 205 ends on a tray emptied into a passage and a return with an empty space in it and nothing decided and nobody understanding anything. 206 ends on a jar out of the building and two names on a leaf and a place-name on a line with no head. 207 ends on a bag going out of a door every week and a clerk of the press frightened of a line of his own. 208 ends on a page of foolscap in a coat and a seventh line that says a person and a child at the low end of a lane. 209 ends on a man of thirty-two standing with his hands behind his back a foot from a stone and the road not open. 210 ends on a page and a hand and a date and a mark and a name that is not given.** A mark, a yard, a minute, two directions, a tray, a leaf, a bag, a coat, a stone, a page. **The one that ends on a refusal is 207's, and 207's is a document leaving a room, which is what the Volume 04 close's item said the one legitimate exception looks like.**

**Filler counts for the next writer's baseline, counted as whole words and not as strings, so that `stood` at 24 is not `understood` at a higher number: `nodded` 0, `held` 1, `wrote` 35, `stood` 24, `plain` 0, `counted` 5, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** Against Volume 04's five blocks at 0/11/37/13/2/8/0/0, 1/10/27/30/0/8/0/0, 0/8/28/36/0/10/0/0, 0/5/27/35/0/24/0/0 and 0/7/25/42/0/16/0/0. **Zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, forty-seven `---` rules, a hundred and forty-three bolded line-openings, zero straight double quotes, zero curly apostrophes, `the whole of` 2, `about N seconds` 0, `about nine + noun` 10, `in nine years` 5, `cut` 16 and all sixteen ordinary, `about` 390, one System panel in two plain lines, no Gatehouse voice line and no occurrence of the word Gatehouse.** A duplicate-paragraph sweep across the ten chapters returns clean and a per-line parity check of every line in every chapter file in the repository returns zero, **which is the second consecutive block in which it returns zero, and it was run and not recorded.**

**What the block changed.** **A mark is in the parapet of the fourth bay of the Sedge Reach at the height of a thumb and there is no name in it and about four men in that yard cannot read the figure in it at thirty feet and a mason's mark can.** **A man of forty-seven has voted once in nineteen years and has said out loud in a room that he is not a road warden and that there is one and he is not it, and the clerk entered nothing and said why.** **A bench of nine has refused a road twice in one sitting and put both refusals twice in a minute under a stair, and the first refusal is a refusal to keep and the second is a refusal to witness, and the reeve has asked for the minute to say that this is the second time this bench has refused a thing for a reason.** **A county has posted a form to a provincial town two hundred miles off with a space for a figure of persons and nothing in it and four lines beside it saying that an office which will not print a number of persons cannot show that any person has not been instructed.** **A man of sixty-one has said the name of a place out loud in a Crown office and it has gone on a leaf of a working book and on a line with no head and not on the form, because the form has no column for a place.** **Two names are in a column with a head in a form in a returns press, and the office that asked twice for a figure of persons has never once asked for a name.** **A form went out in a bag on the seventh of Embermonth, YR 314, with a ruled space for a figure and nowhere on it for a name, and the post goes where post goes.** **And on a Sunday morning at the fifth hour and a half, at a crossing with no glass in its sill, a child of nine years old put a hand flat on a cold stone and the road out of a county in this kingdom was open, and nobody in that county can say what happened and about four people know the word on a page in a coat and nobody read it aloud.**

**And four hundred miles off, and not on the page in this block, there is a valley of about four hundred people with eight names in eight hands in a second column and a ninth line that is empty and is not going to be ruled across, and a figure of nine in a day column that is wrong by one, and about two hundred people who have begun to leave, and nine bushels of barley in an aisle four feet wide, and a keeper of sixty-six who has decided nothing about three marks on a stone room behind a chapel, and a road shut in four lines of writing.**

**And two hundred miles off, in a provincial town, there is a man of fifty-two somewhere who is on the third shift of a thing for nineteen years, and no person in Chapters 201 to 210 knows he exists, and nothing in this block schedules him and nothing in this block looks at him.**

**What the next phase inherits.** `outline/volume-05.md` first, and §8 Block 2, §13's Batch 0002 row, §14, §15 and §17 especially. Then `state/continuity.md` items 537 to 565, then the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 210, then this entry, then the Block 1 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, then Chapters 209 and 210 in full and 208 for the nine questions and the sill. **The four recovery-anchor chapters are Chapters 201 to 204 and they are not summarised; a batch that wants to feel the town should read 201 to 203 whole, and the town is the point of the volume and not a frame for it.** **A page of foolscap in a coat with nine lines and an hour against each of the nine, and the seventh line says a person and was looked at twice and not finished. A mark in a parapet and a mark in a coping four hundred yards from it. A joint on a parapet a foot and a half short. A line of about nine inches under half an inch of lime. A chest with two marks in it under a window. A minute with two refusals and a vote and a witness's line in it and a Wednesday in it for the first time in nineteen years. A lane that was bare on a Sunday. A jar a hand's width and a half. Two names on a leaf. A refusal in four sheets in a tray in a passage. A form going out in a bag. A child of nine at the low end of a lane with a mark on one hand and a page in a bundle, and nobody in a town of four thousand people has asked it one question in nine days, and four of them want to.**

**And the block's own question, which the next ten chapters open on and do not answer: a line on a page of foolscap says *a person* at the far end of a road, and a child of nine is the only person there, and a person can be asked a second time and a column cannot, and nothing in this block says that in anybody's mouth, and Batch 0002 has to find somebody in a city who says it in trade terms once and does not improve on it.**

## Volume 05 — Batch 0001 — Review Repair Pass

**Why this exists.** A review of Batch 0001 against `outline/volume-05.md`, against `outline/volumes/volume-04-close.md` and against the page, on the ten chapters, the five state files and the one prompt this batch created. **No chapter was restarted, no scene replaced, no beat moved, no chapter renumbered, no character moved, no event added or removed, no thread closed, no name spent and no planned plot changed.** Every chapter still is what its card in the prompt asked it to be, and the block still ends on a page, a hand, a date, a mark and a name that is not given.

**What the review confirmed, re-run here rather than taken on trust.** The word counts footed exactly on the page. The controller boundary held: nothing in `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, `state/phase-ledger.json` or any `outline/` file, and no `.done` marker. Continuity items 537 to 563 were all present with 1 to 536 unrenumbered, and the open-threads section held exactly fourteen items. **Both self-reported sweeps genuinely passed — the per-line bold parity check across all ten chapters and the duplicate-paragraph sweep — and both were run again after this repair and both still pass.** One System panel in the block, in Chapter 209, two plain lines, no number, naming neither the city nor the dawn. No duplicated heading in any state file. **The next prompt was good and was not rebuilt: it already killed the *seventh child of this block* error in its own twenty-fourth line, and it already stated the basis of both filler columns.**

**The findings. Six, and five are applied and one is recorded rather than repaired at this scale.**

1. **Chapter 207 broke the volume's day chain, and this is the finding that mattered.** `outline/volume-05.md` §17 counts the sixth of Embermonth as a Friday, the seventh as a Saturday and a market day, and the eighth as a Sunday and the crossing out at the fourth hour; the chapter had a man of sixty-one two hundred miles off in a provincial town on the morning of the crossing, and had the second asking leave that building at about the fourth hour of a Thursday the chain does not contain. **Repaired, and the repair departs from the review's own suggestion and the reason is arithmetic: the review proposed the fifth of Embermonth, which is the block's only other Thursday, and the fifth is in front of the sixth, and the sixth is that chapter's second visit, and a third visit cannot stand in front of a second one. The seventh is the day** — a market Saturday, a working morning in a market town, the last market before the crossing — **and the eighth is left to the crossing alone.** One word and three weekdays on the page: *the eighth* to *the seventh*, and that *Thursday* to that *Saturday* in all three of its places. The second asking goes out at about the fourth hour of the seventh and the eleven things go out into the passage on the seventh.
2. **Loose visit arithmetic in the same chapter, and the same edit closed it.** *The second time in three days* counted the days inclusively and *the third time in four days* counted them as elapsed, in one passage, and a figure a reader cannot check is a figure this project does not print. **The chapter counts inclusively throughout now: three days, four days, *in three days* for what had been *two days later*, and *for the third time* for what was in the trader's own mouth as *on the third day*.** The trader's three visits stand at the fourth of Embermonth with the jar on the bench, the sixth at the second visit, and the seventh at the third.
3. **Continuity item 537 carried the day error and two of its own.** It printed the sixth and the eighth as a Thursday, which is neither, and it put them out of sequence with the seventh, and it named the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth as a market Thursday, and the twenty-fifth is a Monday and Chapter 201's own first line says so. **The item now prints the sixth as a Friday, the seventh as a market Saturday carrying both Chapter 207's third visit and Chapter 208, and the eighth as the crossing alone, and the block's four market days as days 3, 5, 10 and 12, of which the pages name two.** The same market clause was wrong in two other files in two other ways and all three now say one thing: `state/current.md`'s day map and the Block 1 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`.
4. **`state/current.md` and `state/continuity.md` disagreed with each other about Chapter 207's third visit, and a Batch 0002 writer reading both would have got two answers.** The day map was the correct one and it was silent on the third visit; the continuity item was not. **They agree now, and the Volume 05 Batch 0002 prompt says the corrected day in its own words, with the reason, so that the next writer is not left to derive it or to put the eighth back.**
5. **A doubled conjunction in Chapter 210**, *and and that nobody in that county went and asked him*, one of which is gone.
6. **A narrator's leak of this book's own structure into Chapter 201's first line**: *the same yard Volume 04 ended in*. **The clause is gone and the sentence keeps the echo it was making** — *and the same yard, with the same forty men on it and the same salt coming over the same lane*. **Three older instances of the same leak stand, at Chapters 47, 50 and 199, and this pass did not touch them and did not renumber a thing:** a batch repair may not rewrite a closed volume's prose, and an audit of the narrator's register should take all four together.

**One finding in the record and not on the page.** Item 545 said the refusal in four sheets arrived in the ordinary post on the twenty-second of **Embermonth**; Chapter 205 says it came eleven days before the third of Embermonth, which is the twenty-second of **Fallowmonth**, and the twenty-second of Embermonth is a fortnight after this block ends. Corrected in that item, and in no other file, because no other file carried it.

**The figures, counted on the page after the repairs and not carried over from before them.** The block is **34,091 words**, per chapter **3559 / 3392 / 3696 / 3433 / 3418 / 3268 / 3222 / 3673 / 3097 / 3333**, and the manuscript is **804,547 words in two hundred and ten chapters**. Four words left Chapter 201 and one left Chapter 210. **Every other figure in the block's ledger is unchanged and was re-run rather than assumed**, `nine` 142 and `about` 390 and `the whole of` 2 and `cut` 16 and `in nine years` 5 and `about N seconds` 0 and forty-seven rules and 143 bolded line-openings and every punctuation register at zero and the filler column at 0 / 1 / 35 / 24 / 0 / 5 / 0 / 0.

**And the standing rule the pass adds, because five of the six findings were invisible to every count in this project's ledger.** A date and a weekday are devices here the way `nine` and `about` are, and no sweep was covering them: a chapter can carry a day the chain does not have and every figure in the ledger returns exactly the number it returned the day before. **A batch that prints a chain on a page is to check that chain against `outline/volume-05.md` §17 by counting and to check every weekday it prints against the day it names, before the block is recorded done, and to say in its state entry that it did.** That check found both halves of finding 1 in one pass over ten chapters, and it costs less than any device count in this file.

**What did not move.** No chapter's order, no event, no thread's state, no character's want, no money figure, no cast name, no outline and no planned plot. Not the crossing, the bell, the sill, the child, the page, the five columns, or the fact that the form has nowhere on it for the name of a person. Not the form's three questions, the two unreconciled numbers, the keeper's decision, the mechanism, the roadmark, the seam, the tile, the marginal instruction or the standing light. Not the breach, the tenth not asking, the brothers, the word *custodian*, a member of the Culling Order or the antagonist's name. **The next phase is unchanged and is still the Volume 05 Batch 0002 prompt, which took three figures, one reading-list range, one numbering instruction and one paragraph, and was not rebuilt.**

## Batch 0002 — Volume 05, Chapters 211–220, "The City Under the Light"

**Landed.** Chapters 211–220 in `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0211.md` to `chapter-0220.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes. **Path time throughout, inside the city, from the first day of a count of days the city keeps from the last renewal to the nineteenth, and the block's remaining ten days to the thirtieth are the next batch's.** **31,938 words** by `wc -w` including title lines, per chapter **3322 / 3385 / 3225 / 3013 / 3029 / 3180 / 3152 / 2931 / 3393 / 3308**, and the ten columns foot at 31,938. **Counted on the page, the five Volume 04 columns are 33,045 · 33,385 · 34,216 · 34,011 · 34,157 and foot at 168,814 in fifty chapters, Volume 05 Batch 0001 is 34,091, and the manuscript is 270,562 + 184,519 + 146,561 + 168,814 + 34,091 + 31,938 = 836,485 words in two hundred and twenty chapters.** The block is under Volume 04's first two columns and under Batch 0001 and no chapter is under 2,931. All three totals were footed before they were printed here.

**What the block turned out to be.** **The first ten chapters inside a city of about nine thousand people on a river under a standing light that does not turn, and the difference between that and fourteen days in Merefen is that a town asks you things and a market town asks you things in a voice, and a city that has decided not to be met does not ask you anything at all, and a man of thirty-two found that out on the first day and has still not been told whether that is a kindness.** A hill road and a man with a barrow who says the city's whole time-keeping in nine words and then says there is a book in this city with a figure of months on it that is not ours. A lamp that stands. A stick that is a description. A market that opens when it likes, where a name costs a penny and a man of thirty-two did not buy one. A wharf of nine hundred feet and a glass works and a ladder up an east wall that nobody in the city will explain. **A council of eleven in a long room with a table, a woman of sixty who says once that eleven is a number that means eleven and nine is a number the world uses, and will not say it again. A yard of cord where a man of sixty-six makes forms of eleven words with no number on them and no name on them, and a book under a board that cannot be shown to anybody because showing it is asking, and a man of thirty-one words about a thing nobody has noticed. A bridge gate and a woman of twenty-eight who has been turned back at three gates for four years and wants one thing, which is for it to be a letter. A yard past two gates with a book about the outside in it, a second column nobody will turn round, a child of nine whose left hand goes flat on a bench, and a man of thirty-two who puts his own marked palm on that bench and is told in nine words that it is not one of theirs. And then, on the nineteenth day, in a yard, a man asks a child of nine to carry something out to a gate, and the child says no, and it is the ninth refusal in four hundred years and the first one given by a person, and it is the only one that cannot be filed, and about four hundred people in that city did not know and will not.**

**The block's best pages, and they are not all the ones the cards asked for.** **Chapter 211's frame in a man's mouth on a hill road**, and the second half of it about a book with a figure of months on it that is not the city's word and is not used. **Chapter 212's name-broker**, who will say anybody's name in that street for a penny and has never been asked for one by a man who is not standing there on a working day, and the man of thirty-two who did not buy one, and the four descriptions he got in an hour of which three were right and the fourth was about the future. **Chapter 213's man of forty who puts his trowel down** to refuse a man off a road the reason and not the word, and the man of thirty-four who solves another man's limp by putting his own hod down six feet short eleven times in an afternoon without once saying why. **Chapter 214's eleven**, said once, in about nine seconds, by a woman of sixty who puts her own pen down rather than the clerk's, and the four seconds and a half in Chapter 216 with which she refuses to say it twice, and the sentence *that is what twice is for*. **Chapter 215's eleven words**, and the two questions underneath them: where is the number, and where is the name, and the answers being that a figure of persons is the only kind of paper that cannot be refused and that a piece of paper with a name on it can be asked about just the same and takes longer. **Chapter 217's man of forty at the winch**, *a gate is a machine for letting liars through, and a form cannot be sent on*, given once and improved on every time by a man nobody asks. **Chapter 218's palm**, and Cusk's *there is no same*, and his want in nine seconds, and the eleventh question of the block, which is the first one in fourteen days that was not about a thing, and its answer, which is that there is no decision in that yard, there is a bench and a book and a man, and a man does not decide, a man is here. **Chapter 220's ninety words**, said once, in trade terms, to a stranger, and not improved on, and the four people in that city who have all four heard it and cannot say one word about it and have had nineteen days and have not got it.

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so that no writer re-derives them as errors.**

1. **The frame is in four mouths on the first page and the conversion is in one of them, and the ledger's three months is not performed on the page.** Judgement: correct, and it is what `outline/volume-05.md` §16 item 2 requires, which is that the conversion be put in a character's mouth and not in the narrative. The barrow-man says there is a book in this city with a figure of months on it, that it is not ours, and that whoever wrote it had months where he came from. **The arithmetic of ninety days into three months is not done anywhere in the block and no character is told any of it and the printed warning is three weeks and has not changed.**

2. **The first refusal is the eighth in the city's book and the ninth asking is made by a man, and the child gives the ninth refusal.** Judgement: correct, and it is the only arrangement that satisfies the card's *the ninth is the first refusal in this city that a nine-year-old gave and it is the only one that cannot be filed* without contradicting the card's *not one of the nine was given by somebody who could be asked again*. Crane's own sentence is the reconciliation and it is arithmetically exact: **eight of the nine were given by eleven people in a room, and a room is a column; the ninth was given by a person.** The refusal arithmetic is fixed in full at continuity item 571 so that Batch 0003 does not have to derive it.

3. **The count in the book did not move in this block, and nobody looked, and the book's count and the city's count are different numbers.** Judgement: correct, and it is the card's *the count goes up in Block 3 and the fact that a child said no and nobody in the city knows they have done it is Block 3's*. Chapter 220 states the sameness of the count twice and states that nobody looked. **The difference between the number in the book and the number in a man's mouth is stated once and is not reconciled, and is Batch 0003's position to work from.**

4. **A ledger turned round is Crane's book of refusals in Chapter 220, with the spine toward himself, and it is not Cusk's roadmark ledger.** Judgement: correct, and it is the only way the prompt's *ends with a ledger turned round* can be honoured without taking `outline/volume-05.md` §8 Block 3's turning of the roadmark ledger off that block. **Cusk's second column is refused in four words in Chapter 218 and is not turned round and is not on the page, and Block 0003's reveal is untouched.**

5. **The first asking is for work and the answer is that a man who is paid is a man somebody has had to write somewhere, and it is made at a glass yard's gate and then carried up a lane to a council that does not answer things and then down a lane to a keeper of forms.** Judgement: correct, and the three stages are what the card's *the first refusal is not given in a room* needs. The council of eleven is in Chapter 214 and does not refuse anybody; the form is in Chapter 215; the second asking is in Chapter 216 and gets the same eleven words.

6. **Eleven words, not nine, for the form's text, and the count of the words is on the page and is not a figure of persons.** Judgement: correct, and it is the volume's echo of the bench's eleven words in Chapter 203, in the same construction and not the same words. **The count of words is a count of words and is never a count of people and is never added to anything.**

7. **Karrow is turned back at a gate inside her own city and is turned back because of what she is carrying, and the trader's account of her in Chapter 207 is not restated, shortened, improved on or alluded to as retractable.** Judgement: correct. The trader's name does not appear in this block, and no character describes her situation in his words, and the two gates in Chapter 217 are the city's own. **The outline's want for her, that a roadmark be a letter and not a door, is in her mouth in Chapter 217 in nine words and is not improved on, and the volume's answer to her — that the carrying is the job and not the failing — is not said to her by anybody in this block and is held.**

8. **Cusk's page is not turned round and the second column's head is one plain word meaning carried and nobody says what is in the column.** Judgement: correct and required. He refuses it in four words, refuses to say why, and says in nineteen words that the head is what we do to a thing and not what the thing is. **The reveal that the second column is a column of routes is Block 0003's and is not anticipated here by anybody, including the man of thirty-two.**

9. **Aren's own palm is put on Cusk's bench and is told not to be one of theirs, and nobody joins it to anything.** Judgement: correct, and it is the only way to use the palm that is canon from Chapter 5 in a city that will not explain a mark, **because Cusk's answer is a negative one and the negative is the join-blocker.** The prose describes the two marks differently, the child's being a line and a bar and a figure and the palm's being a black line healed into a crease, and the chapter says in nine words that there is no same.

10. **The ninth asking is put to the child by Crane in a yard and the man of thirty-two does not put it, and he does not ask the child a second time.** Judgement: correct, and it is the block's cost. Thread 1's *held open: whether the child is asked a second time* is not closed and is not answered, and the volume's mechanic is usable for the first time in two hundred and twenty chapters and the man it is usable to stands forty yards off with four words ready for thirteen days and does not use it. **The seventh line of the page of foolscap is still unfinished at the end of Chapter 220 and he has not looked at the page since the ninth day.**

11. **The city does not name the child, and the reason is given once in trade terms by a woman of thirty with a pail, and the reason the city's reason is the volume's is the post.** Judgement: correct, and it is the mechanism that lets a city of nine thousand people have an unnamed nine-year-old who goes out of a gate four days in seven. A name in a book in this city is a person somebody can be shown; a name in about four hundred heads is not; the child is the one person who goes out and therefore cannot be a name. **The child is not named in any of the ten chapters and the word *post* is used in one mouth and not improved on.**

12. **The trade of the city is recognition and the defensive craft is to be unnameable, and the stick is the best small instance of it.** Judgement: correct. A ferrier's stick for four hundred yards of gravel in a city with no gravel in it is a description of where a man is from, said out loud by one man on a lane and not improved on by the next, and the man cannot put it down and cannot lean on it, and about four men on that lane worked it out in about a minute and a half.

13. **The lamp is a plain noun and is said in Chapter 211 and is not improved on in nine chapters.** Judgement: correct, and it is the only word this block invents for a thing the outline said not to improve on. The woman's sentence is the whole of the explanation of the word: *that is the lamp, it comes up in the east and it stands.* **The word is said in one mouth in Chapter 211 and a woman of about thirty uses the plainest-one register for it in Chapter 219, and a man of about forty in a glass yard refuses the reason and not the word, and nobody in the block says why it does not turn.**

14. **The city's day count is spoken of and is not spoken of by the city.** Judgement: correct, and it is a real institutional fact: a city of about nine thousand people where nobody keeps an hour and two men keep a count of days, and one of the two has worked out that the other keeps one because his book has a fold in it in the same place. **The man of thirty-two keeps a count too, which is the only thing anybody in that city did not expect, and nobody has asked him what is in the book and four people in that city have said that not asking is the single most correct thing anybody did that week.**

15. **The name budget is one of six and five slots unfilled, and the one is Dermit Crane.** Judgement: correct, and it is exactly `outline/volume-05.md` §13's Batch 0002 row. **Volume 05 stands at four names of six per batch used and twenty-six of thirty slots unfilled across the five batches, against the volume's planned five and twenty-four.** Cusk and Karrow are spent Batch 0001 names and both now have pages and neither is re-described. The child is a child and is unnamed and does not count. **The block spent one slot and did not spend a second and did not spend none.**

16. **`nine` is 189 in 31,938 words, 5.92 per 1,000, per chapter 10 / 4 / 19 / 17 / 15 / 16 / 28 / 15 / 34 / 31, and the volume's six-block ladder reads 2.87 / 4.58 / 7.31 / 5.73 / 7.26 / 4.17 / 5.92.** Judgement: honest, counted, and the second-highest in the volume. The rise is not a tic and it is accounted for on the page: the city keeps a count of days, the ninth refusal is the block's subject, the second hour and the ninth hour are the hours that do the city's work, and a man of thirty-two keeps a notebook he has not written in for four years. **The two figures above thirty-four are Chapter 219 and Chapter 220 and they are the two chapters in which a child of nine and a ninth refusal are the whole of what is happening, and the motif is canon and none of it was cut.**

17. **`about` is 360, per chapter 43 / 41 / 37 / 34 / 29 / 31 / 33 / 36 / 39 / 37, and no chapter is over forty-five.** Judgement: correct against the ceiling. **`about N seconds` came out at forty on the block's first full draft and is 2, one in Chapter 218 and one in Chapter 219, and both are the two figures the batch required** — Cusk's want in about nine seconds and the ninth asking in about nine seconds — and every other instance was replaced with a different true fact and not with a smaller number wearing a coat. `the whole of` is **15**, against a house baseline of two or three a chapter and down from the thirty-one the block carried mid-draft. `about nine + noun` is **1** and `in nine years` is **0**. `cut` is **3** and all three are ordinary.

18. **Fifty-one `---` rules and a hundred and thirty bolded line-openings, and both are recorded rather than dressed.** Judgement: honest. The rules are against Volume 04's fifth block at 37 and Batch 0001 at 47, and the bolded line-openings are against 95 and 143. **The block is made of yards and lanes and rooms and every section is opened by a man with a bar, a hod, a rule nine inches long, a trowel or a pen saying something.** A duplicate-paragraph sweep across the ten chapters returns clean and a per-line bold parity check across all two hundred and twenty chapter files returns zero.

19. **One System panel in the whole block, in Chapter 214, at the door of the council, in two plain lines, with no number in it, and it is not a list: *Nothing here is owed to you and nothing here has refused you. You have not been told which of the two you are in.*** Judgement: correct. It does not restate the three prior panels, it names neither the city, the dawn, the roadmark nor the light, and its two lines are both true of the whole block: a city that has refused eight times has not refused him, and nothing is owed to him. He does not write it down.

20. **Zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, zero straight double quotes, zero curly apostrophes and zero curly opening single quotes, and a per-line bold parity check across every line of all two hundred and twenty chapter files returns zero.** Judgement: correct, **and both the parity check and a duplicate-paragraph sweep were run here and are recorded as having been run, which is the sixth consecutive block and the difference continuity item 516 is about.**

21. **Seven defects of this block's own text were found and all seven repaired, and six of the seven are invisible to every device in this ledger.** Judgement: recorded in full at continuity item 584. **They were: a scripted device pass over ten files that removed forty `about N seconds` and in doing so left seven sentences with a conjunction missing and five narrator's leaks of this book's own structure on the page; two curly apostrophes in Chapters 215 and 217; Chapter 220 putting Crane's book in the wrong yard; Chapter 220's opening contradicting its own later sentence about how long the child took; three bold spans left open by a repair of the bold span, which passed a per-line parity check because each had an even number of markers with one span unclosed; and Chapter 218, whose second-column scene had been inserted above the chapter's opening exchange and which was rewritten whole in the right order rather than patched.** **Every one of them was found by reading a file and not by counting anything, and the standing rule item 561 adds is the rule this batch lived by: a device repair in this project must be done by reading every line it touches, and a parity check is necessary and not sufficient.**

22. **No thread inherited is closed and none is answered.** The fourteen Batch 0001 carried forward, and the twelve the Volume 04 close handed to Batch 0001, all stand. **The form under the byre is not mentioned and its three questions are not answered and are not scheduled; the two unreconciled numbers on a page in a coat are not reconciled and the page is not written on; the keeper's decision about the three marks on a seed vault is not taken; the subscription sentence is not traced; the tile on a coping is not mentioned and no explanation, flashback, origin, maker or deathbed is scheduled; the bell is not in this block and the two Monday facts are not joined; the hearing is not adjourned and not refused and the delegation is not sent for; the number of persons the offer was made out to is not answered; the count of 2,640 is not restated; the word eastern is not on the page in this block; the County of Ash, its road and its figures are not on the page in this block.** The seam is not measured, not pointed at, and not joined to any mark, and Aren's palm is not joined to the child's mark or to either mark in Merefen. The not askings stand at nine and this block enters a tenth and puts no number on the page. **And `outline/series.md`'s Volume 5 escalation line is not satisfied in full, and this block is not to be read as satisfying it, and the first named member of the Culling Order on a page is Volume 06's.**

23. **Nine of this block's ten chapters end on something that is not a refusal.** Judgement: correct, and the block's third deliberate answer to the Volume 03 close's items 1 and 2, and its first inside a path city. **211** on a lamp standing and a woman who did not ask. **212** on a waggon and a stick and a cup that was not his. **213** on a man coming through a gate with his name not wanted. **214** on a boy on a step and a door that has never asked him for anything. **215** on a man of sixty-six who does not say goodnight to a man who is leaving. **216** on three pieces of paper in a coat and two of them that say no. **217** on an empty yard and a stranger four days early. **218** on a man walking out knowing less than he came in with. **219** on a page on a hook four feet away. **220** on a ledger turned round and a child of nine that did not go out. **The one that ends on a refusal is the last one, and 220's is a no given by a person of nine years old in a yard, which is the volume's whole engine and is not a document leaving a room.**

24. **The city is not a mirror of the valley and nothing in the block says it is, and the block does not pity it and does not let a character pity it.** Judgement: correct, and the two places where it could have gone wrong are handled. The man of thirty-two is never shown the city as beautiful, ruined or lonely; he is shown it as a place that feeds him and does not pay him, works nine hours a day, and has asked him nothing in nineteen days, and those are trade facts. **And the one place a reader could want a verdict is Chapter 219, and the reason pitying a thing requires knowing what the thing is for is given as an institutional fact about about four hundred people and about nine hundred figures and not one of the two knowing the other, and no character in the block says one word against this city.**

25. **The Stage 3 tax is on the page as an ordinary working day and is not a symptom and nobody says *you are slower now*.** Judgement: correct. He loses the thread of a conversation in a market and is holding a cup he has not drunk from for eleven minutes, and about four hundred people watch it and say nothing, and in a city where recognition is the trade that is a way of being unable to be given a cup. A man of thirty-four deals with the limp by carrying six feet of another man's run eleven times in an afternoon. **The limp does not worsen, no bodily cost of any kind is added, no melody cost is added and the word does not appear, and the seam is not on the page at all except as a fact that is not moved and not measured.**

26. **The city keeps a count of days and no months and the market is on four days out of seven, and both are stated as trade facts in mouths and neither is in the narrative.** Judgement: correct, and the market's rule is the ordinary consequence of a city that keeps no weeks. **It is a fourth schedule figure in the block and it is recorded here rather than left in the prose unaccounted for: about nine thousand people, eleven on the council, four hundred years, and four days out of seven, and none of the four is a figure of persons and none of the four is a hearth.** The market's rule was corrected once while this block was being repaired, because a Chapter 212 passage had said the street had been busy every day he had been here and another had said the hour was the same four days running, and the two could not both be true, **and the repair moved a money column that had been standing above the purchase it was the price of, and the repair was made by reading the chapter and not by counting anything.**

27. **`four hundred years` is on the page forty times in ten chapters, and that is a register and not a device, and it is recorded here rather than dressed.** Judgement: honest. The city's age is its one canon figure of duration and it is doing structural work in a block about a duty that has been kept every day for that long, and the alternative was a clock, **and a clock in a city whose light does not turn would have been an explanation, and this block explains nothing.** The same is true of the house construction *about four people in that yard have said since*, which appears more often than any figure in the block and is the manuscript's own method for reporting what a place has noticed about itself. **A Batch 0003 writer should carry both and should not go looking for either.**

**Filler counts for the next writer's baseline, counted as whole words and not as strings, so that `stood` at 33 is not `understood` at a higher number: `nodded` 0, `held` 3, `wrote` 14, `stood` 33, `plain` 2, `counted` 9, `breathed` 0, `steady` 0.** Against Volume 04's five blocks at 0/11/37/13/2/8/0/0, 1/10/27/30/0/8/0/0, 0/8/28/36/0/10/0/0, 0/5/27/35/0/24/0/0 and 0/7/25/42/0/16/0/0, and against Volume 05 Batch 0001's 0/1/35/24/0/5/0/0. **Six of the eight are at or under the ten-chapter baseline, `stood` is over Batch 0001's 24 and under Volume 04's 42 and is a man at a bench, a man at a gate and a man in a lane, and `plain` at 2 is over a zero and is the block's two plain nouns, which are a lamp and the head of a column, and the block does not improve on either.** Zero semicolons, zero em-dashes, fifty-one `---` rules, a hundred and thirty bolded line-openings, zero straight double quotes, zero curly apostrophes, `the whole of` 15, `about N seconds` 2, `about nine + noun` 1, `in nine years` 0, `cut` 3 and all three ordinary, `about` 360, one System panel in two plain lines with no number, and no occurrence of the word Gatehouse. **A duplicate-paragraph sweep across the ten chapters returns clean and a per-line parity check of every line in every chapter file in the repository returns zero, which is the sixth consecutive block in which it returns zero, and it was run and not recorded.**

**What the block changed.** **A city of about nine thousand people on a river under a light that has not turned in four hundred years, a lamp, a ladder, a market on about two hundred yards of street with a man who sells names for a penny and a man of thirty-two who did not buy one, three gates that are a machine for letting liars through, a council of eleven in a long room with a book that holds what was asked, a woman of sixty who has said once that eleven is a number that means eleven and will not say it twice, a yard of cord where a man of sixty-six makes eleven-word forms with no number and no name on them and keeps a book of refusals under a board that cannot be shown to anybody, a yard past two gates with a book of marks in it in two columns with the second one refused in four words, a woman of twenty-eight who has been turned back at three gates for four years and wants one thing, a child of nine whose hand goes flat on a bench, and a ninth refusal in four hundred years given by a person of nine years old in a yard and entered in nothing.**

**And four hundred miles off, and not on the page in this block, there is a reach with about forty men on it and a joint a foot and a half short and a line under half an inch of lime that has not moved, a bench of nine that has refused a road twice, a woman in a room with a door on a lane who is not going to walk up that road, a second copyist who has not opened the tenth letter, a form in a bag with a ruled space for a figure of persons and nowhere for a name, a jar in a lock-up with a hundred and forty marks against it, and a yard on Weir Street that has been off the page for twenty chapters and is the volume's second-largest cost after the city.**

**What the next phase inherits.** `outline/volume-05.md` first, and §8 Block 3, §9, §10, §13's Batch 0003 row, §14, §15 and §17 especially. Then `state/continuity.md` items 566 to 584, **with 571 carrying the refusal arithmetic and 573 carrying the two marks that are not joined and 582 carrying the standing list of what this block did not do**, then the Relationship state at the end of Chapter 220, then this entry, then the Block 2 note in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, then Chapters 220 and 219 in full and 218 for Cusk and 215 for Crane and 217 for Karrow. **A city of about nine thousand people. A lamp. Eleven on a council and a form of eleven words and a boy of nine who will not say a thing twice. A book under a board that cannot be shown. A second column that has not been turned round. Two refusals in a coat and a page with an unfinished seventh line. A man of sixty-one who wants to be told to stop and has said so once, to a stranger, in nine seconds. A woman of twenty-eight who wants a letter. And a ninth refusal that nobody in that city knows about and that cannot be filed, which is the first refusal in four hundred years given by somebody who can be asked again.**

## Batch 0003 — Volume 05, Chapters 221–230, "The Count"

**Landed.** Chapters 221–230 in `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0221.md` to `chapter-0230.md`, four digits, ten complete scenes. **Path time throughout, inside the city, from the twentieth day of a count of days the city keeps from the last renewal to the thirtieth, and the block's remaining sixty days of ninety are the next two batches'.** **32,103 words as first written** by `wc -w` including title lines, per chapter **3132 / 3392 / 3397 / 3044 / 3495 / 2972 / 3200 / 3160 / 3068 / 3243**, and the ten columns foot at 32,103. **After the Batch 0003 review repair pass the block is 30,038 words, per chapter 3009 / 3177 / 3114 / 2764 / 3219 / 2783 / 3083 / 2934 / 2912 / 3043, and the ten columns foot at 30,038. The 2,065 words that went are the reporting clause and its *and that* tail and not one of them is a fact, a beat, a scene, an ending or a name.** **Counted on the page, the five Volume 04 columns are 33,045 · 33,385 · 34,216 · 34,011 · 34,157 and foot at 168,814 in fifty chapters, Volume 05 Batch 0001 is 34,091, Volume 05 Batch 0002 is 31,938, and the manuscript is 270,562 + 184,519 + 146,561 + 168,814 + 34,091 + 31,938 + 30,038 = 866,523 words in two hundred and thirty chapters, and 868,588 was true of the page before the repair pass and is left standing as the fact it was.** The block is above Batch 0002 and below Batch 0001 and no chapter is under 2,763. All three totals were footed before they were printed here.

**What the block turned out to be.** **A figure left a city of about nine thousand people and nobody in it knows, twice, and the figure is a count of refusals, and the count is right, and it is wrong by one, and the one is a child of nine years old.** A man of about forty-six with a satchel and a list of about four hundred places comes down the eastern road on the twentieth day and is a man who leaves a printed sheet at a door and has been doing it for eight years and has never once been asked to fill one in, and who says *a form is a thing a door can throw away, that is why it is printed and not written* in the tone of a man saying a price, and who says four things in that tone in nine days and all four of them are the truest things anybody in that city has heard from outside it in four hundred years. **A printed sheet with six columns, five with heads and a sixth with no head at all, and four of them ruled for a figure and nowhere at all for a name.** A man of sixty-six in a bindle yard who has made about four hundred pieces of paper in twenty years and has never put a figure on one of them, and who puts four figures on a printed sheet in a minute and a half because a man fills in the spaces. **A book under a board that says eight and a man's head that says nine and the difference between them is a child, and neither number has ever been put next to the other.** A gate that is a machine for letting liars through and four words in a hut and a man turned round for carrying a sheet and a bale of cord and reed with the sheet flat inside it. **A ledger turned round in a yard past two gates because a figure went out and not because a stranger asked, and eleven roads in a second column over four hundred years, and a city that has always known where it is and has never asked how a road out and back is made.** A form that would make the city evidence in a case and a refusal to be a document, and the cost of that refusal said by the man who asked for it: **it is worth nothing, the first one is gone.** **A lineage reading at a wall, once, which produces a roster, a shift, a man of fifty-two and a decision four hundred years old to pay a cost every day instead of once, and a man of thirty-two who cannot stop seeing a list of eleven lines.** A glass works, a man of fifty-two who is bored and then good at a wall, and two things he says once each in the same conversation, the second of which is the reason the first is a thing a man says. **A child asked a second time in the same yard in the same words, and no, and eight and two is ten, and a form with four tens on it going down a hill.** And a man of fifty-two in a working yard saying the thing the whole block has been arriving at, in nineteen words, to nobody in particular: **we have begun filing a person as a class, and we did it in answer to a form, and we did it out of nothing.**

**The block's best pages, and they are not all the ones the cards asked for.** **Chapter 221's man of forty-six and his two sentences about what a printed thing is for**, and the money footed on a sill in a room the size of a cart in the same sentences a woman of about fifty says to a man with a stick on the first day of Batch 0002 without knowing she is saying them twice. **Chapter 222's man of sixty-six asking *has anybody ever come here* on the first question he has asked anybody in about six years**, and not answering it, and the silence being a man finding out that there is a second thing about a piece of paper that is worse than the first. **Chapter 223's arithmetic**, done out loud in a yard on the twenty-third day, twice, a minute apart, with a book on one side of a bench and a printed sheet on the other, and a man of thirty with a barrow saying the true thing about a page from a hill. **Chapter 224's bundle**, and four turns of cord through loops that are already in a bale for that, and a man of fifty-five in a hut who does not say the four words the second time because a bundle is a bundle. **Chapter 225's second column in two parts**, and *a man does not split a thing he has looked at for nineteen years*, and eleven roads and a man of thirty-two with a limp working out at the bottom of a slope that eleven is not a great many. **Chapter 226's boy of fourteen and a line in a book that is the same age as he is**, and a man of sixty-one saying, before anybody asks him, that there is no column for what became of anybody. **Chapter 227's refusal to make a document, and the twenty-two-word reason a man of sixty-six stands up to give, and the satchel-man's nineteen words about which of the two things in a satchel was the one that mattered.** **Chapter 229's eighth and two is ten**, said out loud in a yard before the figure is written, and a man of fifty-five in a hut whose four words were in his mouth and did not come out. **And Chapter 230's three things in a working yard and a duckboard that has been under a stranger's feet for seven days, five on the twenty-eighth and seven on the thirtieth, and a wall that is four hundred years old with nine places on it where a ladder will go.**

**Card and prompt deviations, recorded so that no writer re-derives them as errors.**
